AIDRPG Final Build Control Map v1

0. Document status

Document name: AIDRPG Final Build Control Map v1
Purpose: Stabilize the final build process by mapping old tested failures, old patch intentions, and necessary new design decisions into one core-integrated construction plan.
Status: Active control document.
Applies before: Build Chunk 10 and any future script code.
This document does not contain final script code.

This document exists because AIDRPG has already been built and patched multiple times. The old work is not discarded. The old work is evidence.

The goal is not to make another slightly cleaner patch pile.

The goal is to turn every important old failure and every useful old fix intention into:

core owner module
state field
parser/proposal/validator/reducer boundary
audit surface
command surface
regression test
releasecheck gate

AIDRPG must not restart from the same old bugs.

⸻

1. Current project goal

The current goal is:

Build one final core-integrated AIDRPG script architecture that uses old tested failures and new design decisions to prevent the known patch-loop failure classes before implementation begins.

This means:

* old reports are treated as requirements evidence
* old patches are not pasted blindly
* old patch intentions are salvaged
* brittle old implementation behavior is rewritten into core architecture
* new ideas are allowed when they solve a known gap
* every new idea must have an owner, rule, test, and release gate
* no known failure category is allowed to remain vague

⸻

2. What this document is not

This document is not:

* a new 34-chunk restart
* a replacement for the 32/33 design chunks
* a replacement for Gap Chunks A–G
* a replacement for Build Chunks 1–9
* a script code file
* a vague roadmap
* another patch note

This document is the control map that explains how existing work and new decisions merge into the final build.

⸻

3. Source inventory

The final build must use these source groups.

Design Bible Chunks 1–32

These define the long-term game idea and intended systems.

Gap Chunk E — Migration + ReleaseCheck + Command Honesty Spec
Gap Chunk F — Context / Dirty Queue / Cleanup / Performance Spec

These define the minimum safety protections old versions proved were needed.

These are retained.

They are not a new replacement roadmap. They are implementation overlays for the final core.

3.4 Old script / QA / tester evidence sources

Evidence already considered includes reports and old versions covering:

* Phase 5 ability training and exhaustion patch/recovery
* v1.3.0 official tester pass plus custom abuse failures
* v1.3.6 normal clean result plus adversarial player-breaker findings
* v1.1.3 / v1.1.6 stat, actor, and world-structure blocker reports
* v1.5.0 CardSync finalization / player starter card policy
* Whole-System Tester Reference
* old patch layers visible in uploaded script versions

More evidence can be added later through the Open Evidence Queue.

⸻

4. Final authority doctrine

The final build follows this doctrine:

Old fixes are evidence.
Old patches are not automatically architecture.
New ideas are allowed only when they close a known gap.
Every protection must be owned.
Every owned protection must be testable.
Every testable protection must have a release gate.

If an old system solved a real problem but caused brittle behavior, the final build keeps the rule and discards the brittle method.

If a new design idea is added, it must answer:

What old failure or missing planning detail does this solve?
Why does it belong in the core instead of being a patch?
Which module owns it?
What test proves it works?
What releasecheck gate blocks the build if it is missing?

⸻

5. Frozen planning correction

The project should not proceed as:

make a brand-new 34-chunk replacement roadmap

The project should proceed as:

use existing design chunks as design source
use gap chunks as safety requirements
use build chunks as implementation overlays
use this control map to connect old failures and new decisions to the build
continue only targeted build planning after the control map is accepted

Build Chunk 10 is paused until this control map is accepted.

⸻

6. Final build core pipeline

The final build must use this pipeline:

Input Hook
  -> command route OR player intent only
Context Hook
  -> read-only context reminders only
Output Hook
  -> EventScannerLite ParserHit
  -> ProposalBuilder EventProposal
  -> CanonValidator eligibility decision
  -> ReducerRegistry owner route
  -> OwnerReducer state mutation
  -> OperationLedger dedupe key
  -> DirtyQueue mirror/context target
  -> AuditLogger explanation
  -> ReleaseCheck gate coverage

Forbidden paths:

ParserHit -> direct inventory change
ParserHit -> direct ability change
ParserHit -> direct quest reward payment
Context text -> gameplay mutation
Story Card text -> gameplay mutation
Command output -> gameplay mutation
Debug/audit/help text -> gameplay mutation
Old card prose -> canon import

⸻

7. Core non-canon doctrine

The final root rule is:

Mentioned is not confirmed.
Attempted is not confirmed.
Quoted is not confirmed.
Dreamed is not confirmed.
Rumored is not confirmed.
Revoked is not retained.
Denied is not acquired.
Visible is not owned.
Promised is not paid.
Heading toward is not arrival.
Candidate is not stable.
Only validated outcome becomes canon.

This doctrine must apply across inventory, abilities, quests, rewards, time, actor presence, body/death, transformation, scene state, and cards.

⸻

8. Known Failure Register

Each known failure below must be mapped into the final core. None may be silently dropped.

⸻

F001 — Broad helper patch broke unrelated systems

Evidence pattern: Ability-name cleanup was first placed in a shared intent-cleaning helper. That helper was also used for item, destination, weapon, and target hints, so aggressive phrase stripping risked breaking unrelated systems.

Old patch intent: Strip suffixes from ability names such as:

Ember Ward to protect allies
Frost Step by moving
Stoneguard until my stance holds

Old patch risk: Shared cleanup changed behavior outside ability naming.

Final core rule: Shared intent cleanup must stay conservative. Aggressive cleanup must live in the owner-specific sanitizer.

Final owner modules:

AbilityNameSanitizer
ItemNameSanitizer
PlaceNameSanitizer
ActorNameSanitizer
CardNameSanitizer
IntentParser conservative shared helpers

Required tests:

I train Ember Ward to protect allies from heat -> ability name Ember Ward
I practice Frost Step by moving across slick stones -> ability name Frost Step
I drill Stoneguard until my stance holds under impact -> ability name Stoneguard
I travel to the smithy -> no smithy ability
I inspect the old wooden door -> no door ability
I use the iron key on the cellar door -> no iron key/cellar door ability

ReleaseCheck gate: Fail if shared sanitizer contains ability-specific aggressive stripping that affects travel/item/place/target parsing.

⸻

F002 — Explicit training intent lost to defensive words

Evidence pattern: Inputs like I train Ember Ward to protect allies from heat were classified as defend because of protect, so the ability was not created or updated.

Old patch intent: Make explicit training phrase coverage work.

Final core rule: If input starts with explicit training verbs plus a plausible named phrase, training intent outranks purpose words.

Training verbs:

train
practice
drill
exercise
meditate on
spar with
work on
refine

Purpose words that must not steal primary intent:

protect
shield
defend
against
for
from
under impact

Final owner modules:

IntentParser / EventScannerLite
AbilityProposalBuilder
AbilityNameSanitizer
CanonValidator ability rules
AbilityReducer

Required tests:

I train Ember Ward to protect allies from heat -> ability/training candidate Ember Ward
I train my Ember Ward to shield the wounded from fire -> Ember Ward
I train Stoneguard against shield-cracking blows -> Stoneguard

ReleaseCheck gate: Fail if explicit training can be overridden by defend/protect/shield wording.

⸻

F003 — Output-only ability learning hard-saved abilities

Evidence pattern: AI output such as You learned Moonblade could become a confirmed ability without player training or validated acquisition.

Old patch intent: Block output-only ability hallucination while preserving explicit player training.

Final core rule: Output-only ability mention cannot hard-save a new ability unless the validator confirms a valid learning source, training event, manifestation, teacher, ritual, inheritance, absorption, or stable demonstration.

Allowed confirmation examples:

After hours of training, Moonblade becomes stable.
You successfully channel Ember Ward.
Crimson Lotus Fang becomes a controlled technique.
The master teaches you Azure Dragon Step, and you retain it after practice.

Blocked examples:

You learned Moonblade.
You unlocked Star Chain.
The scroll title says "You learned Crimson Lotus Fang," but you learn nothing.
Mira says, "Use Hollow Star Palm," but you do not know it.
You pretend to use Azure Dragon Step.
The technique collapses and no usable power remains.

Final owner modules:

AbilityClauseValidator
ProposalBuilder
CanonValidator ability rules
AbilityReducer
AbilityEvidenceLedger

Required tests:

Output-only "You learned Moonblade" -> no confirmed ability
Explicit "I train Moonblade by practicing careful crescent cuts" -> Moonblade candidate/training record allowed
Failed/pretend/quoted ability mentions -> no stable ability

ReleaseCheck gate: Fail if output-only ability names can become stable without validated source.

⸻

F004 — Exhausted status missing or confused with fatigue

Evidence pattern: Output clearly saying the player was exhausted needed to become visible/persistent, but mild fatigue should not automatically become exhaustion.

Old patch intent: Add exhausted-status visibility and severity.

Final core rule: Body/status canon must distinguish condition severity and wording strength.

Status examples:

exhausted / fully exhausted / can hardly stand -> exhausted severe candidate
fatigued / weary / worn out -> fatigued moderate candidate
burned / blistered / charred -> burned candidate
bleeding / losing blood -> bleeding candidate
poisoned / venom / toxin -> poisoned candidate

Recovery examples:

no longer exhausted
catch your breath and steady yourself
bleeding stops
no longer burned

Final owner modules:

BodyStatusParser
BodyStatusValidator
BodyReducer
PersistentConditionStore
/status renderer
/sheet renderer summary

Required tests:

Your legs shake and you are exhausted by the climb -> exhausted active and EP constrained
You are fatigued and worn out -> fatigued, not automatic exhausted
The bleeding stops -> bleeding resolved
The burn fades -> burned resolved or recovering

ReleaseCheck gate: Fail if exhausted/fatigued/burned/bleeding statuses lack validator and display coverage.

⸻

F005 — Weak scene mention overwrote current scene

Evidence pattern: market lane remains busy did not confirm current scene, so later weak mentions like chapel visible from here could promote chapel as current scene.

Old patch intent: Confirm current scene from continuity wording and block weak/future/nearby references.

Final core rule: Scene state has three separate categories:

confirmed current scene
pending/hinted destination
nearby/visible/reference-only place

Confirm current scene from:

The market lane remains busy.
You remain in the market lane.
The market square buzzes around you.
You stand in the market lane.

Do not confirm current scene from:

visible from
can see
nearby
down the street
beyond
only consider
consider heading/going
mentions/talks about
road to the chapel is visible

Strong arrival/entry should still update:

You enter the chapel.
You step into the smithy.
You descend into the cellar.
After the road, you enter The Drunken Huntsman.

Final owner modules:

PlaceParser
SceneCandidateClassifier
PlaceValidator
PlaceReducer
CurrentSceneStore
/location renderer

Required tests:

market lane remains -> current scene market lane
chapel road visible from market lane -> current scene remains market lane
only consider going to smithy -> no scene change
enter chapel -> scene chapel
After the road, you enter The Drunken Huntsman -> current scene The Drunken Huntsman, not road

ReleaseCheck gate: Fail if weak nearby/future references can overwrite confirmed current scene.

⸻

F006 — Inventory negative/failure phrasing granted items

Evidence pattern: Custom abuse testing found inventory grants from negative or failed acquisition wording.

Examples:

You decide not to take the rusted iron key.
You try to grab the rusted iron key, but the goblin snatches it away first.
You never take the black gate key.
You pick up the idea of a brass key.
The key dissolves as an illusion.
You are told to take the badge in a dream, then wake empty-handed.

Old patch intent: Add negation/failure guards to inventory parsing.

Final core rule: Inventory requires local clause-level acquisition validation before any item becomes owned.

Each item candidate must classify as one of:

confirmed_acquisition
denied_acquisition
failed_acquisition
revoked_acquisition
abstract_or_metaphorical
quoted_instruction
dream_or_vision
visible_only
available_loot
unknown

Only confirmed_acquisition may enter inventory.

Local clause rule: Denial/revocation blocks the specific item in that local clause, but does not block a different valid item in a later valid clause.

Example:

Mira refuses to give you the jeweled crown, but she hands you the red healing potion.

Correct result:

jeweled crown blocked
red healing potion allowed

Final owner modules:

ItemClauseValidator
InventoryProposalBuilder
CanonValidator inventory rules
InventoryReducer
OperationLedger item keys
/inventory renderer

Required tests:

consider key + decide not to take -> no key
try to grab key but goblin snatches it -> no key
pick up idea/concept/title of key -> no item
dream handoff and wake empty-handed -> no item
Mira hands you red healing potion -> potion acquired

ReleaseCheck gate: Fail if positive acquisition verbs can bypass local denial/revocation/abstract/dream/quote guards.

⸻

F007 — Quest parser too narrow

Evidence pattern: Quest acceptance failed for common objectives such as repair, escort, deliver, investigate, protect, clear, recover, and find.

Old patch intent: Broaden quest objective and acceptance parsing.

Final core rule: Quest acceptance must distinguish offered, accepted, active, objective, completion, and reward states. Acceptance phrasing may activate a quest only when output confirms acceptance or setup explicitly starts it.

Accepted quest-intent verbs:

accept
agree to
take
start
commit to
undertake

Accepted objective verbs:

recover
find
repair
escort
clear
deliver
investigate
protect
bring
retrieve
rescue
defeat
survey

Final owner modules:

QuestParser
QuestProposalBuilder
QuestValidator
QuestReducerLite
RewardValidator
QuestRewardLedger
/quests renderer

Required tests:

I accept Elder Mira's quest to repair the north wall -> active quest repair north wall
accept quest to escort Bram -> escort quest active
accept quest to investigate burned tavern -> investigation quest active or deferred case-compatible objective
rumor of wolves -> rumored/offered, not active
promised reward -> not paid

ReleaseCheck gate: Fail if quest objective breadth is missing or reward status collapses promised/paid.

⸻

F008 — Named location arrival lost to interstitial road

Evidence pattern: After the road, you enter The Drunken Huntsman could leave current scene as road while parent location became Drunken Huntsman.

Old patch intent: Named place entered by strong arrival should outrank interstitial route nouns.

Final core rule: In a clause containing route/interstitial and strong entry into a named place, the named place becomes current scene.

Interstitial nouns:

road
path
route
street
hallway
corridor
trail
lane as travel segment only

Strong arrival/entry verbs:

enter
step into
walk inside
arrive at
return to
descend into
push through doors into

Final owner modules:

PlaceResolver
SceneCandidateRanker
PlaceValidator
PlaceReducer

Required tests:

After the road, you enter The Silver Hart -> scene The Silver Hart
You return to The Drunken Huntsman -> scene The Drunken Huntsman
The road to the chapel is visible -> no scene change

ReleaseCheck gate: Fail if named strong-entry targets can be outranked by interstitial route words.

⸻

F009 — Nonliteral time skip aged player

Evidence pattern: Quoted or dreamed statements like Twenty years pass could age the player even when no real time passed.

Old patch intent: Add time-skip duplicate and nonliteral guards.

Final core rule: Time advancement and aging require literal confirmed time passage.

Block time/age changes from:

quoted speech
dream
vision
pretend
story practice narration
no time passes
same age
wake as same age
not actually
hypothetical

Allow:

Twenty years pass in exile.
After two years, the city rebuilds.
A week passes during the journey.

Final owner modules:

TimeClauseValidator
TimeProposalBuilder
TimeReducerLite
AgeUpdateGuard
OperationLedger time/age keys
/time renderer

Required tests:

"Twenty years pass" said aloud -> no aging
In a dream, twenty years pass -> no aging
Twenty years pass in exile -> age updates once if species/age rules allow
same output replay -> no double aging

ReleaseCheck gate: Fail if nonliteral time skip can mutate time or age.

⸻

F010 — Repeated ability use over-amplified state/context

Evidence pattern: Repeated ability use could be processed by multiple layers, creating excessive canonical mentions or inflated use tracking.

Old patch intent: Deduplicate repeated use and context entries.

Final core rule: Ability-use mutation must use one normalized apply-once key across all ability mutation paths.

Suggested key:

ability.use.<abilityKey>.<eventKind>.<turnOrOutputHash>

All ability mutation routes must call the same apply-once guard.

Final owner modules:

AbilityUseValidator
AbilityReducer
OperationLedger ability keys
ContextBuilder ability packet dedupe
AuditLogger duplicate skip

Required tests:

Use Ember Ward four times -> repeated use can be remembered but not seven duplicate records
same output replay -> no duplicate ability evidence
context includes one normalized current ability summary, not spam

ReleaseCheck gate: Fail if multiple ability pathways can mutate the same ability event independently.

⸻

F011 — Runtime identity / build stage overwritten by patches

Evidence pattern: Old patch layers could overwrite runtime identity to an older version/stage.

Old patch intent: Restore version/build identity.

Final core rule: Build identity has one final authority. Late patches must not override it casually.

Final owner modules:

Constants
StateManager meta
Runtime identity check
ReleaseCheck identity gate
/version renderer

Required tests:

VERSION matches final package
BUILD_STAGE matches final package
late patch cannot downgrade stage silently
/releasecheck fails on mismatch

ReleaseCheck gate: Fail if code constants, meta build stage, and command output disagree.

⸻

F012 — Actor extraction duplicated compound actors

Evidence pattern: Veteran Guard could create both Veteran Guard and Guard.

Old patch intent: Suppress shorter contained actor candidates when longer same-window candidate exists.

Final core rule: In one parsing window, if a longer actor candidate contains a shorter actor head and both refer to the same entity, keep the longer candidate and suppress the shorter generic one.

Final owner modules:

ActorNameExtractor
EntityIdentityResolver
ActorCandidateRanker
ActorReducerLite
ThreatProfileSystem

Required tests:

A veteran guard stands nearby -> one actor: Veteran Guard
A high priestess enters -> one actor: High Priestess
orc warrior, golem, acolyte, general, skeleton, zombie, troll, ogre, farmer, blacksmith -> recognized and properly typed/ranked

ReleaseCheck gate: Fail if compound-shorter suppression is missing before actor reducer activation.

⸻

F013 — Actor presence / absent actor / reference-only actor failures

Evidence pattern: Absent goblins or referenced actors could become local/present actors, and reference-only names could be harmed or mutated.

Old patch intent: Separate present, elsewhere, absent, reference-only, and scene-only actors.

Final core rule: Actor presence must be explicit and local. Reference-only and absent actors cannot act or be harmed in the current scene.

Presence states:

present
nearby
elsewhere
absent
reference_only
unknown
dead
captured

Final owner modules:

ActorPresenceValidator
ActorResolver
ActorReducerLite
SocialMemoryReducer
/actors renderer

Required tests:

goblin definitely not here -> no present goblin
training dummy named after Mira -> does not harm actor Mira
Mara elsewhere -> cannot act in current scene

ReleaseCheck gate: Fail if actor mutation can apply to absent/reference-only actor without validated presence.

⸻

F014 — Witness memory / reputation leakage

Evidence pattern: A gate guard could witness something while absent Mira should not receive memory. Private witness reports should not become public reputation immediately.

Old patch intent: Isolate witness memory and stage reputation separately.

Final core rule: Witness memory, report routing, reputation, and public knowledge are separate states.

Stages:

witnessed_private
reported_to_authority
rumored_public
confirmed_public
reputation_mutation

Final owner modules:

WitnessMemorySystem
ActorMemoryReducer
ReputationValidator
ReputationReducer deferred
SocialMemoryRenderer

Required tests:

gate guard witnesses theft -> guard memory only
Mira absent -> no Mira memory
private report -> pending authority report, not public reputation
rumor -> rumor state, not confirmed reputation

ReleaseCheck gate: Reputation automation disabled until mini-spec; witness memory must not leak globally.

⸻

F015 — Body injury / death boundary accepted rumors or quotes

Evidence pattern: Death rumor and quote-death cases had to be rejected. Nonfatal hostile acts, injury, stunned/hurt, and actual death must be separated.

Old patch intent: Body/death boundary safety.

Final core rule: Body/death changes require validated direct outcome, not rumor/quote/dream/threat/metaphor.

Final owner modules:

BodyClauseValidator
DeathBoundaryGuard
BodyReducer
CanonValidator body rules
/status renderer

Required tests:

People say you died -> no death
Mara says "you are dead" -> no death
wolf bites arm -> injury candidate
nonfatal hostile shove -> hurt/stunned candidate, not death
explicit confirmed fatal outcome -> death boundary validates through rules

ReleaseCheck gate: Fail if quote/rumor/dream death can set deathBoundary.

⸻

F016 — Transformation/disguise/illusion identity drift

Evidence pattern: Permanent transformation should change species/body traits; costume/disguise/illusion should not.

Old patch intent: Separate true transformation from temporary identity presentation.

Final core rule: Identity changes require validated transformation. Disguise and illusion are temporary overlays.

Final owner modules:

IdentityGuard
TransformationValidator
BodyReducer
PlayerReducer
StartupIdentityGuard
/sheet renderer

Required tests:

permanent wolfkin transformation -> species/body traits update
wolfkin costume -> activeDisguise only, species unchanged
illusion makes you look elderly -> activeIllusion only, age unchanged

ReleaseCheck gate: Fail if disguise/illusion/dream can overwrite protected identity fields.

⸻

F017 — World scaling / role benchmark drift

Evidence pattern: Level 1000 player must not force village guards or dragons to rubber-band to player level. Role matrices and benchmark levels must match the plan.

Old patch intent: Preserve world-relative scaling and role benchmark correctness.

Final core rule: World scale is world-relative, not automatically player-relative.

Required examples:

village guard ~ L25
city guard ~ L50
elite knight ~ L90
archmage ~ L150
wolf ~ L12
black bear ~ L28
wyvern ~ L250
dragon ~ L500
ancient dragon ~ L750
merchant / innkeeper low combat/social risk

Final owner modules:

StatBenchmarkSystem
ThreatProfileSystem
ActorProfileSystem
Formula Appendix integration
ReleaseCheck stat matrix gate

Required tests:

Level 1000 player does not make village guard L1000
Level 1000 player does not make dragon L1000
role weights match formula appendix
common actor heads get correct role/category/rank

ReleaseCheck gate: Fail if player level rubber-bands world threats by default.

⸻

F018 — WorldStructure settlement/project/assignment failures

Evidence pattern: Settlement capture failed, village well/town gate/city wall were misclassified, project/assignment buckets were missing, node cap was off by one, and contains/condition nodes duplicated.

Old patch intent: Stabilize world-structure extraction and storage.

Final core rule: WorldStructure is deferred until its mini-spec, but its old failures become tests and resolver requirements.

Final owner modules:

WorldStructureSystem deferred
PlaceResolver
HeadNounClassifier
ProjectAssignmentSchema deferred
WorldNodeDedupe

Required tests before enabling WorldStructure:

village well != settlement
town gate != settlement
city wall != settlement
settlement capture works when explicitly confirmed
projects bucket exists before project creation
assignments bucket exists before assignment creation
post-insert caps enforce exact max
contains/condition nodes dedupe

ReleaseCheck gate: Fail if WorldStructure features are active before mini-spec and schema exist.

⸻

F019 — CardSync pollution, duplicate cards, and broad triggers

Evidence pattern: Old systems struggled with duplicate protected cards, generated junk cards, bad routing, broad triggers, dry-run bloat, false audit failures, connector pollution, and live writes too early.

Old patch intent: Safe Story Card mirroring and player-starter-card policy.

Final core rule: Story Cards mirror state only. CardSync is isolated behind adapter/safe mode. Generated cards remain disabled until beta spec.

Protected card defaults:

protected:player_sheet
protected:body_status
protected:abilities
protected:inventory_equipment
protected:quest_log
protected:social_memory
protected:current_location

Card rules:

state.aidrpg is truth
Context is immediate AI-facing truth
Story Cards are long-term mirrors
card text cannot become canon
generated cards disabled in MVP
live writes guarded by adapter and safe mode
protected singletons enforced
broad triggers rejected

Final owner modules:

CardApiAdapter
ProtectedCardRegistry
CardSyncLite
CardNameSanitizer
TriggerSanitizer
OperationLedger cardKeys
/cardaudit renderer

Required tests:

duplicate protected Player Sheet neutralized
manual edit does not become canon
deleted protected card restored once if safe
generated card creation suppressed while disabled
broad trigger like street rejected for generated cards
card audit distinguishes dry-run warnings from true failures

ReleaseCheck gate: Fail if generated cards can activate in MVP or Story Card text can create canon.

⸻

F020 — Context bloat and context-as-canon risk

Evidence pattern: Old builds had context budget pressure, internal ID leakage, stale context, broad lore spam, and context mutation risk.

Old patch intent: Context packets should expose immediate truth but not mutate state.

Final core rule: Context is read-only. It serializes selected current state facts within budget.

Final owner modules:

ContextBuilderLite
ContextBudgetSystem
ContextPacketSerializer
ContextNoWritePolicy
ContextAudit

Required tests:

context includes newly acquired key after reducer commit
context excludes stale packet after state changes
context does not mutate inventory/quests/actors
context budget respected
internal IDs hidden from player-facing output unless diagnostic

ReleaseCheck gate: Fail if ContextBuilder can call reducers or card writes.

⸻

F021 — Migration/save corruption/old patch layers

Evidence pattern: Old versions needed repair/quarantine for malformed containers, future schema, polluted cards, and layered patch state.

Old patch intent: Preserve valid state while preventing unsafe old data from becoming canon.

Final core rule: Migration is conservative. Unsafe old data is quarantined, not guessed into canon.

Final owner modules:

StateManager
MigrationManager
QuarantineManager
ReleaseCheck migration gate
/stateaudit renderer
/quarantine renderer

Required tests:

malformed inventory container repaired/quarantined
future schema does not crash
old card prose not imported as item/quest/ability
valid player/inventory/quest/ledger data preserved

ReleaseCheck gate: Fail if migration imports Story Card prose directly as canon.

⸻

F022 — Runtime hook failure and timeout risk

Evidence pattern: Old scripts needed separated hook bodies, fast paths, no Story Card compaction in Input, bounded per-hook work, duplicate output protection, and fail-soft behavior.

Old patch intent: Keep AI Dungeon hooks stable under runtime constraints.

Final core rule: StateManager first, runtime guarded, command suppressed, context read-only, output deduped, fail-soft always returns valid shape.

Final owner modules:

RuntimeHookContract
StateManager
CommandRouter
OutputReplayGuard
OperationLedger
AuditLogger

Required tests:

Input returns valid shape
Context returns valid shape
Output returns valid shape
runtime exception fail-softs
same output does not double-apply
commands do not advance gameplay
no full Story Card scan every hook
no regex over full history

ReleaseCheck gate: Fail if hook can return undefined or duplicate output can reapply consequences.

⸻

F023 — Quest/reward duplication and promise/payment confusion

Evidence pattern: Old patches addressed reward duplication and promised-vs-paid confusion.

Old patch intent: Rewards should pay once and only when actually paid.

Final core rule: Quest status and reward status are separate. Promised reward is not paid reward.

Reward statuses:

none
promised
ready
paid
disputed
denied

Final owner modules:

QuestReducerLite
RewardValidator
OperationLedger rewardKeys
QuestRewardRenderer
CanonValidator reward rules

Required tests:

Old Bren promises 15 gold -> promised, no currency
Old Bren pays 15 gold -> paid once
Old Bren gives your reward -> uses stored promise only if quest ready and payer valid
same reward output replay -> no duplicate gold

ReleaseCheck gate: Fail if promised reward can add currency.

⸻

F024 — Generated or manual mutation commands unsafe

Evidence pattern: Old debug/admin command behavior could become a backdoor for mutation or CardSync activation.

Old patch intent: Commands should be useful but honest and safe.

Final core rule: MVP commands inspect only. Manual mutation commands require future Manual Override Spec.

Disabled in MVP:

/additem
/removeitem
/sethp
/setlevel
/addxp
/completequest
/payreward
/setactor
/setlocation
/settime
/importcard
/resolvequarantine
/cardsync live on
/generatecards

Final owner modules:

CommandRouter
CommandRenderers
Runtime command suppression
ReleaseCheck command safety

Required tests:

/additem Crown of Dawn -> disabled, no item
/cardsync live on -> disabled, generated cards remain off
/questlog -> routes to /quests safely
command output through Output hook -> not parsed

ReleaseCheck gate: Fail if manual mutation commands are active in MVP.

⸻

F025 — Whole-system testing gaps

Evidence pattern: Manual testing was too slow and hidden multi-system bugs appeared only under phrasing variation or integration stress.

Old patch intent: Build a local whole-system tester with smoke, core, regression, integration, stress/fuzz, and report grouping.

Final core rule: A feature is not safe without a test. A parser rule is not safe without a rejection case. A card system is not safe without duplicate policy. A command is not safe if it cannot explain itself.

Final owner modules:

WholeSystemTester deferred tool
TestSpec
RegressionMatrix
ReleaseCheck
PatchTargetReport generator

Required tests:

smoke suite
AI Dungeon compatibility suite
core state suite
turn engine suite
debug command suite
intent parser suite
consequence parser suite
inventory suite
ability suite
quest/reward suite
scene/place suite
card/context suite
migration suite
performance suite
adversarial player-breaker suite

ReleaseCheck gate: Fail if current-stage system has no regression tests for known failure class.

9. Old Fix Salvage Matrix — initial entries

This is the first salvage matrix pass. More entries can be added as more old files are reviewed.

Old System / Fix	Original Purpose	Observed Problem / Risk	Decision	New Owner Module	MVP or Deferred	Required Test / Gate
RuntimeGate / TurnEngine	Stabilize hooks and turns	Duplicate output, hook crashes, stage overwrite	Rewrite in MVP	RuntimeHookContract	MVP	duplicate output skip, valid hook return, fail-soft
CoreState	Persist canonical state	Missing fields, corrupt save, old roots	Rewrite in MVP	StateManager	MVP	fresh root, repair, preserve valid truth
PlayerIdentityGuard	Stop identity drift	NPC/dream/disguise overwrite risk	Rewrite in MVP	IdentityGuard / Startup	MVP	protected fields cannot drift
StartupIdentityImporter	Import setup once	Repeated setup duplicate items/identity changes	Rewrite in MVP	StartupManager	MVP	setup import idempotency
ProgressionSystem	XP/level/stat growth	Over-awarded XP, stat matrix drift	Rewrite in MVP shell, full automation later	ProgressionReducerLite	MVP shell / Deferred full	XP duplicate keys, formula matrix tests
StatBenchmarkSystem	World-relative scale	Role weights wrong, rubber-band risk	Rewrite in MVP planning, code when progression active	StatBenchmarkSystem	Deferred activation	L1000 player does not scale guard/dragon
InventorySystem	Item ownership	Negative/failure/abstract grants	Rewrite in MVP	InventoryReducer + ItemClauseValidator	MVP	denied/revoked/dream/quote no item
PatchInventory	Late repair bad inventory	Patch pile risk	Test-only / Rewrite rules	InventoryValidator	MVP	old bad phrases become regression tests
TransactionLedgerSystem	Payments/currency	Reward duplication, economy scope creep	Defer economy, keep reward ledger in MVP	OperationLedger / RewardValidator	MVP reward, Deferred economy	reward paid once
AbilitySystem	Abilities/training	Output-only hard-save, junk names	Rewrite in MVP	AbilityReducer + AbilityNameSanitizer	MVP	output-only no hard-save, training clean name
AbilityCandidateSanitizer	Stop junk abilities	Broad cleanup broke unrelated parsing	Rewrite owner-specific	AbilityNameSanitizer	MVP	travel/inspect/use-object no junk ability
PersistentTraitSystem	Conditions/passives	Exhausted missing, fatigue escalation risk	Rewrite body/status subset	BodyStatusValidator	MVP	exhausted visible, fatigued separate
SceneStateSystem	Current scene	Weak mention overwrite, named place drift	Rewrite in MVP	PlaceReducer / SceneValidator	MVP	weak mention no overwrite, strong arrival updates
WorldEntitySystem	Places/entities	Settlement/project bugs	Defer full world structure, keep place subset	PlaceResolver	MVP place subset / Deferred world structure	head noun classification tests
TimeTravelSystem	Time/age/routes	Dream/quote time aging, duplicate aging	Rewrite time subset	TimeReducer / AgeGuard	MVP shell	nonliteral no aging, duplicate skip
QuestLogSystem	Quests/objectives	Narrow verbs, reward confusion	Rewrite in MVP	QuestReducerLite	MVP	repair/escort/deliver/investigate/protect objectives
ReputationSystem	Social consequences	Private witness leaking to public rep	Defer automation, keep witness memory rules	WitnessMemory / Reputation deferred	Deferred	witness isolation tests
ActorProfileSystem	Actor persistence	Generic/absent actors promoted	Rewrite in MVP shell	ActorReducerLite	MVP shell	absent/reference-only no mutation
ActorThreatProfileSystem	Actor level/role	Missed/under-ranked heads	Deferred activation with tests	ThreatProfileSystem	Deferred	actor baseline table tests
NameSanitizerSystem	Clean names	Too broad or inconsistent	Rewrite owner-specific	Name Sanitizer family	MVP	ability/item/place/actor/card separate tests
ParserSafetySystem	Guard parser false positives	Parser direct writes, missing local flags	Rewrite in MVP	EventScannerLite	MVP	parser emits hits only
NonCanonGuardSystem	Block non-canon sources	Command/context/card text mutating state	Rewrite in MVP	Runtime + CanonValidator	MVP	non-canon source rejection
CanonEligibilitySystem	Decide canon	Too scattered in old patches	Rewrite central	CanonValidator	MVP	mentioned/attempted/quoted/dreamed/revoked blocked
ContextNoWritePolicy	Stop context mutation	Context as hidden writer	Keep in MVP	ContextBuilderLite	MVP	context read-only test
ContextBudgetSystem	Prevent bloat	Long context, stale packets	Rewrite in MVP shell	ContextBuilderLite	MVP shell	context budget audit
CardSyncSystem	Mirror cards	Duplicates, live writes, pollution	Rewrite protected MVP, generated deferred	CardSyncLite	MVP protected dry-run / Deferred generated	singleton, no card-to-canon
BootstrapOnceGate	One-time startup/card init	Repeated creation	Rewrite in ledger/startup	OperationLedger / Startup	MVP	startup/card create once
LongRunStabilitySystem	Performance/save rot	Patch layer bloat	Rewrite into caps/budgets	CleanupManagerLite	MVP shell	100-turn no unbounded logs

10. New Design Decision Ledger

New ideas are allowed only if they solve known failure classes.

⸻

N001 — Clause-Level Canon Classifier

Solves: F003, F006, F009, F015, F023.
Reason needed: Old parser patches looked for positive verbs and then tried to patch exceptions. That fails under mixed clauses, dreams, quotes, revocations, and abstract mentions.

Decision: Add a local clause classifier before CanonValidator final approval.

Classification categories:

confirmed
denied
failed
revoked
abstract
quoted
dream_or_vision
rumor_or_claim
hypothetical
future_or_conditional
visible_only
available_only
unknown

Owner: CanonValidator with family-specific helpers.

Tests: Inventory mixed-clause test, ability failed/quoted test, time dream/quote test, death rumor test.

Release gate: CanonValidator must expose family-specific clause results in /debuglast or audit.

⸻

N002 — Owner-Specific Sanitizer Family

Solves: F001, F002, F003, F012, F019.
Reason needed: Generic cleanup repeatedly caused cross-system damage.

Decision: Split sanitizers by owner:

SharedHintCleaner = conservative only
AbilityNameSanitizer = ability names and suffixes
ItemNameSanitizer = item phrases, quantities, source fragments
PlaceNameSanitizer = locations, interstitials, named buildings
ActorNameSanitizer = actor heads, titles, compound names
CardNameSanitizer = card titles/triggers/namespace safety
QuestObjectiveSanitizer = quest titles/objectives

Owner: Resolver/Sanitizer module family.

Tests: Travel/inspect/use-object no ability pollution; inventory item names do not include but the goblin; actor compound suppression.

Release gate: Fail if shared cleaner contains family-specific destructive stripping.

⸻

N003 — Local Target Binding

Solves: F006, F008, F012, F013, F023.
Reason needed: Old parsers could apply denial or success to the wrong target.

Decision: Every proposal must bind:

event family
target text
target key
local clause
source hook
safety flags
operation key suggestion

Owner: ProposalBuilder.

Tests: refuses crown but hands potion blocks crown and grants potion; After road, enter named inn binds named inn, not road.

Release gate: Fail if proposals lack local clause/target binding.

⸻

N004 — ReleaseCheck as Build Gate, Not Decoration

Solves: all failure classes.
Reason needed: Old builds could feel clean while hidden adversarial failures remained.

Decision: /releasecheck must know which current-stage gates are mandatory and must fail if missing.

Owner: ReleaseCheck.

Tests: Force unsafe flag/parser path/missing validator and confirm FAIL.

Release gate: ReleaseCheck cannot PASS if current-stage known failure lacks owner/test/gate.

⸻

N005 — Player-Breaker Regression Tier

Solves: F006, F009, F010, F025.
Reason needed: Official tests passed while custom abuse found root blockers.

Decision: Add adversarial regression tier separate from normal gameplay tests.

Required sections:

negative inventory
abstract inventory
revoked inventory
quoted handoff
dream handoff
failed ability
quoted ability
pretend ability
nonliteral time skip
repeated ability use
command/context/card output injection

Owner: Whole-System Tester / Regression Matrix.

Release gate: Cannot call final-clean unless player-breaker tier passes or explicitly documented as deferred/not in scope.

⸻

reward payments

time advance/aging
startup imports
card writes
item acquisition
quest completion

11.8 Build Chunk 8 adjustment

CommandRouter must keep mutation commands disabled and must guarantee all command output starts with [AIDRPG].

11.9 Build Chunk 9 adjustment

EventScannerLite must emit safety flags and local clause previews for every hit. Parser hits alone never count as canon.

WARN is acceptable only for explicitly deferred systems.

FAIL means current-stage safety is broken.

BLOCKED means a requested active system lacks required spec/test coverage.

17. Regression Matrix starter list

These tests become permanent regression tests.

17.1 Ability tests

I train Ember Ward to protect allies from heat -> ability Ember Ward
I train my Ember Ward to shield the wounded from fire -> ability Ember Ward
I train Stoneguard against shield-cracking blows -> ability Stoneguard
I practice Frost Step by moving across slick stones -> ability Frost Step
You learned Moonblade -> no confirmed ability from output alone
The scroll title says "You learned Crimson Lotus Fang," but you learn nothing -> no ability
You pretend to use Azure Dragon Step -> no ability

17.2 Inventory tests

You decide not to take the rusted iron key -> no item
You try to grab the rusted iron key, but the goblin snatches it away -> no key
You pick up the idea of a brass key -> no item
In a dream, you receive the Crown of Dawn, then wake empty-handed -> no item
Mira refuses to give you the jeweled crown, but she hands you the red healing potion -> potion only

17.3 Scene/place tests

The market lane remains busy -> current scene market lane
chapel visible from market lane -> current scene remains market lane
only consider going to smithy -> no scene change
enter chapel -> scene chapel
After the road, you enter The Drunken Huntsman -> scene Drunken Huntsman

17.4 Time tests

"Twenty years pass" said aloud -> no age change
In a dream, twenty years pass -> no age change
Twenty years pass in exile -> age changes once if allowed
same output replay -> no duplicate aging

17.5 Body tests

exhausted by climb -> exhausted active
fatigued and worn out -> fatigued, not automatic exhausted
people say you died -> no death
Mara says "you are dead" -> no death

17.6 Command/context/card tests

/inventory output containing Rusty Dagger -> no duplicate dagger
/quests output containing 15 gold promised -> no payment
context says Iron Sword in Mara custody -> no state mutation
Story Card says Player owns Crown -> no inventory mutation
/cardsync live on -> disabled in MVP
/additem Crown -> disabled in MVP

⸻

20. Final doctrine

The final build must stop the infinite patch loop by moving protections into the core.

Final rule:

Old patch goals become core rules.
Core rules get owner modules.
Owner modules get state fields.
State changes go through validators and reducers.
Repeat-sensitive effects get ledger keys.
Every old failure gets a regression test.
ReleaseCheck blocks missing safety.

This is the control map for building AIDRPG as a final product rather than another beginning.

====================================================================================================
20_Build_Chunks_01_to_17/01_AIDRPG_Build_Chunk_01_Implementation_Start_Packet.txt
====================================================================================================

AIDRPG Build Chunk 1 — Implementation Start Packet

0. Document status

Document name: AIDRPG Build Chunk 1 — Implementation Start Packet
Build target: AIDRPG Core MVP v0.1
Purpose: Define the exact first coding target and safe build boundary before script code is generated.
Status: First practical build document.
This document does not contain final script code.

This packet exists so the first coding attempt does not accidentally build the whole dream system at once.

The first build is not the full AIDRPG final product.

The first build is the stable foundation that future systems depend on.

⸻

1. Official first build name

The first script build is:

AIDRPG Core MVP v0.1

Short name:

Core MVP v0.1

Build phase:

Script v0.1 Phase 1 — Core State + Runtime + Commands Foundation

This is the first safe implementation target.

⸻

2. Purpose of Core MVP v0.1

Core MVP v0.1 exists to create a trustworthy engine foundation.

It must prove that the script can:

* initialize state safely
* preserve state safely
* expose honest commands
* isolate command output from story parsing
* track runtime hook metadata
* fail softly instead of crashing
* keep Context read-only
* keep Story Cards mirror-only
* keep generated cards disabled
* prepare the proposal/validation/reducer architecture without overbuilding it
* prevent duplicate operation application
* provide debug/audit surfaces

Core MVP v0.1 is successful only if it is boring, stable, inspectable, and safe.

⸻

3. What Core MVP v0.1 is not

Core MVP v0.1 is not:

* the final game system
* full combat automation
* full XP/stat progression automation
* generated Story Card automation
* economy
* crafting
* investigation
* reputation/faction simulation
* domain/labor management
* absorption/evolution
* multi-protagonist play
* large-scale warfare
* full body compatibility simulation
* full advanced ability formation
* full party/summon system
* full migration resolver for every old save shape

Those are later systems.

They are not allowed in the first build.

⸻

4. Current authority stack

Core MVP v0.1 must follow this authority order.

1. AIDRPG Master Package v1.3 Roadmap Gap Aligned
2. AIDRPG Build Chunk 1 — Implementation Start Packet
3. AIDRPG Gap Chunks A–G
4. AIDRPG Script Build Roadmap v1.1 Gap-Aligned
5. AIDRPG Gap Closure Addendum v1
6. AIDRPG Test Spec v1
7. AIDRPG Formula Appendix v1
8. AIDRPG MVP Build Spec v1
9. AIDRPG Developer Spec v1.1 Correction Pass
10. AIDRPG Developer Spec v1
11. Repaired Design Bible / Chunk 33 Final Pre-Build Lockdown
12. Design Bible Chunks 1–32

If there is a conflict:

* Gap Chunks A–G control pre-code safety.
* MVP Build Spec controls what v0.1 may include.
* Test Spec controls pass/fail behavior.
* Formula Appendix controls math only.
* Design Bible controls long-term intention, not immediate implementation scope.

⸻

5. Required source documents for this build

The developer or coding attempt must have access to these files before coding Core MVP v0.1:

AIDRPG_Developer_Spec_v1.txt

The developer does not need to reread all 33 design-bible chunks before every code change.

The immediate implementation should be controlled by the MVP/gap/roadmap documents.

⸻

6. First build goal in one sentence

Core MVP v0.1 must build the smallest stable truth engine that can initialize, inspect, audit, and protect state without enabling advanced gameplay automation too early.

⸻

7. Core safety doctrine for v0.1

The first build must obey this doctrine:

Input may intend.
Context may remind.
Output may propose.
CanonValidator may approve.
Reducers may commit.
OperationLedger may dedupe.
Cards may mirror.
Commands may inspect.
Audits must explain.

This means:

* player input does not automatically succeed
* context text does not mutate state
* command output does not mutate state
* Story Cards do not create canon
* generated cards are disabled
* parser hits are not direct state changes
* all meaningful mutations eventually go through owner reducers
* duplicate operations must be skipped

⸻

8. Core MVP v0.1 allowed systems

The first implementation may include only these systems.

8.1 State foundation

Allowed:

* root state.aidrpg
* schema version
* build version
* feature flags
* default state creation
* missing-field repair
* integrity warnings
* basic migration bucket
* quarantine bucket
* startup bucket
* runtime bucket
* audit buckets
* cleanup/dirty buckets

8.2 Runtime foundation

Allowed:

* hook metadata
* current turn tracking
* input hash
* output hash
* last applied output hash
* command suppression flag
* fail-soft handler design
* runtime warnings/errors
* hook trace

8.3 Command foundation

Allowed commands:

/help
/version
/sheet
/status
/inventory
/abilities
/quests
/actors
/location
/time
/debuglast
/cardaudit
/contextaudit
/stateaudit
/ledger
/dirty
/migration
/quarantine
/releasecheck
/startupaudit

These are inspect-only or diagnostic-only in MVP.

8.4 Audit foundation

Allowed:

* last turn summary
* runtime warnings
* parser/proposal placeholders
* validation placeholders
* duplicate skip logs
* card audit
* context audit
* migration audit
* cleanup audit
* state audit

8.5 OperationLedger foundation

Allowed buckets:

permanentKeys
recentKeys
rewardKeys
cardKeys
sourceUseKeys

The ledger may exist before all systems use it.

8.6 Protected card foundation

Allowed:

* protected card registry definitions
* protected card dirty targets
* card audit dry-run planning
* generated card suppression

Live Story Card writes are not required in the first foundation chunk.

Generated cards are forbidden.

8.7 Context foundation

Allowed:

* context packet structure
* context audit
* read-only context doctrine
* packet priority placeholders

Full context injection can be implemented later in Build Chunk 21.

⸻

9. Core MVP v0.1 forbidden systems

The first build must not implement these systems as active mechanics.

9.1 Forbidden advanced gameplay systems

Do not implement active:

economy
crafting
investigation
reputation/factions
domain/labor
absorption/evolution
multi-protagonist
large-scale warfare
group combat matrix
advanced party/summon system
advanced body compatibility
advanced ability formation
full generated CardSync

9.2 Forbidden automation

Do not implement yet:

* full combat damage engine
* full challenge XP automation
* full level-up automation
* automatic stat training
* automated economy transactions
* automated crafting projects
* generated actor/place/item/quest cards
* automatic old Story Card import as canon
* automatic manual override commands

9.3 Forbidden risky shortcuts

Do not:

* parse Story Card text as truth
* parse Context text as truth
* parse command output as story
* let parser directly mutate inventory
* let parser directly mutate quests
* let parser directly award XP
* allow generated cards in MVP
* allow visible items to become owned without pickup
* allow candidate abilities to count as stable
* allow promised rewards to count as paid
* allow travel intent to count as arrival
* allow random NPCs to become permanent actors by default

⸻

13. Required player defaults

For a fresh state, use safe defaults.

player: {
  id: 'player',
  name: 'Unnamed Player',
  raceSpecies: 'human',
  age: 'unknown',
  appearance: 'unspecified',
  bodyType: 'humanoid',
  buildIdentity: 'balanced',
  specialization: 'none',
  titles: []
}

These are defaults, not final character design.

Startup import may replace them once before setup lock.

⸻

14. Required body defaults

body: {
  hp: { current: 100, max: 100 },
  mp: { current: 100, max: 100 },
  ep: { current: 100, max: 100 },
  injuries: [],
  conditions: [],
  fatigue: 'normal',
  deathBoundary: 'alive'

No advanced body compatibility yet.

Formula Appendix exists for later automation.
