⸻

26. First modules to build

Core MVP v0.1 should begin with these internal modules/sections.

00 Constants and Version
01 Utility Helpers
02 StateManager
03 RuntimeHookContract
04 CommandRouter
05 RenderCommands
06 AuditLogger
07 OperationLedger
08 ReleaseCheckLite
09 Hook Entry Points

Do not start with combat, cards, crafting, economy, or generated cards.

⸻

27. Module responsibilities for first build

27.1 Constants and Version

Defines:

* build name
* schema version
* feature flags
* command names
* protected card keys
* caps/budgets

27.2 Utility Helpers

Defines:

* safe object checks
* array caps
* stable key normalization
* hash helper
* text preview helper
* enum validation
* fail-soft helper

27.3 StateManager

Defines:

* initialize root state
* ensure missing buckets
* repair safe defaults
* migrate minimal old shape
* preserve valid state
* log integrity warnings

27.4 RuntimeHookContract

Defines:

* input/context/output hook wrappers
* current hook metadata
* turn handling
* hash tracking
* command suppression
* fail-soft behavior

27.5 CommandRouter

Defines:

* slash command detection
* command alias routing
* disabled command messages
* inspect-only policy
* command output suppression

27.6 RenderCommands

Defines outputs for:

* /help
* /version
* /sheet
* /status
* /inventory
* /abilities
* /quests
* /actors
* /location
* /time
* /debuglast
* /cardaudit
* /contextaudit
* /stateaudit
* /ledger
* /dirty
* /migration
* /quarantine
* /releasecheck
* /startupaudit

27.7 AuditLogger

Defines:

* append debug message
* append runtime warning
* append card audit
* append context audit
* append cleanup audit
* cap arrays

27.8 OperationLedger

Defines:

* hasKey
* addKey
* addRecentKey
* addPermanentKey
* addRewardKey
* addCardKey
* compactRecentKeys

27.9 ReleaseCheckLite

Defines initial release checks:

* root buckets exist
* feature flags safe
* generated cards disabled
* OperationLedger exists
* command output suppression exists
* context no-write declared
* deferred systems disabled

27.10 Hook Entry Points

Defines platform-facing hook wrappers.

They must return safe valid shapes.

⸻

28. First build parser/proposal boundary

Core MVP v0.1 may include parser/proposal placeholders.

It should not yet implement the full parser rulebook.

Allowed placeholder:

EventScannerLite exists as inert shell or debug-only placeholder.
ProposalBuilder exists as inert shell or debug-only placeholder.
CanonValidator exists as inert shell or minimal always-safe validator.
ReducerRegistry exists as inert shell.

The placeholders must not falsely claim full parsing works.

If included, /releasecheck should say:

Parser pipeline foundation present; full event scanning not active yet.

⸻

29. First build card boundary

Core MVP v0.1 may include:

* protected card key list
* card registry defaults
* dry-run card audit
* generated card disabled flag
* /cardaudit

Core MVP v0.1 must not include:

* generated card creation
* live Story Card writes without adapter tests
* automatic old card import as canon
* broad trigger generation

If CardSync is only dry-run, /cardaudit must say so.

⸻

30. First build context boundary

Core MVP v0.1 may include:

* ContextBuilderLite placeholder
* read-only context doctrine
* context audit placeholder
* no-write check in releasecheck

Core MVP v0.1 should not yet require full smart context packet generation unless the build chunk specifically reaches ContextBuilderLite later.

If context is placeholder, /contextaudit must say:

Context packet system not fully active yet; read-only guard present.

⸻

31. First build migration boundary

Core MVP v0.1 may include:

* migration bucket
* quarantine bucket
* safe missing-field repairs
* /migration
* /quarantine
* basic old-shape warnings

Core MVP v0.1 should not attempt aggressive old-save/card migration yet.

If old card migration is not fully implemented, /migration must say:

Legacy migration shell present; full polluted-save repair requires migration build step.

⸻

32. Required command honesty in v0.1

Every command must be honest about partial systems.

Examples:

/help

Must say:

AIDRPG Core MVP v0.1 is foundation-focused.
Generated cards, economy, crafting, investigation, domain, absorption, and advanced combat are disabled.

/cardaudit

Must say whether CardSync is:

dry-run
safe mode
live protected writes disabled
generated cards disabled

/releasecheck

Must not say PASS if required safety buckets are missing.

Disabled commands

Must not crash.

Example:

/craft is disabled in MVP. Crafting requires the Crafting Mini-Spec and later build phase.

⸻

33. Required first tests

Core MVP v0.1 must pass these first tests before expanding.

BC1-001 — Fresh state initializes

Given missing state.aidrpg, run script.

Expected:

* root state created
* required buckets exist
* generatedCardsEnabled false
* no crash

BC1-002 — Missing bucket repairs safely

Given state with missing inventory bucket but valid player data.

Expected:

* inventory bucket added
* player data preserved
* repair warning logged

BC1-003 — /help is honest

Run:

/help

Expected:

* active commands listed
* disabled systems listed
* no false claims about generated cards/economy/crafting

BC1-004 — Commands do not mutate gameplay

Run:

/inventory
/quests
/abilities

Expected:

* output displays state
* no new item/quest/ability created

BC1-005 — Command output suppression exists

Run /inventory displaying an item.

Expected:

* item does not duplicate from command output
* suppression flag/audit exists

BC1-006 — Runtime fail-soft exists

Simulate safe internal error.

Expected:

* valid return shape
* runtime warning/error logged
* state not erased

BC1-007 — Generated cards disabled

Check state and /cardaudit.

Expected:

* generatedCardsEnabled false
* generated creates per turn 0
* card audit says disabled

BC1-008 — OperationLedger exists

Check /ledger or /releasecheck.

Expected:

* permanentKeys, recentKeys, rewardKeys, cardKeys, sourceUseKeys exist

BC1-009 — /releasecheck catches unsafe flag

Set generatedCardsEnabled true in test state.

Expected:

* /releasecheck FAIL

BC1-010 — Context declared read-only

Run Context hook or /contextaudit placeholder.

Expected:

* no gameplay mutation
* audit says read-only guard present

⸻

34. First blocker failures

Core MVP v0.1 cannot be considered ready if any of these are true:

* root state does not initialize
* commands crash
* /help lies about active systems
* generatedCardsEnabled is true
* command output can create items/quests/abilities
* Story Cards are treated as canon
* Context can mutate gameplay state
* OperationLedger buckets are missing
* /releasecheck missing or dishonest
* missing-field repair erases valid state
* deferred systems are active

⸻

35. What Build Chunk 2 should do next

After this Implementation Start Packet, the next build document is:

Build Chunk 2 — File/Module Layout Plan

Build Chunk 2 should define the exact single-file section order and internal module layout before code generation.

It should answer:

* what sections appear in the script
* what order they appear in
* what each section owns
* what section cannot call what
* how future chunks append safely
* how line budgets are respected

Do not write final script code until the layout is locked.

⸻

36. Final Build Chunk 1 doctrine

This packet begins implementation safely.

The first build must not chase every exciting feature.

The first build must make the engine hard to corrupt.

Final rule:

Build the stable truth shell first.
Do not build the whole game at once.
Do not activate deferred systems.
Do not let old bugs return through shortcuts.
Make commands honest.
Make state safe.
Make runtime fail softly.
Make duplicates impossible to apply silently.

Core MVP v0.1 begins here.

====================================================================================================
20_Build_Chunks_01_to_17/02_AIDRPG_Build_Chunk_02_File_and_Module_Layout_Plan.txt
====================================================================================================

AIDRPG Build Chunk 2 — File and Module Layout Plan

0. Document status

Document name: AIDRPG Build Chunk 2 — File and Module Layout Plan
Build target: AIDRPG Core MVP v0.1
Purpose: Define the exact script organization, section order, module boundaries, call rules, and append-safe layout before any final script code is generated.
Status: Practical build document.
Follows: Build Chunk 1 — Implementation Start Packet.
This document does not contain final script code.

This chunk exists because previous versions became difficult to maintain when fixes were layered into scattered patches, wrappers, late repair systems, and large tangled code blocks.

Core MVP v0.1 must be organized so future build chunks can add systems without breaking the foundation.

⸻

1. Purpose of Build Chunk 2

Build Chunk 2 locks the internal layout of the future script.

It answers:

* what sections the script should contain
* what order those sections should appear in
* what each module owns
* what each module must not own
* which modules may call each other
* which modules must stay read-only
* where future systems will attach later
* how to avoid patch-pile growth
* how to keep the file buildable in single-message chunks

This chunk still does not write the final script.

It prepares the code layout so script generation can start safely later.

⸻

2. Core layout doctrine

The first script must be structured as one clear engine, not a patch pile.

Doctrine:

One root namespace.
One state root.
One runtime contract.
One command router.
One audit layer.
One ledger.
One owner module per truth type.
No direct parser writes.
No card-to-canon path.
No context writes.
No generated cards in MVP.

The script may physically exist as one AI Dungeon script file, but internally it must behave like modules.

⸻

3. Single-file reality

AI Dungeon scripts are often edited as one large script body.

Therefore, the build should use a single-file modular layout.

That means:

* sections are clearly labeled
* module objects/functions are grouped together
* future chunks append or fill designated sections
* no random code is inserted between unrelated modules
* section order stays stable
* every future chunk declares touched sections

Do not create a scattered script where functions are pasted wherever convenient.

⸻

4. Recommended top-level namespace

Use one root namespace object internally.

Recommended name:

AIDRPG

All major modules should live under it conceptually:

AIDRPG.Constants
AIDRPG.Utils
AIDRPG.StateManager
AIDRPG.Runtime
AIDRPG.CommandRouter
AIDRPG.Commands
AIDRPG.AuditLogger
AIDRPG.OperationLedger
AIDRPG.ReleaseCheck
AIDRPG.EventScannerLite
AIDRPG.ProposalBuilder
AIDRPG.CanonValidator
AIDRPG.ReducerRegistry
AIDRPG.Reducers
AIDRPG.ContextBuilderLite
AIDRPG.CardSyncLite
AIDRPG.CleanupManagerLite
AIDRPG.Hooks

If platform constraints make namespace objects awkward, equivalent section-scoped constants/functions are acceptable, but naming must remain consistent.

⸻

5. Required section order

The final script should use this section order.

00 HEADER / BUILD WARNING
01 CONSTANTS AND VERSION
02 FEATURE FLAGS AND DEFAULTS
03 UTILITY HELPERS
04 STATE SCHEMA DEFAULTS
05 STATE MANAGER
06 RUNTIME HOOK CONTRACT
07 AUDIT LOGGER
08 OPERATION LEDGER
09 COMMAND ROUTER
10 COMMAND RENDERERS
11 RELEASE CHECK LITE
12 EVENT SCANNER LITE SHELL
13 PROPOSAL BUILDER SHELL
14 CANON VALIDATOR SHELL
15 REDUCER REGISTRY SHELL
16 MVP REDUCER SHELLS
17 CONTEXT BUILDER LITE SHELL
18 CARD SYNC LITE SHELL
19 CLEANUP MANAGER LITE SHELL
20 HOOK ENTRY POINTS
21 RESERVED FUTURE APPENDICES / DO NOT ACTIVATE

This order is intentional.

Foundational utilities and state come before runtime.

Runtime comes before commands.

Commands come before advanced parsing.

Parsing/validation/reducers are shells at first.

Context/Card/Cleanup shells come after the state and audit systems they depend on.

Hook entry points come last.

⸻

6. Section 00 — Header / Build Warning

Purpose

Make the script self-identifying and protect future users from misunderstanding the build stage.

Should contain

* build name
* build stage
* schema version
* generated cards disabled warning
* deferred systems warning
* no direct editing warning if relevant

Required header facts

AIDRPG Core MVP v0.1
Foundation build
Generated cards disabled
Advanced systems disabled
Cards mirror state only
Context read-only
Commands inspect-only

Must not contain

* gameplay logic
* parser rules
* hardcoded player-specific details

⸻

7. Section 01 — Constants and Version

Purpose

Hold stable constants used across the script.

Owns

* project name
* build name
* build stage
* schema version
* command list
* feature names
* protected card keys
* status enum values
* access state enum values
* caps and limits

Must include constants for

PROJECT_NAME
BUILD_NAME
BUILD_STAGE
SCHEMA_VERSION
MAX_RECENT_KEYS
MAX_DIRTY_TARGETS
MAX_AUDIT_ENTRIES
MAX_RUNTIME_WARNINGS
MAX_RUNTIME_ERRORS
MAX_HOOK_TRACE
PROTECTED_CARD_KEYS
MVP_ACTIVE_COMMANDS
MVP_DISABLED_SYSTEMS

Must not own

* mutable state
* command output text generation
* parser decisions

⸻

8. Section 02 — Feature Flags and Defaults

Purpose

Define default enabled/disabled system flags.

Owns default flags

generatedCardsEnabled = false
protectedCardWritesEnabled = false
cardSyncDryRun = true
cardSyncSafeMode = true
economyEnabled = false
craftingEnabled = false
investigationEnabled = false
reputationEnabled = false
domainEnabled = false
absorptionEnabled = false
multiProtagonistEnabled = false
advancedCombatEnabled = false
manualMutationCommandsEnabled = false

Must not allow

* generated cards active by default
* economy/crafting/investigation/domain active by default
* manual mutation commands active by default

Dependency

Used by StateManager and ReleaseCheckLite.

⸻

9. Section 03 — Utility Helpers

Purpose

Provide safe small helpers used by all modules.

Owns

* safe object checks
* safe array checks
* text preview
* stable normalization
* simple hashing
* cap array helper
* enum validation
* clone/copy helper if needed
* safe number clamp
* safe string conversion
* operation key normalization

Recommended utility functions

isObject(value)
ensureArray(value)
safeString(value)
normalizeKey(text)
shortPreview(text, maxLength)
simpleHash(text)
capArray(array, max)
clampNumber(value, min, max)
ensureEnum(value, allowed, fallback)
nowTurn(state)

Must not own

* gameplay decisions
* validation rules
* state mutation outside helper returns
* card writes

Important rule

Utilities must stay boring.

Do not hide gameplay behavior inside helpers.

⸻

10. Section 04 — State Schema Defaults

Purpose

Define default shapes for every root state bucket.

Owns default factory functions

createDefaultMeta()
createDefaultFeatures()
createDefaultRuntime()
createDefaultStartup()
createDefaultPlayer()
createDefaultBody()
createDefaultProgression()
createDefaultInventory()
createDefaultAbilities()
createDefaultQuests()
createDefaultActors()
createDefaultPlaces()
createDefaultTime()
createDefaultCards()
createDefaultOperationLedger()
createDefaultAudits()
createDefaultMigration()
createDefaultQuarantine()
createDefaultDirty()
createDefaultCleanup()
createDefaultRootState()

Must not own

* runtime hook execution
* command rendering
* event scanning

Required rule

Default factories must not share mutable arrays/objects accidentally.

Each fresh state should get fresh arrays.

⸻

11. Section 05 — State Manager

Purpose

Own initialization, repair, migration shell, and integrity protection.

Owns

StateManager.getRoot()
StateManager.init()
StateManager.ensureDefaults()
StateManager.repairMissingBuckets()
StateManager.migrateIfNeeded()
StateManager.validateIntegrity()
StateManager.markGameplayStarted()
StateManager.lockStartup()

May mutate

* root state shape
* missing buckets
* schema repair fields
* migration warnings
* startup lock flags
* audit repair warnings

Must not mutate

* inventory from prose
* quests from cards
* abilities from old card text
* generated cards
* advanced systems

Must preserve

* valid existing player data
* valid inventory data
* valid ability data
* valid quest data
* valid actor/place data
* operation keys

Call dependencies

May call:

* Constants
* Defaults
* Utils
* AuditLogger for warnings, if already defined or via safe deferred audit pattern

Must not call:

* EventScanner
* Reducers
* CardSync live writes

⸻

12. Section 06 — Runtime Hook Contract

Purpose

Own Input/Context/Output hook safety.

Owns

Runtime.beginHook(hookName, text)
Runtime.endHook(hookName, result)
Runtime.failSoft(hookName, phase, error, originalText)
Runtime.hashInput(text)
Runtime.hashOutput(text)
Runtime.isDuplicateOutput(root, outputHash)
Runtime.markOutputApplied(root, outputHash)
Runtime.setCommandSuppression(root, commandName, commandHash)
Runtime.clearCommandSuppression(root)
Runtime.shouldSuppressScanForCommandOutput(root, text)

May mutate

* runtime hook metadata
* current turn
* hook trace
* runtime warnings/errors
* command suppression fields
* input/output hashes

Must not mutate

* gameplay state directly
* inventory
* quests
* abilities
* actors
* places
* cards

Hook boundary rule

Runtime may manage hook metadata only.

It does not decide gameplay truth.

⸻

13. Section 07 — Audit Logger

Purpose

Provide capped audit writing across modules.

Owns

AuditLogger.debug(root, message)
AuditLogger.warn(root, message)
AuditLogger.runtimeError(root, record)
AuditLogger.card(root, message)
AuditLogger.context(root, message)
AuditLogger.cleanup(root, message)
AuditLogger.migration(root, message)
AuditLogger.validation(root, record)
AuditLogger.proposal(root, record)
AuditLogger.release(root, result)
AuditLogger.capAll(root)

May mutate

* audit buckets only

Must not mutate

* gameplay truth
* cards
* dirty targets, unless explicitly audit dirty later

Important rule

Audit output is non-canon.

Command renderers may show audit entries, but they must not become parser sources.

⸻

14. Section 08 — Operation Ledger

Purpose

Prevent duplicate operation application.

Owns

OperationLedger.hasAny(root, key)
OperationLedger.hasRecent(root, key)
OperationLedger.hasPermanent(root, key)
OperationLedger.hasReward(root, key)
OperationLedger.hasCard(root, key)
OperationLedger.addRecent(root, key)
OperationLedger.addPermanent(root, key)
OperationLedger.addReward(root, key)
OperationLedger.addCard(root, key)
OperationLedger.addSourceUse(root, key)
OperationLedger.compact(root)

May mutate

* operationLedger only

Must not mutate

* inventory
* XP
* quests
* cards except ledger card keys

Important rule

Ledger records dedupe keys.

Ledger does not itself perform gameplay effects.

⸻

15. Section 09 — Command Router

Purpose

Detect commands, route them, enforce inspect-only MVP behavior, and suppress command output scanning.

Owns

CommandRouter.isCommand(text)
CommandRouter.parseCommand(text)
CommandRouter.route(root, commandText)
CommandRouter.resolveAlias(commandName)
CommandRouter.renderDisabled(commandName, reason)
CommandRouter.markSuppression(root, commandName, outputText)

May mutate

* runtime command suppression fields
* audit diagnostic fields

Must not mutate gameplay

MVP command router must not add/remove:

* items
* quests
* abilities
* actors
* XP
* HP/MP/EP
* location
* cards

Alias handling

Aliases should be defined here, not scattered.

Examples:

/questlog -> /quests
/finalcheck -> /releasecheck
/cardedits -> /cardaudit

⸻

16. Section 10 — Command Renderers

Purpose

Render human-readable command outputs.

Owns renderers for

renderHelp(root)
renderVersion(root)
renderSheet(root)
renderStatus(root)
renderInventory(root)
renderAbilities(root)
renderQuests(root)
renderActors(root)
renderLocation(root)
renderTime(root)
renderDebugLast(root)
renderCardAudit(root)
renderContextAudit(root)
renderStateAudit(root)
renderLedger(root)
renderDirty(root)
renderMigration(root)
renderQuarantine(root)
renderReleaseCheck(root)
renderStartupAudit(root)
renderDisabledSystem(root, systemName)

May read

* all state buckets needed for display

May mutate

* nothing except maybe audit that command was run, if needed

Must not mutate gameplay

Renderers are read-only.

Output style

Command output should be readable and concise.

Do not dump full raw JSON by default.

⸻

17. Section 11 — Release Check Lite

Purpose

Provide the first readiness checker.

Owns

ReleaseCheck.run(root)
ReleaseCheck.checkMeta(root)
ReleaseCheck.checkFeatures(root)
ReleaseCheck.checkRuntime(root)
ReleaseCheck.checkStateBuckets(root)
ReleaseCheck.checkCommands(root)
ReleaseCheck.checkLedger(root)
ReleaseCheck.checkCards(root)
ReleaseCheck.checkContext(root)
ReleaseCheck.checkDeferredSystems(root)
ReleaseCheck.formatResult(result)

May read

* all state/config buckets

May mutate

* releaseCheck audit result

Must not mutate gameplay

ReleaseCheck reports, it does not fix gameplay truth.

Required result levels

PASS
WARN
FAIL
BLOCKED

⸻

18. Section 12 — Event Scanner Lite Shell

Purpose

Reserve the parser scanner location without activating full parsing too early.

Owns initially

EventScannerLite.scanInputIntent(text, root)
EventScannerLite.scanOutput(text, root)

v0.1 initial behavior

In the first foundation build, this may return empty arrays or very limited debug-safe hits.

It must not mutate state.

Future expansion

Build Chunk 9 will define real EventScannerLite behavior.

Must not do yet

* item pickup parsing
* reward parsing
* ability success parsing
* quest automation
* XP automation
* actor/place promotion

unless later build chunks explicitly unlock them.

⸻

19. Section 13 — Proposal Builder Shell

Purpose

Reserve the place where parser hits become EventProposal objects.

Owns initially

ProposalBuilder.fromParserHit(hit, root)
ProposalBuilder.createDebugProposal(type, data)

v0.1 initial behavior

May be inert or minimal.

Must not mutate state.

Future expansion

Build Chunk 10 will define the full EventProposal schema and creation behavior.

⸻

20. Section 14 — Canon Validator Shell

Purpose

Reserve the central validation gate.

Owns initially

CanonValidator.validate(proposal, root)
CanonValidator.reject(reason)
CanonValidator.accept(notes)
CanonValidator.pending(reason)

v0.1 initial behavior

If no real proposal pipeline is active yet, it may exist as a shell.

If called, default should be conservative.

Recommended safe default:

unknown proposal types reject or pending

Must not do

* accept every event by default
* bypass OperationLedger
* mutate state

Future expansion

Build Chunk 11 defines full validation rules.

⸻

21. Section 15 — Reducer Registry Shell

Purpose

Reserve owner reducer routing.

Owns initially

ReducerRegistry.getOwner(eventType)
ReducerRegistry.apply(validatedProposal, root)
ReducerRegistry.unknownReducerResult(proposal)

v0.1 initial behavior

May reject unknown proposals and avoid active gameplay commits.

Must not do

* route unknown events to random reducers
* commit unvalidated proposals
* mutate multiple truth buckets without ownership

Future expansion

Build Chunk 12 defines full routing.

⸻

22. Section 16 — MVP Reducer Shells

Purpose

Reserve owner reducers without fully implementing every mechanic yet.

Shell reducers

BodyReducer
InventoryReducer
AbilityReducer
QuestReducerLite
ActorReducerLite
PlaceReducerLite
TimeReducerLite
ProgressionReducerLite

v0.1 initial behavior

Reducers may expose empty/safe apply methods and dirty target helper placeholders.

Must not do yet

* full combat
* full inventory parsing
* full quest rewards
* full progression automation
* generated cards

Future expansion

Build Chunks 14–20 define reducer behavior in detail.

⸻

23. Section 17 — Context Builder Lite Shell

Purpose

Reserve read-only context generation.

Owns initially

ContextBuilderLite.buildPackets(root)
ContextBuilderLite.serialize(packets, root)
ContextBuilderLite.audit(root, packets, skipped)

v0.1 initial behavior

May return minimal safe context or placeholder.

Must be read-only.

Must not do

* mutate gameplay state
* create proposals
* create cards
* import card text

Future expansion

Build Chunk 21 defines full ContextBuilderLite.

⸻

24. Section 18 — Card Sync Lite Shell

Purpose

Reserve Story Card mirror logic without unsafe live writes.

Owns initially

CardSyncLite.ensureRegistry(root)
CardSyncLite.planProtectedUpdates(root)
CardSyncLite.audit(root)
CardSyncLite.suppressGeneratedCards(root)

v0.1 initial behavior

Dry-run/audit only.

Generated cards disabled.

Live writes disabled unless later explicitly unlocked.

Must not do

* live generated card creation
* live protected writes without adapter tests
* parse Story Card text
* import old card prose as canon

Future expansion

Build Chunk 22 defines protected CardSyncLite.

Generated card beta later after MVP tests.

⸻

25. Section 19 — Cleanup Manager Lite Shell

Purpose

Reserve conservative cleanup behavior.

Owns initially

CleanupManagerLite.lightCleanup(root)
CleanupManagerLite.compactAudits(root)
CleanupManagerLite.compactDirtyQueue(root)
CleanupManagerLite.audit(root, message)

v0.1 initial behavior

May cap audits and dirty queue only.

Must not do

* delete active inventory
* delete active quests
* delete important actors
* delete protected card registry
* resolve quarantine automatically
* create truth

Future expansion

Build Chunk F already defines safety; later coding can implement it.

⸻

26. Section 20 — Hook Entry Points

Purpose

Expose actual AI Dungeon hook functions.

Owns

Platform-facing wrappers like:

inputModifier(text)
contextModifier(text)
outputModifier(text)

or platform equivalent.

Required behavior

Input entry point:

StateManager.init
Runtime.beginHook('input')
CommandRouter.route if command
Intent shell only if non-command
Runtime.endHook
return safe shape

Context entry point:

StateManager.init
Runtime.beginHook('context')
ContextBuilderLite read-only shell
Runtime.endHook
return safe shape

Output entry point:

StateManager.init
Runtime.beginHook('output')
Duplicate output check
Scanner/Proposal/Validator/Reducer shells if active
CardSync/Cleanup shells if safe
Runtime.endHook
return safe shape

Must not do

* direct gameplay mutation in entry points
* raw Story Card API calls
* direct inventory/quest/XP changes

Entry points orchestrate; modules own behavior.

⸻

27. Section 21 — Reserved Future Appendices / Do Not Activate

Purpose

Make clear where future systems belong without activating them.

May include comments/placeholders for:

EconomyReducer later
CraftingReducer later
InvestigationReducer later
ReputationReducer later
DomainReducer later
PartySystem later
ScaleCombatSystem later
AbsorptionEvolutionSystem later
GeneratedCardSync beta later
WholeSystemTester later

Must include warning

Reserved only. Do not activate without mini-spec and build chunk.

⸻

28. Allowed call graph

The first build should follow this call graph.

28.1 Universal dependencies

Most modules may call:

Constants
Utils
AuditLogger

28.2 StateManager may call

Constants
Defaults
Utils
AuditLogger

StateManager must not call:

EventScanner
Reducers
CardSync live writes
ContextBuilder

28.3 Runtime may call

Constants
Utils
StateManager
AuditLogger
CommandRouter only through hook orchestration

Runtime must not call reducers directly.

28.4 CommandRouter may call

CommandRenderers
Runtime suppression helpers
AuditLogger
ReleaseCheck

CommandRouter must not call gameplay reducers in MVP.

28.5 CommandRenderers may call

Utils
ReleaseCheck formatter

CommandRenderers must not mutate gameplay state.

28.6 ReleaseCheck may call

Utils
AuditLogger

ReleaseCheck may read all state.

ReleaseCheck must not repair gameplay.

28.7 EventScanner may call

Utils

EventScanner must not call reducers.

28.8 ProposalBuilder may call

Utils
Resolver later

ProposalBuilder must not call reducers.

28.9 CanonValidator may call

OperationLedger read checks
Resolvers later
Utils

CanonValidator must not mutate gameplay except maybe audit through AuditLogger.

28.10 ReducerRegistry may call

owner reducers
OperationLedger after validation
AuditLogger

ReducerRegistry must not route unknown events loosely.

28.11 Reducers may call

Utils
OperationLedger if commit design requires
AuditLogger
DirtyQueue helpers

Reducers must not call parser or context.

28.12 ContextBuilder may call

Utils
AuditLogger

ContextBuilder must not call reducers or CardSync writes.

28.13 CardSync may call

Utils
AuditLogger
OperationLedger card keys
CardApiAdapter later

CardSync must not call parsers or reducers.

28.14 CleanupManager may call

Utils
AuditLogger
OperationLedger cleanup keys if needed

CleanupManager must not call parsers or create gameplay events.

⸻

29. Forbidden call paths

These call paths are forbidden.

EventScanner -> InventoryReducer direct
EventScanner -> QuestReducer direct
EventScanner -> ProgressionReducer direct
ContextBuilder -> any gameplay reducer
CardSync -> any gameplay reducer
CommandRenderer -> any gameplay reducer
Story Card API -> CanonValidator as input source
ReleaseCheck -> gameplay mutation
CleanupManager -> create item/quest/actor/XP
StateManager migration -> import Story Card prose as canon
Hook entry point -> direct item/quest/XP mutation

If a future chunk needs an exception, it must be explicitly specified and tested.

⸻

30. Append-safe coding policy

Future build chunks should be append-safe.

Each coding chunk must declare:

Touched sections:
New functions added:
Existing functions modified:
Why modification is required:
Tests affected:
Deferred systems still disabled:
Rollback note:

30.1 No silent rewrites

Do not rewrite the full script unless the build explicitly calls for a consolidation version.

30.2 No hidden patches

Do not add surprise modules like:

FinalFixSystem
EmergencyPatchSystem
RandomRepairLayer
PolishEverythingSystem

Every fix belongs to an owner module.

30.3 No ambiguous insertion instructions

Avoid instructions like:

Find the old function and replace part of it.

Prefer:

In Section 05 StateManager, replace function StateManager.ensureDefaults with this full function.

or:

Append this function at the end of Section 08 OperationLedger.

⸻

31. Line budget policy

The final code should remain manageable.

31.1 Per production chunk budget

Recommended:

Target max per coding chunk: 700 lines
Absolute max per coding chunk: 800 lines

If a coding chunk would exceed the limit, split it.

31.2 Per section caution

If any section becomes too large, future chunks should consider:

* sub-section labels
* helper extraction
* shell now, expansion later
* separate later build chunk

31.3 No giant all-at-once script

Do not attempt to generate the entire final AIDRPG script in one response.

The first code version should be foundation only.

⸻

32. Naming conventions

32.1 Function names

Use clear module-prefixed names if namespace style is not possible.

Examples:

StateManager_init
Runtime_beginHook
CommandRouter_route
ReleaseCheck_run

If namespace style is possible:

StateManager.init
Runtime.beginHook
CommandRouter.route
ReleaseCheck.run

32.2 IDs and keys

Use normalized lowercase snake keys.

Examples:

protected:player_sheet
item:rusty_dagger
actor:mara
quest:clear_mill_wolves
ability:mana_slash

32.3 Operation keys

Use dot-separated operation keys.

Examples:

item.pickup.rusty_dagger.scene_12
quest.reward.clear_mill_wolves.old_bren
card.update.protected.player_sheet.hash_abc
startup.import.player.name

⸻

33. Commenting policy

Comments should explain boundaries, not narrate obvious code.

Good comments:

// Context is read-only. Do not mutate gameplay state here.
// Generated cards are disabled in MVP.
// Parser shell must not commit state directly.

Bad comments:

// increment i
// set x to y

Important safety comments should stay near the relevant section.

⸻

34. Initial implementation sequence after this layout

Once this layout is accepted, actual code should be built in this order:

1. Header / constants / feature defaults
2. Utility helpers
3. State schema defaults
4. StateManager init/repair
5. AuditLogger basic caps
6. Runtime hook metadata/fail-soft
7. OperationLedger shell
8. CommandRouter + command renderers
9. ReleaseCheckLite
10. Hook entry points
11. Inert scanner/proposal/validator/reducer/context/card/cleanup shells
12. Run first tests

Do not code advanced parser/reducer systems before the foundation tests pass.

⸻

35. Build Chunk 2 acceptance tests

These are planning/layout acceptance tests, not runtime script tests yet.

BC2-001 — Section order exists

Expected:

* final code plan has sections 00–21 in the required order

Fail if:

* modules are unordered or scattered

BC2-002 — Each module has one owner role

Expected:

* StateManager owns state init/repair
* Runtime owns hook metadata
* CommandRouter owns commands
* AuditLogger owns audits
* OperationLedger owns dedupe

Fail if:

* multiple modules own same core truth without boundary

BC2-003 — Forbidden call paths listed

Expected:

* parser cannot call reducers directly
* context cannot call reducers
* cards cannot feed canon
* commands cannot mutate gameplay in MVP

Fail if:

* forbidden paths are unspecified

BC2-004 — Deferred systems reserved but inactive

Expected:

* economy/crafting/investigation/domain/absorption/generated cards are reserved only

Fail if:

* layout implies they are active in v0.1

BC2-005 — Append-safe policy defined

Expected:

* future chunks must declare touched sections and modified functions

Fail if:

* future edits can be pasted randomly

BC2-006 — Line budget defined

Expected:

* coding chunks target 700 lines, absolute 800 lines

Fail if:

* no production size control exists

BC2-007 — Hook entry points last

Expected:

* platform-facing hooks appear after all internal modules

Fail if:

* hook logic appears before dependencies or contains direct state mutation

BC2-008 — Shell modules are honest

Expected:

* parser/validator/reducer/context/card shells do not claim full automation yet

Fail if:

* placeholders pretend to implement systems not active

⸻

36. Relationship to Gap Chunks A–G

Build Chunk 2 incorporates Gap Chunks A–G by enforcing these layout decisions:

* Gap A: old fixes become owner modules, not patches
* Gap B: runtime hook contract has its own section
* Gap C: CardSync and card API are isolated from canon
* Gap D: parser/resolver have shells and cannot mutate state
* Gap E: migration/releasecheck/command honesty have dedicated sections
* Gap F: context/dirty/cleanup/performance have dedicated shells and caps
* Gap G: startup/identity/natural confirmation are represented in state/defaults and future parser/validator boundaries

⸻

37. What Build Chunk 3 should do next

Next build document:

Build Chunk 3 — State Schema Lock

Build Chunk 3 should fully lock field names and default structures for every root state bucket so coding can initialize state without guessing.

It should expand the defaults from Build Chunk 1 into a precise schema document.

⸻

38. Final Build Chunk 2 doctrine

AIDRPG must be built as a controlled engine, not as a growing pile of fixes.

The final rule is:

Stable section order.
One owner per truth type.
No forbidden call paths.
No hidden patch systems.
No active deferred systems.
Append safely or do not append.

With this layout locked, the next safe step is to lock the complete state schema.

====================================================================================================
20_Build_Chunks_01_to_17/03_AIDRPG_Build_Chunk_03_State_Schema_Lock.txt
====================================================================================================

AIDRPG Build Chunk 3 — State Schema Lock

0. Document status

Document name: AIDRPG Build Chunk 3 — State Schema Lock
Build target: AIDRPG Core MVP v0.1
Purpose: Lock the exact state schema, bucket names, default values, ownership rules, and mutation boundaries before final script code is generated.
Status: Practical build document.
Follows: Build Chunk 2 — File and Module Layout Plan.
This document does not contain final script code.

This chunk exists because the script cannot be stable if each build step invents field names while coding.

The state schema must be locked before StateManager, commands, runtime hooks, validation, reducers, ContextBuilder, CardSync, migration, cleanup, or tests are coded.

⸻

1. Purpose of Build Chunk 3

Build Chunk 3 defines the canonical root state shape for AIDRPG Core MVP v0.1.

It answers:

* what state.aidrpg contains
* what every root bucket is named
* what every bucket owns
* what default values are used
* which module may mutate each bucket
* which modules may only read each bucket
* what must be preserved during repair/migration
* what must never be inferred from cards/context/commands
* what fields are MVP-active versus placeholder/deferred

The goal is to let the first real code build initialize state without guessing.

⸻

2. Core schema doctrine

AIDRPG uses one canonical state root:

state.aidrpg

All AIDRPG gameplay truth, runtime metadata, audits, migration records, card registry data, and feature flags live under that root.

No major system should create hidden sibling roots like:

state.tasis
state.playerData
state.cardSync
state.inventorySystem
state.patchData

If legacy roots are found later, migration may read/quarantine them, but new truth belongs under state.aidrpg.

⸻

3. Required root state buckets

The root state must contain exactly these primary buckets for Core MVP v0.1:

state.aidrpg = {
  meta,
  features,
  runtime,
  startup,
  player,
  body,
  progression,
  inventory,
  abilities,
  quests,
  actors,
  places,
  time,
  cards,
  operationLedger,
  audits,
  migration,
  quarantine,
  dirty,
  cleanup
}

Every bucket must exist after StateManager initialization.

If a bucket is missing, StateManager must create that bucket without erasing valid existing buckets.

⸻

4. Root state ownership map

Bucket	Primary Owner	May Read	Must Not Mutate
meta	StateManager	all modules	parser, cards, context
features	StateManager / ReleaseCheck	all modules	parser, cards, context
runtime	RuntimeHookContract	commands, audits	reducers except via hook metadata
startup	StateManager / Startup importer later	commands, releasecheck	parser after setup lock
player	StateManager / future PlayerReducer	commands, context, cards	cards, context, commands
body	BodyReducer	commands, context, cards	parser direct, cards, context
progression	ProgressionReducerLite	commands, context, cards	parser direct, cards, context
inventory	InventoryReducer	commands, context, cards	parser direct, cards, context
abilities	AbilityReducer	commands, context, cards	parser direct, cards, context
quests	QuestReducerLite	commands, context, cards	parser direct, cards, context
actors	ActorReducerLite	commands, context, cards	parser direct, cards, context
places	PlaceReducerLite	commands, context, cards	parser direct, cards, context
time	TimeReducerLite	commands, context, cards	parser direct, cards, context
cards	CardSyncLite	commands, releasecheck	parser, context
operationLedger	OperationLedger	validators, reducers, audits	parser direct, cards, context
audits	AuditLogger	commands, releasecheck	parser as gameplay
migration	StateManager / Migration	commands, releasecheck	cards as canon
quarantine	Migration / StateManager	commands, releasecheck	cleanup as auto-resolver
dirty	DirtyQueue helpers / reducers	CardSync, ContextBuilder	dirty target as gameplay truth
cleanup	CleanupManagerLite	commands, releasecheck	cleanup creating truth

⸻

5. Schema version policy

5.1 Required fields

The schema must include:

meta.schemaVersion
meta.buildName
meta.buildStage
meta.projectName

5.2 MVP value

For Core MVP v0.1:

schemaVersion = 1
buildName = 'AIDRPG Core MVP v0.1'
buildStage = 'core_mvp_v0_1_foundation'
projectName = 'AIDRPG'

5.3 Version mutation rule

Only StateManager or explicit future migration may update schemaVersion.

No gameplay event may update schemaVersion.

⸻

6. meta schema

6.1 Purpose

meta identifies the project, build, schema version, and authority state.

6.2 Schema

meta: {
  projectName: string,
  buildName: string,
  buildStage: string,
  schemaVersion: number,
  packageAuthority: string,
  createdBy: string,
  createdTurn: number,
  lastUpdatedTurn: number,
  lastReleaseCheckResult: 'unknown' | 'PASS' | 'WARN' | 'FAIL' | 'BLOCKED',
  notes: string[]
}

6.3 Default

projectName = 'AIDRPG'
buildName = 'AIDRPG Core MVP v0.1'
buildStage = 'core_mvp_v0_1_foundation'
schemaVersion = 1
packageAuthority = 'v1.3 roadmap gap aligned'
createdBy = 'AIDRPG script'
createdTurn = 0
lastUpdatedTurn = 0
lastReleaseCheckResult = 'unknown'
notes = []

6.4 Mutation rules

May mutate:

* StateManager during initialization/migration
* ReleaseCheck may update lastReleaseCheckResult

Must not mutate:

* EventScanner
* ContextBuilder
* CardSync from card text
* Command renderers except ReleaseCheck result through official path

⸻

7. features schema

7.1 Purpose

features controls which systems are active.

7.2 Schema

features: {
  generatedCardsEnabled: boolean,
  protectedCardWritesEnabled: boolean,
  cardSyncDryRun: boolean,
  cardSyncSafeMode: boolean,
  economyEnabled: boolean,
  craftingEnabled: boolean,
  investigationEnabled: boolean,
  reputationEnabled: boolean,
  domainEnabled: boolean,
  absorptionEnabled: boolean,
  multiProtagonistEnabled: boolean,
  advancedCombatEnabled: boolean,
  manualMutationCommandsEnabled: boolean,
  fullParserEnabled: boolean,
  reducerCommitsEnabled: boolean,
  notes: string[]
}

7.3 Default

generatedCardsEnabled = false
protectedCardWritesEnabled = false
cardSyncDryRun = true
cardSyncSafeMode = true
economyEnabled = false
craftingEnabled = false
investigationEnabled = false
reputationEnabled = false
domainEnabled = false
absorptionEnabled = false
multiProtagonistEnabled = false
advancedCombatEnabled = false
manualMutationCommandsEnabled = false
fullParserEnabled = false
reducerCommitsEnabled = false or conservative shell mode until reducers are built
notes = []

7.4 Important rule

A disabled feature must not silently create state.

Example:

If craftingEnabled = false, then /craft must not create crafting projects.

If generatedCardsEnabled = false, no generated cards may be created.

⸻

8. runtime schema

8.1 Purpose

runtime tracks hook lifecycle, command suppression, duplicate output hashes, and fail-soft information.

8.2 Schema

runtime: {
  currentHook: 'none' | 'input' | 'context' | 'output' | 'command' | 'system',
  currentTurn: number,
  hookDepth: number,
  hookTrace: HookTraceRecord[],
  lastInputHash: string,
  lastContextHash: string,
  lastOutputHash: string,
  lastAppliedOutputHash: string,
  lastCommandHash: string,
  commandActive: boolean,
  suppressEventScanForCommandOutput: boolean,
  lastCommandTurn: number,
  lastCommandName: string,
  pendingInputIntentId: string,
  outputReplayDetected: boolean,
  runtimeWarnings: string[],
  runtimeErrors: RuntimeErrorRecord[],
  failSoftCount: number
}

8.3 HookTraceRecord

HookTraceRecord: {
  turn: number,
  hook: 'input' | 'context' | 'output' | 'command' | 'system',
  textHash: string,
  startedAt: string,
  endedAt: string,
  status: 'started' | 'completed' | 'failed' | 'fail_soft',
  notes: string[]
}

8.4 RuntimeErrorRecord

RuntimeErrorRecord: {
  turn: number,
  hook: string,
  phase: string,
  message: string,
  safeTextReturned: boolean,
  partialCommitBlocked: boolean
}

8.5 Defaults

currentHook = 'none'
currentTurn = 0
hookDepth = 0
hookTrace = []
lastInputHash = ''
lastContextHash = ''
lastOutputHash = ''
lastAppliedOutputHash = ''
lastCommandHash = ''
commandActive = false
suppressEventScanForCommandOutput = false
lastCommandTurn = 0
lastCommandName = ''
pendingInputIntentId = ''
outputReplayDetected = false
runtimeWarnings = []
runtimeErrors = []
failSoftCount = 0

8.6 Caps

hookTrace max = 30
runtimeWarnings max = 25
runtimeErrors max = 20

⸻

9. startup schema

9.1 Purpose

startup tracks one-time setup import and prevents repeated or drifting setup.

9.2 Schema

startup: {
  setupStatus: 'not_started' | 'in_progress' | 'completed' | 'completed_with_warnings' | 'locked' | 'failed',
  setupImported: boolean,
  gameplayStarted: boolean,
  setupLockedTurn: number,
  setupSourceHash: string,
  importedFields: StartupImportRecord[],
  rejectedFields: StartupRejectRecord[],
  warnings: string[],
  errors: string[],
  startupOperationKeys: string[]
}

9.3 StartupImportRecord

StartupImportRecord: {
  field: string,
  valuePreview: string,
  source: 'structured_setup' | 'initial_text' | 'manual' | 'migration',
  confidence: number,
  reason: string,
  turn: number
}

9.4 StartupRejectRecord

StartupRejectRecord: {
  field: string,
  rejectedValuePreview: string,
  source: string,
  reason: string,
  severity: 'info' | 'warning' | 'danger',
  turn: number
}

9.5 Defaults

setupStatus = 'not_started'
setupImported = false
gameplayStarted = false
setupLockedTurn = 0
setupSourceHash = ''
importedFields = []
rejectedFields = []
warnings = []
errors = []
startupOperationKeys = []

9.6 Mutation rule

Startup import may mutate starting truth only before setup lock.

After gameplayStarted = true, setup cannot casually overwrite player identity or starting inventory.

⸻

10. player schema

10.1 Purpose

player stores protected player identity and stable player profile fields.

10.2 Schema

player: {
  id: string,
  name: string,
  raceSpecies: string,
  age: string | number,
  appearance: string,
  bodyType: string,
  buildIdentity: string,
  specialization: string,
  titles: string[],
  nicknames: string[],
  origin: string,
  temporaryAppearance: string,
  activeDisguise: string,
  activeIllusion: string,
  identityWarnings: string[]
}

10.3 Defaults

id = 'player'
name = 'Unnamed Player'
raceSpecies = 'human'
age = 'unknown'
appearance = 'unspecified'
bodyType = 'humanoid'
buildIdentity = 'balanced'
specialization = 'none'
titles = []
nicknames = []
origin = ''
temporaryAppearance = ''
activeDisguise = ''
activeIllusion = ''
identityWarnings = []

10.4 Protected fields

These must not drift from vague narration:

name
raceSpecies
age
appearance
bodyType
buildIdentity
specialization

10.5 Mutation rule

Allowed mutation paths:

* startup import before lock
* validated transformation event later
* validated title grant later
* validated aging/time skip later
* explicit manual override later
* safe structured migration

Forbidden mutation paths:

* Story Card text
* Context text
* command output
* dream/vision
* disguise
* NPC introduction
* vague metaphor

⸻

11. body schema

11.1 Purpose

body stores HP/MP/EP, injuries, conditions, fatigue, and death boundary.

11.2 Schema

body: {
  hp: ResourceTrack,
  mp: ResourceTrack,
  ep: ResourceTrack,
  injuries: InjuryRecord[],
  conditions: ConditionRecord[],
  fatigue: 'normal' | 'tired' | 'exhausted' | 'collapsed' | 'unknown',
  deathBoundary: 'alive' | 'unconscious' | 'dying' | 'dead' | 'captured' | 'unknown',
  bodyTraits: string[],
  bodyWarnings: string[]
}

11.3 ResourceTrack

ResourceTrack: {
  current: number,
  max: number
}

11.4 InjuryRecord

InjuryRecord: {
  injuryId: string,
  name: string,
  location: string,
  severity: 'minor' | 'moderate' | 'severe' | 'critical' | 'unknown',
  status: 'active' | 'healing' | 'healed' | 'scarred',
  sourceEventKey: string,
  createdTurn: number,
  notes: string[]
}

11.5 ConditionRecord

ConditionRecord: {
  conditionId: string,
  name: string,
  severity: 'minor' | 'moderate' | 'severe' | 'critical' | 'unknown',
  status: 'active' | 'fading' | 'resolved',
  sourceEventKey: string,
  createdTurn: number,
  expiresTurn?: number,
  notes: string[]
}

11.6 Defaults

hp.current = 100
hp.max = 100
mp.current = 100
mp.max = 100
ep.current = 100
ep.max = 100
injuries = []
conditions = []
fatigue = 'normal'
deathBoundary = 'alive'
bodyTraits = []
bodyWarnings = []

11.7 Mutation owner

Only BodyReducer may mutate body gameplay fields after startup/migration.

Context, cards, commands, and parser hits may not mutate body directly.

⸻

12. progression schema

12.1 Purpose

progression stores level, XP, stats, and formula automation status.

12.2 Schema

progression: {
  level: number,
  currentXP: number,
  totalXP: number,
  xpToNext: number,
  stats: StatBlock,
  statXP: StatXPBlock,
  levelHistory: LevelHistoryRecord[],
  xpHistory: XPHistoryRecord[],
  formulaStatus: string,
  progressionWarnings: string[]
}

12.3 StatBlock

StatBlock: {
  ATK: number,
  DEF: number,
  SPD: number,
  INT: number,
  LCK: number
}

12.4 StatXPBlock

StatXPBlock: {
  ATK: number,
  DEF: number,
  SPD: number,
  INT: number,
  LCK: number
}

12.5 LevelHistoryRecord

LevelHistoryRecord: {
  fromLevel: number,
  toLevel: number,
  turn: number,
  sourceEventKey: string,
  notes: string[]
}

12.6 XPHistoryRecord

XPHistoryRecord: {
  amount: number,
  reason: string,
  sourceEventKey: string,
  turn: number,
  duplicateBlocked: boolean
}

12.7 Defaults

level = 1
currentXP = 0
totalXP = 0
xpToNext = 125
stats.ATK = 10
stats.DEF = 10
stats.SPD = 10
stats.INT = 10
stats.LCK = 10
statXP all = 0
levelHistory = []
xpHistory = []
formulaStatus = 'formula_appendix_v1_available_not_fully_automated'
progressionWarnings = []

12.8 Mutation owner

ProgressionReducerLite owns XP, levels, stats, and statXP.

Formula Appendix informs math but does not mutate state by itself.

⸻

13. inventory schema

13.1 Purpose

inventory stores owned items, equipment, currency, scene items, and custody/access states.

13.2 Schema

inventory: {
  items: ItemRecord[],
  equipped: EquippedRecord[],
  currency: CurrencyRecord,
  sceneItems: SceneItemRecord[],
  accessStates: string[],
  inventoryWarnings: string[]
}

13.3 ItemRecord

ItemRecord: {
  itemId: string,
  name: string,
  canonicalKey: string,
  itemType: string,
  quantity: number,
  owner: 'player' | 'actor' | 'unknown' | 'none',
  ownerId: string,
  custody: 'player' | 'actor' | 'place' | 'unknown',
  custodyId: string,
  accessState: 'held' | 'equipped' | 'stored' | 'npc_custody' | 'inaccessible' | 'lost' | 'sold' | 'destroyed' | 'consumed',
  equippedSlot: string,
  questLinked: boolean,
  unique: boolean,
  personalName: string,
  baseName: string,
  condition: 'new' | 'good' | 'worn' | 'damaged' | 'broken' | 'unknown',
  createdTurn: number,
  sourceEventKey: string,
  notes: string[]
}

13.4 EquippedRecord

EquippedRecord: {
  slot: string,
  itemId: string
}

13.5 CurrencyRecord

CurrencyRecord: {
  copper: number,
  silver: number,
  gold: number,
  abstractValue: number,
  notes: string[]
}

MVP may use whichever currency fields are relevant, but must not treat future economy as active.

13.6 SceneItemRecord

SceneItemRecord: {
  sceneItemId: string,
  name: string,
  canonicalKey: string,
  itemType: string,
  quantity: number,
  accessState: 'visible' | 'available_loot' | 'inaccessible',
  locationId: string,
  sceneId: string,
  custodyId: string,
  questLinked: boolean,
  expiresOnSceneChange: boolean,
  createdTurn: number,
  sourceEventKey: string,
  notes: string[]
}

13.7 Defaults

items = []
equipped = []
currency.copper = 0
currency.silver = 0
currency.gold = 0
currency.abstractValue = 0
currency.notes = []
sceneItems = []
accessStates = ['visible','available_loot','held','equipped','stored','npc_custody','inaccessible','lost','sold','destroyed','consumed']
inventoryWarnings = []

13.8 Key rule

visible and available_loot are scene item states, not inventory ownership.

⸻

14. abilities schema

14.1 Purpose

14.2 Schema

abilities: {
  records: AbilityRecord[],
  statuses: string[],
  abilityWarnings: string[]
}

14.3 AbilityRecord

AbilityRecord: {
  abilityId: string,
  name: string,
  canonicalKey: string,
  status: 'unknown' | 'hint' | 'candidate' | 'unstable' | 'stable' | 'sealed' | 'unavailable',
  category: 'active' | 'passive' | 'talent' | 'skill' | 'specialization' | 'unknown',
  rank: string,
  evidence: AbilityEvidenceRecord[],
  costLabel: string,
  resourceCosts: ResourceCostRecord[],
  requirements: string[],
  cooldown: string,
  reliability: 'none' | 'low' | 'medium' | 'high' | 'stable' | 'unknown',
  sealedReason: string,
  createdTurn: number,
  lastUsedTurn: number,
  sourceEventKey: string,
  notes: string[]
}

  evidenceId: string,

  confidence: number,
  turn: number,
  sourceEventKey: string,
  notes: string[]
}

14.5 ResourceCostRecord

ResourceCostRecord: {
  resource: 'HP' | 'MP' | 'EP' | 'hybrid' | 'unknown',
  amountLabel: string,
  numericEstimate: number | null
}

14.6 Defaults

records = []
statuses = ['unknown','hint','candidate','unstable','stable','sealed','unavailable']
abilityWarnings = []

14.7 Key rule

Candidate ability is not stable.

Failed attempt may add evidence but must not create stable ability.

15.7 Key rule

22.4 Key rule

Old Story Card prose is not imported as canon.

Unsafe old data is quarantined, not guessed.

⸻

23. quarantine schema

23.1 Purpose

quarantine stores unsafe but potentially relevant data that must not affect gameplay until manually resolved in a future system.

23.2 Schema

quarantine: {
  records: QuarantineRecord[],
  unresolvedCount: number,
  quarantineWarnings: string[]
}

23.3 QuarantineRecord

QuarantineRecord: {
  quarantineId: string,
  source: 'state' | 'card' | 'setup' | 'manual' | 'system',
  sourceKey: string,
  originalPreview: string,
  reason: string,
  severity: 'info' | 'warning' | 'danger' | 'blocked',
  suggestedAction: 'ignore' | 'manual_review' | 'repair_later' | 'delete_later' | 'map_later',
  createdTurn: number,
  resolved: boolean,
  resolution: string
}

23.4 Defaults

records = []
unresolvedCount = 0
quarantineWarnings = []

23.5 Key rule

Quarantined records do not affect gameplay state.

A quarantined item is not inventory.

A quarantined quest is not active.

A quarantined ability is not usable.

⸻

24. dirty schema

24.1 Purpose

dirty stores queued mirror/context/audit update targets.

Dirty targets are not gameplay truth.

24.2 Schema

dirty: {
  targets: DirtyTarget[],
  maxDirtyTargets: number,
  dirtyWarnings: string[]
}

24.3 DirtyTarget

DirtyTarget: {
  dirtyId: string,
  family: 'player_sheet' | 'body_status' | 'abilities' | 'inventory_equipment' | 'quest_log' | 'social_memory' | 'current_location' | 'context' | 'card_registry' | 'migration' | 'audit',
  stateIds: string[],
  reason: string,
  priority: number,
  createdTurn: number,
  lastAttemptTurn: number,
  attempts: number,
  status: 'queued' | 'processed' | 'skipped' | 'blocked' | 'error',
  lastResult: string
}

24.4 Defaults

targets = []
maxDirtyTargets = 100
dirtyWarnings = []

24.5 Key rule

Dirty targets may trigger card/context/audit refreshes only.

They may not create gameplay facts.

⸻

25. cleanup schema

25.1 Purpose

cleanup stores cleanup status, schedules, warnings, and errors.

25.2 Schema

cleanup: {
  lastCleanupTurn: number,
  nextDeepCleanupTurn: number,
  lastCleanupType: 'none' | 'lightweight' | 'scene' | 'deep' | 'migration',
  recordsInspectedLastRun: number,
  recordsChangedLastRun: number,
  warnings: string[],
  errors: string[]
}

25.3 Defaults

lastCleanupTurn = 0
nextDeepCleanupTurn = 25
lastCleanupType = 'none'
recordsInspectedLastRun = 0
recordsChangedLastRun = 0
warnings = []
errors = []

25.4 Key rule

Cleanup may not delete important truth.

Cleanup may not resolve quarantine automatically.

Cleanup may not create gameplay state.

⸻

26. Deferred system placeholder policy

Do not add active root buckets for deferred systems unless needed as disabled flags.

Avoid active buckets like:

economy
crafting
investigation
domain
absorption
warfare

in MVP root state unless a later build chunk explicitly unlocks them.

MVP may mention deferred systems only in:

* features flags
* help text
* releasecheck disabled-system checks
* notes/warnings

This prevents the script from pretending deferred systems are active.

⸻

27. State repair policy

StateManager may repair:

* missing root state
* missing buckets
* missing default fields
* invalid enum values, using safe fallback and warning
* malformed arrays, replacing with empty arrays only if no valid data exists
* resource current/max bounds, with warning
* cap oversized audit arrays

StateManager must not:

* erase valid records
* import Story Card prose as truth
* create gameplay outcomes from old logs
* enable generated cards
* resolve quarantine automatically
* delete active inventory/quests/actors/abilities

⸻

28. State mutation source policy

28.1 Allowed gameplay mutation path

The normal gameplay mutation path is:

AI output -> ParserHit -> EventProposal -> CanonValidator -> ReducerRegistry -> OwnerReducer -> OperationLedger -> DirtyTargets -> Audit

28.2 Non-gameplay mutation path

Allowed structural mutations:

StateManager initialization
StateManager repair
migration/quarantine structural repair
audit writes
runtime hook metadata
command suppression metadata
cleanup of temporary/audit records

28.3 Forbidden mutation sources

Forbidden as gameplay sources:

* Story Card text
* Context text
* command output
* debug output
* help text
* old logs
* archived summaries
* generated cards
* protected cards

⸻

29. Required command views by bucket

Each command reads specific buckets.

Command	Reads
/help	meta, features
/version	meta, features, migration, releaseCheck
/sheet	player, progression
/status	body
/inventory	inventory
/abilities	abilities
/quests	quests
/actors	actors
/location	places
/time	time
/debuglast	audits, runtime
/cardaudit	cards, audits.cardAudit
/contextaudit	audits.contextAudit
/stateaudit	meta, migration, quarantine, cleanup, dirty, audits
/ledger	operationLedger
/dirty	dirty
/migration	migration
/quarantine	quarantine
/releasecheck	all buckets read-only
/startupaudit	startup

Commands may not mutate gameplay state.

⸻

30. Required dirty target mapping by bucket

When future reducers mutate a bucket, they should mark dirty families.

Mutated Bucket	Dirty Families
player	player_sheet, context
body	body_status, context
progression	player_sheet, context
inventory	inventory_equipment, current_location, context
abilities	abilities, context
quests	quest_log, context
actors	social_memory, current_location, context
places	current_location, context
time	current_location, quest_log, context
cards	card_registry, audit
migration/quarantine	migration, audit
cleanup	audit

Dirty targets do not create gameplay truth.

⸻

31. Required state caps

Recommended caps from Gap Chunk F:

recentProposals max = 50
recentValidationResults max = 50
debugMessages max = 50
runtimeWarnings max = 25
runtimeErrors max = 20
hookTrace max = 30
recentOperationKeys max = 250
contextPacketHashes max = 100, if added later
dirtyTargets max = 100
sceneItems max = 50
sceneActors max = 30
actorCandidates max = 50, if added later
placeCandidates max = 50, if added later
abilityEvidenceRecent max = 50
quarantineRecords max = 100 before summary compaction

If exact cap behavior is not coded yet, ReleaseCheck should warn until implemented.

⸻

32. State schema acceptance tests

These are planning/schema tests.

BC3-001 — Root buckets complete

Expected:

state.aidrpg contains all required buckets:

meta, features, runtime, startup, player, body, progression, inventory, abilities, quests, actors, places, time, cards, operationLedger, audits, migration, quarantine, dirty, cleanup

Fail if any required bucket is missing.

BC3-002 — Feature defaults safe

Expected:

* generatedCardsEnabled false
* cardSyncDryRun true
* cardSyncSafeMode true
* economy/crafting/investigation/domain/absorption disabled
* manualMutationCommandsEnabled false

Fail if dangerous features default active.

BC3-003 — Runtime duplicate fields exist

Expected:

* lastOutputHash exists
* lastAppliedOutputHash exists
* suppressEventScanForCommandOutput exists

Fail if duplicate output/command suppression cannot be tracked.

BC3-004 — Inventory separates scene items from owned items

Expected:

* inventory.items exists
* inventory.sceneItems exists
* accessStates include visible and available_loot
* visible/available_loot are not owned inventory states

Fail if visible items are stored as owned by default.

BC3-005 — Ability statuses include candidate/stable distinction

Expected:

* candidate
* unstable
* stable
* sealed
* unavailable

Fail if candidate and stable are collapsed.

BC3-006 — Quest reward statuses separate promised/paid

Expected:

* promised
* ready
* paid
* disputed
* denied

Fail if promised reward implies paid currency.

BC3-007 — Actor presence supports absent/elsewhere

Expected:

Presence states include:

present, nearby, elsewhere, unknown, absent, dead, captured

Fail if absent companion cannot be represented.

BC3-008 — Place schema separates travel intent from current location

Expected:

* currentLocation exists
* currentScene exists
* travelIntent exists separately

Fail if heading toward a location must overwrite current location.

BC3-009 — Card defaults suppress generated cards

Expected:

* generatedCardsEnabled false in features and card sync
* maxGeneratedCreatesPerTurn 0
* maxGeneratedUpdatesPerTurn 0

Fail if generated cards can activate by default.

BC3-010 — OperationLedger buckets exist

Expected:

* permanentKeys
* recentKeys
* rewardKeys
* cardKeys
* sourceUseKeys

Fail if reward/card duplicate prevention cannot be represented.

BC3-011 — Quarantine exists and is non-gameplay

Expected:

* quarantine.records exists
* quarantined records do not affect gameplay state

Fail if quarantine data is treated as active truth.

BC3-012 — Dirty targets are mirrors only

Expected:

* dirty.targets exists
* dirty target families exist
* dirty targets cannot create gameplay state

Fail if dirty queue can create item/quest/actor/card truth.

⸻

33. Relationship to Gap Chunks A–G

Build Chunk 3 incorporates Gap Chunks A–G in the schema:

* Gap A: old fix owners are represented as buckets/modules, not patch roots
* Gap B: runtime hook contract fields are included
* Gap C: card registry/sync/tombstone/duplicate structures are included
* Gap D: parser safety is supported through separate state ownership and future audit fields
* Gap E: migration/quarantine/releasecheck fields are included
* Gap F: dirty/cleanup/caps/audits are included
* Gap G: startup and protected identity fields are included

⸻

34. What Build Chunk 4 should do next

Next build document:

Build Chunk 4 — Command Output Format Lock

Build Chunk 4 should define exact output formats for:

/help
/version
/sheet
/status
/inventory
/abilities
/quests
/actors
/location
/time
/debuglast
/cardaudit
/contextaudit
/stateaudit
/ledger
/dirty
/migration
/quarantine
/releasecheck
/startupaudit

It should make commands readable, honest, and non-mutating.

⸻

35. Final Build Chunk 3 doctrine

The schema is now the contract.

Do not invent new field names casually during coding.

Do not add active deferred buckets early.

Do not let display text become state.

Do not let temporary records masquerade as permanent truth.

Final rule:

One root state.
Named buckets.
Known owners.
Safe defaults.
No hidden roots.
No guessed canon.
No state mutation without authority.

With this schema locked, command output formats can be finalized next.

====================================================================================================
20_Build_Chunks_01_to_17/04_AIDRPG_Build_Chunk_04_Command_Output_Format_Lock.txt
====================================================================================================

AIDRPG Build Chunk 4 — Command Output Format Lock

0. Document status

Document name: AIDRPG Build Chunk 4 — Command Output Format Lock
Build target: AIDRPG Core MVP v0.1
Purpose: Lock the exact command output formats for MVP commands before command rendering code is generated.
Status: Practical build document.
Follows: Build Chunk 3 — State Schema Lock.
This document does not contain final script code.

This chunk exists because commands are one of the main ways the player will inspect whether the system is working.

Commands must be:

* readable
* honest
* consistent
* non-mutating
* safe from parser feedback
* clear about disabled systems
* useful for testing
* useful for debugging

Command output must not become gameplay truth.

⸻

1. Command output doctrine

The doctrine is:

Commands inspect.
Commands explain.
Commands do not play the game.
Commands do not mutate gameplay state.
Command output is non-canon.

A command may display:

* inventory items
* quest rewards
* ability names
* actor names
* card warnings
* migration warnings
* debug reasons

But that displayed text must not create or alter state.

Example:

/inventory displays Rusty Dagger.

This must not add another Rusty Dagger.

Example:

/quests displays Reward: 15 gold promised.

This must not pay 15 gold.

Example:

/abilities displays Shadow Step — candidate.

This must not make Shadow Step stable.

⸻

2. Required command suppression rule

Every command output must set or preserve:

runtime.commandActive = true
runtime.suppressEventScanForCommandOutput = true
runtime.lastCommandName = commandName
runtime.lastCommandHash = hash(commandOutput)
runtime.lastCommandTurn = runtime.currentTurn

The Output hook must recognize command output and suppress event scanning.

If command output is later passed through Output hook, it must be ignored as story.

⸻

3. Global command format rules

All command outputs should follow this general style:

[AIDRPG] Command Name
Status: <short status>
<section heading>
- <line>
- <line>
Notes:
- <line>

3.1 Required prefix

Every command output should begin with:

[AIDRPG]

This helps identify command output for suppression.

3.2 Required non-canon reminder for diagnostics

Diagnostic commands should include a compact note:

Command output is non-canon and does not change story state.

Normal player-facing commands do not need to repeat this every time unless helpful.

3.3 No raw giant dumps

Commands must not dump full raw state by default.

Allowed:

* concise summary
* counts
* important records
* warnings
* recent audit entries

Forbidden by default:

* full state.aidrpg JSON
* huge arrays
* every audit line
* every operation key
* every old log
* every card body

3.4 Empty-state output

Every command must handle empty state gracefully.

Example:

No active quests.

not:

undefined
null
[object Object]

3.5 Unknown/malformed state output

If a bucket is missing or malformed, commands should say:

Warning: <bucket> data was missing or repaired. Run /stateaudit for details.

Commands should not crash.

⸻

4. Output length rules

Command output must be compact enough for AI Dungeon use.

Recommended maximums:

/help: 2000 characters
/version: 1200 characters
/sheet: 1500 characters
/status: 1200 characters
/inventory: 1800 characters
/abilities: 1800 characters
/quests: 2200 characters
/actors: 1800 characters
/location: 1500 characters
/time: 900 characters
/debuglast: 2500 characters
/cardaudit: 2500 characters
/contextaudit: 2200 characters
/stateaudit: 2500 characters
/ledger: 1800 characters
/dirty: 1800 characters
/migration: 1800 characters
/quarantine: 2200 characters
/releasecheck: 3000 characters
/startupaudit: 1800 characters

If output exceeds the limit:

* show highest priority lines
* summarize the rest
* say how many entries were omitted

Example:

Showing 10 of 34 inventory entries. Use a future filtered command when available.

MVP does not need advanced pagination unless later specified.

⸻

5. Required MVP commands

Core MVP v0.1 must support these commands:

/help
/version
/sheet
/status
/inventory
/abilities
/quests
/actors
/location
/time
/debuglast
/cardaudit
/contextaudit
/stateaudit
/ledger
/dirty
/migration
/quarantine
/releasecheck
/startupaudit

Each command is inspect-only or diagnostic-only.

None of these commands may mutate gameplay state.

⸻

6. Disabled/deferred commands

Any disabled command must return a safe disabled message.

Required disabled command format:

[AIDRPG] <Command Name>
Status: Disabled in Core MVP v0.1
This system is not active yet.
Required before activation: <required mini-spec or build phase>.
No gameplay state was changed.

6.1 Required disabled systems

Commands for these systems must be disabled in MVP:

economy
shop
buy
sell
craft
recipes
materials
cases
clues
evidence
reputation
factions
domain
workers
projects
absorb
evolve
devour
party advanced controls
summons
warfare
scale
battlefield
protagonists
players
cardsync live on
generatecards
manual mutation commands

⸻

7. Alias command output rules

Old aliases should route safely.

If an alias maps to an active command, output may show the active command name and alias note.

Example:

[AIDRPG] Quest Log
Alias: /questlog -> /quests
Status: Active

If an alias maps to a disabled system, output the disabled command format.

⸻

8. /help format

8.1 Purpose

Show what the current build can do and what is disabled.

8.2 Required format

[AIDRPG] Help
Status: Core MVP v0.1 Foundation
Active Player Commands:
- /sheet — show player identity, level, stats, and build summary
- /status — show HP, MP, EP, injuries, conditions, fatigue
- /inventory — show equipped, owned, nearby, and unavailable items
- /abilities — show stable, unstable, candidate, passive, and sealed abilities
- /quests — show quest states and reward status
- /actors — show important actors, companions, presence, and social notes
- /location — show current location, scene, hazards, exits, and visible key items
- /time — show current time text, last time advance, and deadlines
Diagnostic Commands:
- /version — show build and feature flags
- /debuglast — show last runtime/proposal/validation summary
- /cardaudit — show Story Card sync status
- /contextaudit — show context packet status
- /stateaudit — show schema and integrity status
- /ledger — show duplicate-prevention ledger summary
- /dirty — show pending mirror/context update targets
- /migration — show migration status
- /quarantine — show unsafe legacy data held for review
- /releasecheck — run readiness checks
- /startupaudit — show startup import and setup-lock status
Disabled in MVP:
- generated cards
- economy, shop, buying, selling
- crafting and materials projects
- investigation cases/evidence
- reputation and faction automation
- domain/labor systems
- absorption/evolution mechanics
- advanced combat, warfare, and multi-protagonist systems
- manual mutation commands
Safety Notes:
- Commands inspect only.
- Command output is non-canon.
- Story Cards mirror state only.
- Context is read-only.
- Candidate abilities are not stable abilities.
- Promised rewards are not paid rewards.
- Visible items are not inventory until acquired.

8.3 Required honesty

/help must not claim disabled systems are active.

If a system is shell-only, say shell-only.

⸻

9. /version format

9.1 Purpose

Show the build identity and feature flags.

9.2 Required format

[AIDRPG] Version
Status: Foundation Build
Build:
- Project: AIDRPG
- Build Name: AIDRPG Core MVP v0.1
- Build Stage: core_mvp_v0_1_foundation
- Schema Version: 1
- Authority: v1.3 roadmap gap aligned
Feature Flags:
- Generated Cards: disabled
- Protected Card Writes: disabled or dry-run only
- CardSync Mode: dry-run / safe mode
- Economy: disabled
- Crafting: disabled
- Investigation: disabled
- Reputation/Factions: disabled
- Domain/Labor: disabled
- Absorption/Evolution: disabled
- Multi-Protagonist: disabled
- Advanced Combat: disabled
- Manual Mutation Commands: disabled
Migration:
- Status: <migrationStatus>
- Quarantine: <unresolvedCount> unresolved
Last Release Check:
- Result: <unknown/PASS/WARN/FAIL/BLOCKED>

9.3 Empty/missing values

If values are missing:

Warning: version data incomplete. Run /stateaudit.

⸻

Temporary Identity Effects:

Notes:

10.3 Safety requirements

/sheet must separate:

* name from title
* appearance from disguise
* permanent species from dream/illusion state

It must not update the player.

- none

- <bodyWarnings or none>

11.3 Safety requirements

/status must not heal, damage, resolve, or worsen conditions.

It only displays state.

⸻

12. /inventory format

12.1 Purpose

Show owned items, equipped items, nearby available/visible scene items, unavailable/custody items, and currency.

12.2 Required format

[AIDRPG] Inventory
Status: Active
Currency:
- Copper: <copper>
- Silver: <silver>
- Gold: <gold>
- Abstract Value: <abstractValue if used>
Equipped:
- <slot>: <item name> (<condition>)
- none
Inventory:
- <item name> x<quantity> — <accessState>, <condition>
- none
Available Nearby:
- <scene item name> x<quantity> — <visible/available_loot>, at <location/scene>
- none
Unavailable / In Custody:
- <item name> — custody: <actor/place>, access: <accessState>
- none
Lost / Sold / Destroyed / Consumed:
- <item name> — <accessState>
- none
Warnings:
- Visible items are not owned inventory until acquired.
- Available loot is not usable until picked up.

12.3 Item display priority

If output is long, show in this priority:

1. equipped
2. quest-linked inventory
3. important owned items
4. unavailable/custody warnings
5. nearby available loot
6. other items summarized

12.4 Safety requirements

/inventory must not:

* add displayed items
* move available loot into inventory
* equip items
* resolve custody
* pay or spend currency

⸻

13. /abilities format

13.1 Purpose

Show abilities by status and prevent candidate/stable confusion.

13.2 Required format

[AIDRPG] Abilities
Status: Active
Stable Abilities:
- <name> — <category>, cost: <costLabel>, reliability: stable
- none
Unstable Abilities:
- <name> — <category>, reliability: <low/medium/high>, notes: <short note>
- none
Candidate / Hinted Abilities:
- <name> — status: <candidate/hint>, evidence: <count>, reliability: not reliable
- none
Passives / Talents:
- <name> — <category>, status: <stable/candidate/etc.>
- none
Sealed / Unavailable:
- <name> — reason: <sealedReason>
- none
Safety Notes:
- Candidate abilities are not stable.
- Failed attempts may add evidence, but they do not create mastered abilities.

13.3 Safety requirements

/abilities must not:

* stabilize candidates
* create abilities from display text
* spend resources
* clear cooldowns
* unseal abilities

⸻

14. /quests format

14.1 Purpose

Show quest states and reward states clearly.

14.2 Required format

[AIDRPG] Quests
Status: Active
Rumored / Offered:
- <title> — <status>, giver: <actor or unknown>
- none
Active:
- <title>
  Status: active
  Objective: <objective text> (<progressCurrent>/<progressRequired>, <objective status>)
  Giver: <giver or unknown>
  Deadline: <deadline or none>
  Reward: <description> — <rewardStatus>
- none
Blocked:
- <title> — blocked by <reason or unknown>
- none
Ready to Turn In:
- <title> — reward: <description>, status: <ready/promised>
- none
Completed / Rewarded:
- <title> — quest status: <completed/rewarded>, reward: <paid/disputed/denied/etc.>
- none
Failed / Abandoned / Archived:
- <title> — <status>
- none
Safety Notes:
- Rumored quests are not active quests.
- Offered quests are not accepted quests.
- Promised rewards are not paid rewards.
- Completing an objective does not automatically pay the reward.

14.3 Safety requirements

/quests must not:

* activate offered quests
* complete objectives
* pay rewards
* add currency
* archive quests
