After Build Chunk 6, the project will be close to generating the first foundation code pass.

⸻

28. Final Build Chunk 5 doctrine

StateManager is not exciting, but it is one of the most important parts of the whole project.

If StateManager is correct, later systems have a safe place to live.

If StateManager is wrong, every later system inherits corruption.

Final rule:

StateManager creates structure.
StateManager repairs structure.
StateManager preserves truth.
StateManager quarantines unsafe data.
StateManager does not invent gameplay.
StateManager does not erase valid records.

With StateManager planned, the next step is to plan the runtime hooks that will call it every turn.

====================================================================================================
20_Build_Chunks_01_to_17/06_AIDRPG_Build_Chunk_06_Runtime_Hook_Contract_Implementation_Plan.txt
====================================================================================================

AIDRPG Build Chunk 6 — Runtime Hook Contract Implementation Plan

0. Document status

Document name: AIDRPG Build Chunk 6 — Runtime Hook Contract Implementation Plan
Build target: AIDRPG Core MVP v0.1
Purpose: Define the exact runtime hook behavior, hook flow, valid return handling, command suppression, duplicate output protection, and fail-soft rules before runtime code is generated.
Status: Practical build document.
Follows: Build Chunk 5 — StateManager Plan.
This document does not contain final script code.

This chunk is useful because the runtime hooks are where AI Dungeon actually touches the script.

If the hooks are unsafe, every later system becomes unsafe.

If Input commits success, the player gets items or quest progress too early.

If Context mutates state, reminders become hidden gameplay writers.

If Output replays are not blocked, rewards, XP, items, time, cards, and damage duplicate.

If command output is scanned as story, commands become corrupting narration.

If fail-soft behavior is missing, a single error can crash the whole script or partially mutate state.

Build Chunk 6 turns the runtime plan into exact implementation rules.

⸻

1. Runtime doctrine

The runtime doctrine is:

Input may intend.
Context may remind.
Output may propose.
Commands may inspect.
Runtime may protect.
Runtime may not decide gameplay truth.

Runtime owns the safety of the hook surfaces.

Runtime does not own gameplay consequences.

⸻

2. Runtime responsibilities

Runtime owns:

* hook entry safety
* hook metadata
* current hook name
* current turn number
* hook depth / recursion guard
* input hash tracking
* context hash tracking
* output hash tracking
* last applied output hash
* command suppression flags
* command hash tracking
* duplicate output replay detection
* valid return shapes
* fail-soft behavior
* runtime warning/error records
* hook trace records

Runtime does not own:

* item acquisition
* reward payment
* XP gain
* ability creation
* quest activation/completion
* actor promotion
* location arrival
* Story Card canon
* Context packet truth
* parser validation decisions

⸻

3. Runtime placement in final script

Runtime belongs in:

Section 06 — Runtime Hook Contract

Runtime depends on:

Constants
Utility Helpers
StateManager
AuditLogger
OperationLedger read/write only for duplicate support later, if needed

Runtime must not depend on:

EventScannerLite directly for decisions
ReducerRegistry directly for gameplay commits
CardSync live writes
ContextBuilder mutation
Cleanup destructive behavior

Hook entry points orchestrate modules later, but Runtime itself stays focused on hook safety.

⸻

4. Required runtime public functions

The final script should provide these Runtime functions or equivalent section-scoped functions.

Runtime.validateHookInput(hookName, text, rawState)
Runtime.beginHook(root, hookName, text)
Runtime.endHook(root, hookName, resultText, status)
Runtime.failSoft(root, hookName, phase, error, originalText, isCommand)
Runtime.makeReturn(text)
Runtime.safeText(value)
Runtime.hashText(text)
Runtime.markInput(root, text)
Runtime.markContext(root, text)
Runtime.markOutput(root, text)
Runtime.isDuplicateOutput(root, outputHash)
Runtime.markOutputApplied(root, outputHash)
Runtime.detectCommandOutputReplay(root, text)
Runtime.setCommandSuppression(root, commandName, commandOutput)
Runtime.clearCommandSuppression(root)
Runtime.shouldSuppressEventScan(root, text)
Runtime.recordWarning(root, message)
Runtime.recordError(root, hookName, phase, message, safeTextReturned, partialCommitBlocked)
Runtime.pushHookTrace(root, record)
Runtime.capRuntime(root)
Runtime.guardReentry(root, hookName)

The exact names can change during coding, but these responsibilities must exist.

⸻

5. Valid hook surfaces

The implementation must support three conceptual hook surfaces.

Input hook
Context hook
Output hook

Actual platform names may be different.

Possible AI Dungeon-style wrappers may be:

inputModifier(text)
contextModifier(text)
outputModifier(text)

or equivalent.

The internal contract remains the same even if the platform wrapper names differ.

⸻

6. Universal hook wrapper flow

Every hook should follow the same safety shell.

1. Capture original text.
2. Convert text to safe string.
3. Call StateManager.init.
4. Call Runtime.beginHook.
5. Run hook-specific logic inside try/catch.
6. Prevent forbidden mutation based on hook role.
7. Call Runtime.endHook.
8. Return valid text shape.
9. If error occurs, call Runtime.failSoft and return valid text shape.

No hook may return undefined.

No hook may leave runtime.hookDepth stuck above zero after success or fail-soft.

⸻

7. Valid return shape

7.1 Preferred internal return

All hook logic should internally normalize to:

{ text: string }

7.2 Platform adaptation

If AI Dungeon requires a plain string or another shape, the platform-facing entry point may adapt at the final boundary.

Internal logic must still use the safe return contract.

7.3 Forbidden returns

Runtime must avoid:

undefined
null
false
true
number
object without text
uncaught thrown error

7.4 Return text rule

If the script does not need to alter text, return the original safe text.

For story hooks, fail-soft should usually return original text.

For command failures, fail-soft should return a safe command error message.

⸻

8. Runtime state fields used

Runtime implementation uses the fields locked in Build Chunk 3.

runtime.currentHook
runtime.currentTurn
runtime.hookDepth
runtime.hookTrace
runtime.lastInputHash
runtime.lastContextHash
runtime.lastOutputHash
runtime.lastAppliedOutputHash
runtime.lastCommandHash
runtime.commandActive
runtime.suppressEventScanForCommandOutput
runtime.lastCommandTurn
runtime.lastCommandName
runtime.pendingInputIntentId
runtime.outputReplayDetected
runtime.runtimeWarnings
runtime.runtimeErrors
runtime.failSoftCount

StateManager must ensure these fields exist before Runtime uses them.

⸻

9. Hook trace behavior

Every hook should create a trace record.

9.1 Start trace

At hook start:

turn
hook
textHash
startedAt
status = 'started'
notes

9.2 End trace

At hook end:

endedAt
status = 'completed' | 'failed' | 'fail_soft'
notes

9.3 Trace cap

Keep most recent 30 trace records.

Do not let hookTrace grow forever.

9.4 Trace safety

Trace is diagnostic only.

Trace does not create gameplay truth.

⸻

10. Hook depth and recursion guard

10.1 Hook depth rule

At beginHook:

runtime.hookDepth += 1

At endHook or failSoft:

runtime.hookDepth = max(0, runtime.hookDepth - 1)

10.2 Reentry rule

If hookDepth is already greater than zero before entering a new hook:

* record runtime warning
* allow safe diagnostic continuation if necessary
* block gameplay mutation
* return safe text if recursion is unsafe

10.3 Why this matters

Nested hook behavior can happen through platform quirks, commands, cards, context generation, or failed tool-like behavior.

The script must not recursively parse its own output.

⸻

11. Turn counting policy

11.1 Gameplay turn increment

A new gameplay turn begins on non-command Input hook.

Recommended:

Input hook receives non-command text -> runtime.currentTurn += 1

11.2 Commands do not advance gameplay turn

Commands should not advance gameplay time or turn consequences.

They may record command metadata.

11.3 Context and Output turn use

Context and Output use the current turn created by Input.

If Output arrives without prior Input, Runtime should still fail softly and use existing currentTurn.

Do not invent gameplay consequences due to unusual hook order.

⸻

12. Input hook implementation plan

12.1 Input hook responsibilities

Input hook owns:

* state initialization
* runtime begin/end
* command detection
* command routing
* command suppression
* non-command input hash
* pending intent shell only, if included

Input hook must not own:

* completed item pickup
* combat victory
* quest completion
* reward payment
* XP gain
* stable ability creation
* confirmed travel arrival
* Story Card updates

12.2 Input hook flow

1. originalText = safeText(text)
2. root = StateManager.init(state)
3. Runtime.beginHook(root, 'input', originalText)
4. If CommandRouter.isCommand(originalText):
   a. commandOutput = CommandRouter.route(root, originalText)
   b. Runtime.setCommandSuppression(root, commandName, commandOutput)
   c. Runtime.endHook(root, 'input', commandOutput, 'completed')
   d. return Runtime.makeReturn(commandOutput)
5. Else:
   a. Runtime.markInput(root, originalText)
   b. increment currentTurn if appropriate
   c. optionally create pending input intent shell only
   d. StateManager.markGameplayStarted(root) if first gameplay input
   e. Runtime.endHook(root, 'input', originalText, 'completed')
   f. return Runtime.makeReturn(originalText)
6. Catch errors through failSoft.

12.3 Input intent shell

Input may create a pending intent shell later, but in early v0.1 this can be inert.

If it exists, it must not become success.

Example:

Player input:

I take the crown.

Input may record:

pending intent: attempt_take_crown

Input must not add Crown to inventory.

12.4 Input command suppression

When a command is handled, Runtime must set:

commandActive = true
suppressEventScanForCommandOutput = true
lastCommandName = commandName
lastCommandHash = hash(commandOutput)
lastCommandTurn = currentTurn

This prevents command output from becoming story.

⸻

13. Context hook implementation plan

13.1 Context hook responsibilities

Context hook owns:

* state initialization
* runtime begin/end
* context hash
* read-only ContextBuilderLite call
* context audit
* valid context return

Context hook must not own:

* gameplay mutation
* proposal creation
* reducer commits
* Story Card updates
* cleanup mutation
* command routing

13.2 Context hook flow

1. originalText = safeText(text)
2. root = StateManager.init(state)
3. Runtime.beginHook(root, 'context', originalText)
4. Runtime.markContext(root, originalText)
5. packets = ContextBuilderLite.buildPackets(root) if active, read-only
6. contextText = ContextBuilderLite.serialize(packets, root) or originalText/minimal safe context
7. ContextBuilderLite.audit(root, packets, skipped)
8. Runtime.endHook(root, 'context', contextText, 'completed')
9. return Runtime.makeReturn(contextText)
10. Catch errors through failSoft.

13.3 Context no-write guard

Runtime cannot perfectly prevent every code path from mutating state, but the design must enforce:

* ContextBuilderLite does not call reducers
* ContextBuilderLite does not call CardSync writes
* ContextBuilderLite does not import card text
* ContextBuilderLite only writes context audit metadata if needed

ReleaseCheck must fail if ContextBuilderLite is capable of gameplay mutation.

13.4 Early v0.1 context behavior

If full context packets are not active yet, Context hook may return original text or minimal safe context.

It must honestly report through /contextaudit:

Context packet system shell present; gameplay mutation disabled.

⸻

14. Output hook implementation plan

14.1 Output hook responsibilities

Output hook owns:

* state initialization
* runtime begin/end
* output hash
* command output suppression check
* duplicate output replay check
* parser/proposal/validator/reducer orchestration later
* dirty targets later
* card sync dry-run/shell later
* cleanup safe shell later

Output hook must not directly own:

* parser direct mutation
* command output consequences
* Context text consequences
* Story Card text consequences
* generated card creation in MVP

14.2 Output hook flow

1. originalText = safeText(text)
2. root = StateManager.init(state)
3. Runtime.beginHook(root, 'output', originalText)
4. outputHash = Runtime.markOutput(root, originalText)
5. If Runtime.shouldSuppressEventScan(root, originalText):
   a. audit suppression
   b. Runtime.clearCommandSuppression(root) if safe
   c. Runtime.endHook(root, 'output', originalText, 'completed')
   d. return Runtime.makeReturn(originalText)
6. If Runtime.isDuplicateOutput(root, outputHash):
   a. runtime.outputReplayDetected = true
   b. audit duplicate output skip
   c. Runtime.endHook(root, 'output', originalText, 'completed')
   d. return Runtime.makeReturn(originalText)
7. If parser pipeline active:
   a. hits = EventScannerLite.scanOutput(originalText, root)
   b. proposals = ProposalBuilder.fromHits(hits, root)
   c. validationResults = CanonValidator.validateAll(proposals, root)
   d. ReducerRegistry.applyAccepted(validationResults, root)
8. If parser pipeline inactive:
   a. audit shell state if needed
9. Runtime.markOutputApplied(root, outputHash) only after safe processing decision.
10. CardSyncLite dry-run/safe shell if enabled.
11. CleanupManagerLite lightweight safe caps if enabled.
12. Runtime.endHook(root, 'output', originalText, 'completed')
13. return Runtime.makeReturn(originalText)
14. Catch errors through failSoft.

14.3 Early v0.1 output behavior

Early v0.1 may not have full parsing active.

In that case Output hook should:

* track output hash
* suppress command output
* block duplicate output reprocessing
* not create gameplay consequences
* honestly report parser shell status in /debuglast and /releasecheck

This is useful because it proves runtime safety before automation.

⸻

15. Command output suppression implementation

15.1 Suppression markers

Command output should be identifiable by:

* runtime.suppressEventScanForCommandOutput = true
* runtime.lastCommandHash
* command prefix [AIDRPG]
* last command name
* last command turn

15.2 Suppression check

Output hook should suppress scanning if:

runtime.suppressEventScanForCommandOutput = true

and any of the following are true:

hash(outputText) === runtime.lastCommandHash
outputText begins with [AIDRPG]
runtime.commandActive is true and output appears immediately after command

15.3 Suppression result

If suppressed:

* do not scan as story
* do not create proposals
* do not run reducers
* do not update gameplay state
* record suppression audit
* clear command suppression if safe
* return original output

15.4 Suppression must protect command content

Examples:

/inventory output showing:

Rusty Dagger

must not add another dagger.

/quests output showing:

Reward: 15 gold promised

must not pay gold.

/abilities output showing:

Shadow Step — candidate

must not stabilize Shadow Step.

⸻

16. Duplicate output replay implementation

16.1 Hash-level replay

Output hook must compare:

outputHash
runtime.lastAppliedOutputHash

If equal:

* skip consequence processing
* audit replay skip
* return safe text

16.2 lastOutputHash versus lastAppliedOutputHash

lastOutputHash records the most recent output observed.

lastAppliedOutputHash records the most recent output that was allowed through consequence processing.

These are different for safety.

16.3 When to mark output applied

Mark output as applied only after:

* command suppression check passes or skips appropriately
* duplicate check passes
* parser/proposal/validator/reducer stage completes safely or intentionally shell-skips

Do not mark output applied before a crash-prone step unless fail-soft policy accounts for it.

16.4 OperationLedger remains second layer

Hash replay only catches exact repeats.

OperationLedger later catches semantic duplicates.

Example:

Old Bren pays you 15 gold.
Old Bren hands you the promised fifteen gold.

These may have different hashes, but same reward key.

⸻

17. Fail-soft implementation

17.1 Fail-soft purpose

Fail-soft prevents one bug from crashing the script or corrupting state.

17.2 Fail-soft flow

1. Catch error.
2. Convert error to safe message.
3. Record runtime error.
4. Increment failSoftCount.
5. Mark hook trace fail_soft.
6. Reset hookDepth safely.
7. Preserve original text for story hooks.
8. Return command failure text for command hooks.
9. Do not continue optional mutation after error.

17.3 Story hook fail-soft return

For Input/Context/Output story text:

return original text

or safe context fallback for Context if required.

17.4 Command fail-soft return

For commands:

[AIDRPG] Command Error
Status: Failed Safely
The command failed safely. Gameplay state was not intentionally changed.
Run /debuglast or /stateaudit for details.

17.5 Fail-soft must not

* erase state
* reset player
* clear inventory
* enable generated cards
* retry Story Card writes endlessly
* continue reducer commits after critical error
* return undefined

⸻

18. Partial commit protection boundary

Runtime itself should not perform gameplay commits.

But it must define when processing stops after an error.

18.1 Early v0.1

Since reducers may be inactive or shell-only, partial gameplay commits should not happen yet.

18.2 Later reducer era

Once reducers commit proposals, the safe unit is:

validated proposal -> owner reducer apply -> operation key -> dirty target -> audit

If an error happens mid-processing:

* Runtime fail-soft stops further optional processing
* AuditLogger records partial commit risk
* ReleaseCheck may warn/fail if partial commit protection is incomplete

18.3 Runtime must not pretend rollback exists

If rollback is not implemented, do not claim rollback.

Say:

Partial commit protection is limited to proposal-level commit ordering and fail-soft interruption.

⸻

19. Runtime warnings and errors

19.1 Runtime warning examples

Runtime warning: Context hook received non-string text and converted it safely.
Runtime warning: Duplicate output replay detected; consequence scanning skipped.
Runtime warning: Command output suppression triggered.
Runtime warning: Hook reentry detected; gameplay mutation blocked.

19.2 Runtime error examples

Runtime error: output hook failed during EventScannerLite.scanOutput.
Runtime error: command route failed while rendering /inventory.
Runtime error: StateManager.init failed; returned safe text.

19.3 Caps

Use Build Chunk 3 caps:

runtimeWarnings max 25
runtimeErrors max 20
hookTrace max 30

⸻

20. Runtime and AuditLogger relationship

Runtime may write to:

runtime.runtimeWarnings
runtime.runtimeErrors
runtime.hookTrace
audits.runtimeWarnings
audits.debugMessages

Runtime should not duplicate every message into every bucket.

Recommended:

* runtime-specific warnings/errors in runtime
* user-visible summaries in audits
* /debuglast reads both

⸻

21. Runtime and CommandRouter relationship

Input hook uses CommandRouter for slash commands.

CommandRouter returns command output text.

Runtime then marks suppression.

Recommended order:

CommandRouter.route -> commandOutput
Runtime.setCommandSuppression(commandName, commandOutput)
return commandOutput

Do not scan command output as player intent.

Do not route commands from Output hook.

⸻

22. Runtime and StateManager relationship

Every hook starts with:

root = StateManager.init(state)

If StateManager repairs state, Runtime proceeds.

If StateManager fails, Runtime fail-soft returns safe text.

Runtime assumes StateManager has ensured required runtime fields exist.

⸻

23. Runtime and ReleaseCheck relationship

ReleaseCheck must verify:

* runtime bucket exists
* hook depth field exists
* duplicate hash fields exist
* command suppression fields exist
* fail-soft count exists
* hook trace exists and capped
* runtime error arrays capped
* command output suppression rule active
* duplicate output protection active
* Context read-only guard declared

ReleaseCheck must fail if:

* command output can be scanned as story
* duplicate output can apply consequences twice
* hooks can return undefined
* generated cards can activate during runtime in MVP

⸻

24. Runtime and parser shells

Runtime may call EventScannerLite only from Output hook and only after:

* command suppression check
* duplicate output hash check
* feature/parser flag check

Runtime must not call EventScannerLite for:

* command output
* context packets
* Story Card text
* debug/audit/help text

In early v0.1, EventScannerLite may be inactive.

Runtime must be honest about that.

⸻

25. Runtime and ContextBuilderLite

Context hook may call ContextBuilderLite.

Runtime must enforce the design expectation:

ContextBuilderLite is read-only.

Early implementation may not fully enforce with snapshots, but ReleaseCheck and code boundaries must identify it.

If a future test detects Context mutated gameplay state, releasecheck fails.

⸻

26. Runtime and CardSyncLite

Runtime may orchestrate CardSyncLite from Output hook only, and only in safe/dry-run mode for early v0.1.

Runtime must not run CardSyncLite from Context hook.

Runtime must not let CardSyncLite parse cards into gameplay state.

Generated cards remain disabled.

⸻

27. Runtime and CleanupManagerLite

Runtime may call CleanupManagerLite after safe Output processing.

Early v0.1 cleanup should be limited to:

* cap audits
* cap runtime arrays
* cap dirty queue

Runtime must not call destructive cleanup that can remove important truth.

If cleanup fails, fail-soft should not crash story output.

⸻

28. Hook-specific mutation permissions

28.1 Input hook may mutate

* runtime metadata
* command suppression metadata
* startup gameplayStarted/lock flags
* audit warnings
* pending input intent shell if implemented

Input hook may not mutate gameplay outcomes.

28.2 Context hook may mutate

* runtime metadata
* context audit metadata
* context hash

Context hook may not mutate gameplay outcomes.

28.3 Output hook may mutate

Early v0.1:

* runtime metadata
* output hash
* duplicate replay audit
* command suppression clearing
* audit shell entries
* card/cleanup dry-run metadata if safe

Later:

* validated reducer commits through owner reducers only

Output hook may not directly mutate gameplay outside validated reducer path.

⸻

29. Runtime test plan

BC6-001 — Input hook returns valid shape

Input:

I look around.

Expected:

* StateManager initializes state
* Runtime begins/ends input hook
* valid return shape
* non-command input does not create item/XP/quest

Fail if:

* undefined return
* gameplay consequence committed from input alone

⸻

BC6-002 — Command handled in Input hook

Input:

/help

Expected:

* CommandRouter routes command
* command output begins with [AIDRPG]
* suppression flag set
* gameplay turn does not advance as story consequence
* valid return shape

Fail if:

* command output not suppressed
* command mutates gameplay

⸻

BC6-003 — Command output not scanned in Output hook

Given command output from /inventory showing Rusty Dagger.

Expected:

* Output hook suppresses event scan
* no duplicate Rusty Dagger
* suppression audit recorded

Fail if:

* command output creates item

⸻

BC6-004 — Context hook read-only

Run Context hook.

Expected:

* context hash/audit may update
* no inventory/body/quest/actor/place changes
* valid return shape

Fail if:

* Context mutates gameplay state

⸻

BC6-005 — Output duplicate hash skip

Output:

You pick up the rusty dagger.

Run Output hook twice with same text.

Expected:

* second pass detects duplicate output
* consequence processing skipped
* one or zero dagger depending parser activation, never duplicate

Fail if:

* duplicate consequence applies

⸻

BC6-006 — Output shell does not falsely automate

Early v0.1 with parser disabled.

Output:

You gain 20 XP.

Expected:

* no XP gain unless parser/reducer build explicitly active
* debug/releasecheck says parser shell inactive

Fail if:

* shell unexpectedly awards XP

⸻

BC6-007 — Fail-soft on command renderer error

Simulate command renderer error for /inventory.

Expected:

* safe command error output
* runtime error logged
* no inventory erased
* valid return shape

Fail if:

* crash or state reset

⸻

BC6-008 — Fail-soft on output parser error

Simulate EventScannerLite error.

Expected:

* original output returned
* runtime error logged
* no partial gameplay mutation after error

Fail if:

* undefined return or partial unsafe mutation

⸻

BC6-009 — Hook depth resets after error

Simulate hook error.

Expected:

* hookDepth returns to 0
* hook trace records fail_soft

Fail if:

* hookDepth remains stuck > 0

⸻

BC6-010 — Runtime warning caps

Add more than 25 runtime warnings.

Expected:

* warnings capped
* recent warnings preserved

Fail if:

* unbounded growth

⸻

BC6-011 — Runtime error caps

Add more than 20 runtime errors.

Expected:

* errors capped
* recent critical errors preserved

Fail if:

* unbounded growth

⸻

BC6-012 — Command unknown safely returns

Input:

/banana

Expected:

* unknown command output
* suppression flag set
* no gameplay mutation

Fail if:

* crash or parser treats /banana as story

⸻

BC6-013 — Context text not scanned by output parser

If context output includes:

The Iron Sword is in Mara's custody.

Expected:

* not scanned as item transfer or inventory event
* no custody mutation from context text itself

Fail if:

* context reminder changes state

⸻

BC6-014 — Story Card text not routed through Runtime as canon

If card audit/card text includes:

Player owns Crown of Dawn.

Expected:

* Runtime does not scan it as output story
* no Crown added

Fail if:

* card text becomes canon

⸻

BC6-015 — ReleaseCheck catches missing runtime fields

Remove runtime.lastAppliedOutputHash in test state.

Expected:

* StateManager repairs it or ReleaseCheck warns/fails
* no crash

Fail if:

* missing field ignored and duplicate output protection impossible

⸻

30. Runtime acceptance checklist

Runtime Hook Contract Plan is complete when implementation will clearly support:

* Input hook flow
* Context hook flow
* Output hook flow
* valid return shapes
* StateManager-first hook order
* command detection and routing
* command output suppression
* output hash tracking
* duplicate output replay skip
* hook trace
* hook depth guard
* fail-soft behavior
* runtime warnings/errors with caps
* context read-only boundary
* parser shell honesty
* generated card suppression
* ReleaseCheck runtime checks
* tests BC6-001 through BC6-015

⸻

31. Relationship to previous build chunks

Build Chunk 6 depends on:

* Build Chunk 1 for allowed/forbidden v0.1 scope
* Build Chunk 2 for runtime section placement and call graph
* Build Chunk 3 for runtime state fields
* Build Chunk 4 for command output and suppression format
* Build Chunk 5 for StateManager-first behavior
* Gap Chunk B for full runtime doctrine
* Gap Chunk E for command honesty
* Gap Chunk F for fail-soft/cap/performance behavior

⸻

32. What Build Chunk 7 should do next

Next build document:

Build Chunk 7 — AuditLogger and OperationLedger Implementation Plan

Build Chunk 7 should plan:

* audit append functions
* audit caps
* last-turn summary format
* runtime warning/error display
* OperationLedger key functions
* permanent/recent/reward/card/source key handling
* duplicate skip audit
* first dedupe tests

After Build Chunk 7, the project will have the foundation needed to begin writing the first code skeleton soon.

⸻

33. Final Build Chunk 6 doctrine

The runtime hooks are the script’s front door.

They must be boring, strict, and safe.

Final rule:

StateManager first.
Commands suppressed.
Context read-only.
Output deduped.
Errors fail softly.
Hooks return valid text.
Runtime protects the pipeline but does not decide canon.

With Runtime planned, the next safe step is to plan AuditLogger and OperationLedger.

====================================================================================================
20_Build_Chunks_01_to_17/07_AIDRPG_Build_Chunk_07_AuditLogger_and_OperationLedger_Implementation_Plan.txt
====================================================================================================

AIDRPG Build Chunk 7 — AuditLogger and OperationLedger Implementation Plan

0. Document status

Document name: AIDRPG Build Chunk 7 — AuditLogger and OperationLedger Implementation Plan
Build target: AIDRPG Core MVP v0.1
Purpose: Define the exact AuditLogger and OperationLedger behavior before their code is generated.
Status: Practical build document.
Follows: Build Chunk 6 — Runtime Hook Contract Implementation Plan.
This document does not contain final script code.

This chunk is useful because two old failure categories keep returning unless they are handled at the foundation level:

1. The script cannot explain what it did or rejected.
2. The script repeats the same operation and duplicates rewards, items, XP, cards, time, or startup imports.

AuditLogger makes the system inspectable.

OperationLedger makes repeated effects idempotent.

Together, they make the script safer to debug, test, and expand.

⸻

1. Audit and ledger doctrine

The doctrine is:

Every important decision should be explainable.
Every one-time effect should have a key.
Every repeated effect should be skipped safely.
Audits explain; they do not mutate gameplay.
Ledger keys dedupe; they do not create gameplay.

AuditLogger is not a hidden gameplay system.

OperationLedger is not a reducer.

They are support systems that make the real pipeline trustworthy.

⸻

2. Why this chunk matters before coding

Without AuditLogger:

* rejected events become mysterious
* command output cannot explain state
* releasecheck cannot prove safety
* parser false positives are hard to detect
* card failures are invisible
* migration/quarantine issues are hidden
* fail-soft errors vanish

Without OperationLedger:

* repeated output duplicates item pickup
* repeated reward output pays twice
* repeated XP output awards twice
* repeated startup import duplicates items/abilities
* repeated card sync creates/updates too often
* repeated time skip ages or advances time twice
* repeated cleanup/audit actions bloat state

Build Chunk 7 prevents those problems from being improvised later.

⸻

3. Placement in final script

AuditLogger belongs in:

Section 07 — Audit Logger

OperationLedger belongs in:

Section 08 — Operation Ledger

They come after:

State Schema Defaults
StateManager
Runtime Hook Contract

They come before:

CommandRouter
Command Renderers
ReleaseCheckLite
EventScannerLite
CanonValidator
ReducerRegistry
Reducers
CardSyncLite
CleanupManagerLite

Reason:

Commands, runtime, validation, reducers, card sync, cleanup, migration, and releasecheck all need audit/ledger support.

⸻

4. AuditLogger responsibilities

AuditLogger owns:

* debug messages
* runtime warnings surfaced to audits
* proposal summaries
* validation summaries
* duplicate skip messages
* card audit lines
* context audit lines
* cleanup audit lines
* migration audit lines
* releasecheck result storage
* last-turn summary
* capped array handling for audit buckets

AuditLogger does not own:

* gameplay mutation
* validation decisions
* reducer commits
* Story Card writes
* Context packet generation
* parser decisions
* OperationLedger keys, except displaying or logging key use

⸻

5. OperationLedger responsibilities

OperationLedger owns:

* permanent operation keys
* recent operation keys
* reward operation keys
* card operation keys
* source-use keys
* duplicate key detection
* safe key insertion
* recent-key compaction
* duplicate skip audit hooks

OperationLedger does not own:

* applying gameplay effects
* deciding whether a proposal is valid
* rendering commands
* parsing text
* cleaning gameplay state
* writing Story Cards

⸻

6. Required AuditLogger public functions

The final script should provide these functions or equivalent section-scoped functions.

AuditLogger.debug(root, message)
AuditLogger.warn(root, message)
AuditLogger.runtimeWarning(root, message)
AuditLogger.runtimeError(root, record)
AuditLogger.proposal(root, record)
AuditLogger.validation(root, record)
AuditLogger.duplicate(root, key, reason)
AuditLogger.card(root, message)
AuditLogger.context(root, message)
AuditLogger.cleanup(root, message)
AuditLogger.migration(root, message)
AuditLogger.release(root, releaseCheckRecord)
AuditLogger.turnSummary(root, summaryRecord)
AuditLogger.repair(root, message)
AuditLogger.capAll(root)
AuditLogger.capBucket(root, bucketPath, max)
AuditLogger.safeLine(value, maxLength)

Names may change during code, but responsibilities must exist.

⸻

7. Required OperationLedger public functions

The final script should provide these functions or equivalent section-scoped functions.

OperationLedger.hasAny(root, key)
OperationLedger.hasPermanent(root, key)
OperationLedger.hasRecent(root, key)
OperationLedger.hasReward(root, key)
OperationLedger.hasCard(root, key)
OperationLedger.hasSourceUse(root, key)
OperationLedger.addPermanent(root, key)
OperationLedger.addRecent(root, key)
OperationLedger.addReward(root, key)
OperationLedger.addCard(root, key)
OperationLedger.addSourceUse(root, key)
OperationLedger.tryAdd(root, bucketName, key, reason)
OperationLedger.compactRecent(root)
OperationLedger.normalizeKey(key)
OperationLedger.makeKey(parts)
OperationLedger.auditDuplicate(root, key, reason)
OperationLedger.summary(root)

Names may change during code, but responsibilities must exist.

⸻

8. Audit bucket schema recap

AuditLogger writes to the audits bucket locked in Build Chunk 3.

audits: {
  lastTurnSummary,
  recentProposals,
  recentValidationResults,
  debugMessages,
  runtimeWarnings,
  cardAudit,
  contextAudit,
  cleanupAudit,
  migrationAudit,
  releaseCheck
}

Runtime-specific details may also live in:

runtime.runtimeWarnings
runtime.runtimeErrors
runtime.hookTrace

AuditLogger may help cap both audit and runtime arrays.

⸻

9. OperationLedger schema recap

OperationLedger writes to:

operationLedger: {
  permanentKeys,
  recentKeys,
  rewardKeys,
  cardKeys,
  sourceUseKeys,
  maxRecentKeys,
  ledgerWarnings
}

These arrays are not interchangeable.

Each one has a different lifespan and purpose.

⸻

10. Audit record style

Audit records should be compact, readable, and capped.

10.1 Good audit lines

Rejected item pickup: "jeweled cup" was hypothetical, not acquired.
Duplicate skipped: quest.reward.clear_mill_wolves.old_bren already used.
Command suppression: /inventory output was not scanned as story.
CardSync skipped generated card: generated cards are disabled in MVP.

10.2 Bad audit lines

bad event
ValidationResult[7].flags.negated true bool reason no write
undefined

Audit lines should be useful to a human testing the script.

⸻

11. Audit record object format

Some audit buckets can use strings, but proposal/validation/turn summaries should use structured records.

11.1 AuditRecord

AuditRecord: {
  turn: number,
  family: string,
  outcome: string,
  reason: string,
  sourceEventKey: string,
  preview: string,
  notes: string[]
}

11.2 TurnSummaryRecord

TurnSummaryRecord: {
  turn: number,
  inputHash: string,
  outputHash: string,
  proposalsCreated: number,
  accepted: number,
  rejected: number,
  duplicateSkipped: number,
  dirtyTargets: string[],
  notes: string[]
}

11.3 ReleaseCheckRecord

ReleaseCheckRecord: {
  turn: number,
  result: 'PASS' | 'WARN' | 'FAIL' | 'BLOCKED',
  passes: string[],
  warnings: string[],
  failures: string[],
  blockers: string[]
}

11.4 RuntimeErrorRecord

Runtime errors should use the runtime schema:

RuntimeErrorRecord: {
  turn: number,
  hook: string,
  phase: string,
  message: string,
  safeTextReturned: boolean,
  partialCommitBlocked: boolean
}

⸻

12. Audit caps

AuditLogger must cap all audit arrays.

Recommended MVP caps:

recentProposals max = 50
recentValidationResults max = 50
debugMessages max = 50
runtimeWarnings max = 25
cardAudit max = 50
contextAudit max = 50
cleanupAudit max = 50
migrationAudit max = 50
runtime.runtimeWarnings max = 25
runtime.runtimeErrors max = 20
runtime.hookTrace max = 30

12.1 Cap behavior

When capped:

* keep most recent records
* preserve critical recent failures
* avoid expensive deep sorting
* do not crash if bucket malformed
* repair malformed audit bucket safely if needed

12.2 Cap must not touch

AuditLogger must not cap or delete:

operationLedger.permanentKeys
operationLedger.rewardKeys
operationLedger.cardKeys
startup.startupOperationKeys
migration.migrationOperationKeys
active inventory
active quests
important actors
stable abilities

⸻

13. AuditLogger safe append rules

Every append function must:

1. ensure the target bucket exists or fail softly
2. convert message/record to safe format
3. add turn number if relevant
4. cap the target bucket
5. never throw uncaught errors
6. never mutate gameplay state

If audit writing itself fails, the script should fail softly or skip the audit rather than corrupt gameplay.

⸻

14. Audit categories

14.1 Debug messages

Used for general developer-readable notes.

Examples:

* StateManager repaired missing inventory bucket
* CommandRouter routed /questlog to /quests
* Parser shell inactive in v0.1

14.2 Runtime warnings

Used for hook/runtime safety issues.

Examples:

* duplicate output replay detected
* command output suppression triggered
* hook reentry blocked
* non-string hook input converted safely

14.3 Proposal audits

Used later for parser hits and proposed events.

Examples:

* proposed item pickup
* proposed quest reward payment
* proposed actor presence update

In early v0.1, proposal audit may report shell inactive.

14.4 Validation audits

Used later for accepted/rejected/pending proposals.

Examples:

* rejected because hypothetical
* rejected because item in NPC custody
* accepted because output confirmed actual handoff

14.5 Duplicate audits

Used whenever a ledger or hash skip blocks repeated effects.

Examples:

* duplicate output hash skip
* duplicate quest reward key
* duplicate startup import key

14.6 Card audit

Used for CardSync and Story Card safety.

Examples:

* generated cards disabled
* protected card registry dry-run planned
* duplicate Player Sheet detected
* card API unavailable

14.7 Context audit

Used for ContextBuilderLite.

Examples:

* selected current location packet
* skipped low-priority quest packet due to budget
* read-only guard active

14.8 Cleanup audit

Used for CleanupManagerLite.

Examples:

* capped audit logs
* deduped dirty targets
* skipped important actor

14.9 Migration audit

Used for StateManager/migration/quarantine.

Examples:

* old root detected
* old card prose not imported
* malformed field quarantined

⸻

15. Operation key doctrine

Every one-time or repeat-sensitive effect needs an operation key.

A key should answer:

What happened?
To what target?
From what source/context?
Should it ever happen again?

Key examples:

startup.import.player.name
startup.import.item.iron_sword
item.pickup.rusty_dagger.scene_12
quest.reward.clear_mill_wolves.old_bren.15_gold
xp.quest.clear_mill_wolves.complete
card.create.protected.player_sheet
card.update.protected.player_sheet.hash_abcd
cleanup.audit.compact.turn_50
time.advance.five_years.event_77

⸻

16. Operation key normalization

Operation keys must be safe and stable.

16.1 Normalize by

* converting to lowercase
* trimming whitespace
* replacing spaces with underscores
* removing unsafe punctuation
* preserving meaningful colon/dot separators if chosen
* collapsing repeated separators
* limiting length

16.2 Forbidden key contents

Keys should not contain:

* raw full output text
* huge descriptions
* unstable timestamps only
* player-facing prose paragraphs
* unbounded random values

16.3 Length recommendation

Recommended maximum:

operation key max length = 160 characters

If longer, hash the unstable/detail part.

⸻

17. Ledger bucket purposes

17.1 permanentKeys

For operations that should never repeat in this save.

Examples:

* startup imports
* unique quest completions
* unique irreversible transformations
* schema migration steps
* major one-time discoveries if needed later

17.2 recentKeys

For recent operations that should be blocked for a short window but can expire.

Examples:

* repeated minor damage line
* repeated visible item detection
* repeated scene note
* repeated low-risk parser output

17.3 rewardKeys

For rewards/payments/XP that must not duplicate.

Examples:

* quest reward paid
* bounty paid
* explicit XP award
* level milestone reward

Reward keys should be durable.

Do not cap rewardKeys like recentKeys.

17.4 cardKeys

For Story Card create/update/restore/neutralization operations.

Examples:

* protected card created
* protected card updated to render hash
* duplicate card warning
* generated card suppressed/tombstoned later

17.5 sourceUseKeys

For tracking source-specific actions.

Examples:

* output hash consumed
* command output suppressed
* setup source imported
* migration source processed

⸻

18. Ledger add behavior

18.1 Basic add flow

1. Normalize key.
2. Validate non-empty key.
3. Check target bucket exists.
4. If key already exists, return duplicate result.
5. Add key to target bucket.
6. Cap bucket if bucket is recentKeys.
7. Audit add/duplicate if needed.
8. Return result.

18.2 Add result format

LedgerResult {
  ok: boolean,
  duplicate: boolean,
  key: string,
  bucket: string,
  reason: string
}

18.3 Duplicate behavior

If duplicate:

* do not add another copy
* return duplicate result
* optionally audit duplicate skip
* reducer/caller must not apply effect again

Ledger duplicate detection must be easy for callers to use.

⸻

19. Ledger check behavior

19.1 hasAny

Checks all relevant buckets.

Use when the caller does not care where the key lives.

19.2 Specific checks

Use specific checks when bucket matters.

Examples:

* hasReward for quest reward payment
* hasCard for card update/create
* hasPermanent for startup import

19.3 Read-only checks

Checking the ledger must not mutate the ledger.

Only add or tryAdd mutates.

⸻

20. Recent key compaction

Only recentKeys should be capped by count.

Default cap:

maxRecentKeys = 250

When over cap:

* keep newest keys
* drop oldest recent keys
* do not drop permanent/reward/card keys
* audit only if useful

⸻

21. Required key families by system

21.1 Startup keys

Use permanent or startup operation keys.

Examples:

startup.import.player.name
startup.import.player.species
startup.import.item.iron_sword
startup.import.ability.mana_slash
startup.lock.turn_1

Purpose:

* prevent repeated startup import
* prevent duplicate starting items/abilities

21.2 Runtime/output keys

Use sourceUseKeys or runtime hash fields.

Examples:

source.output.hash_abc123.processed
source.command.inventory.hash_def456.suppressed

Purpose:

* prevent output replay processing
* track command suppression

21.3 Inventory keys

Use recent or permanent depending item.

Examples:

item.pickup.rusty_dagger.scene_12
item.transfer.iron_key.mara_to_player.turn_8
item.consume.healing_potion.item_44
item.destroy.old_shield.event_22

Purpose:

* prevent duplicate pickups/transfers/consumption/destruction

21.4 Quest/reward keys

Use rewardKeys/permanentKeys.

Examples:

quest.accept.clear_mill_wolves.old_bren
quest.objective.clear_mill_wolves.kill_wolves.complete
quest.reward.clear_mill_wolves.old_bren.15_gold
xp.quest.clear_mill_wolves.complete

Purpose:

* prevent duplicate quest reward/XP/objective completion

21.5 Ability keys

Examples:

ability.evidence.shadow_step.failed_attempt.turn_12
ability.stabilize.mana_slash.training_3
ability.use.mana_slash.output_hash_abc

Purpose:

* prevent repeated evidence or use cost duplication
* protect candidate/stable transitions

21.6 Body/time keys

Examples:

body.damage.wolf_bite.output_hash_abc
body.heal.rest.turn_18
time.advance.one_hour.output_hash_def
time.age.player.five_year_skip.event_77

Purpose:

* prevent repeated damage/healing/time/aging

21.7 Card keys

Examples:

card.create.protected.player_sheet
card.update.protected.player_sheet.hash_abc
card.duplicate.protected.player_sheet.turn_14
card.restore.protected.quest_log.turn_20

Purpose:

* prevent duplicate protected cards
* prevent repeated unchanged writes

21.8 Cleanup/migration keys

Examples:

cleanup.audit.compact.turn_50
cleanup.scene.expire.actor.generic_guard.scene_12
migration.init.schema_v1
migration.quarantine.card.old_inventory_crown

Purpose:

* prevent cleanup spam
* prevent repeated migration/quarantine actions

⸻

22. Duplicate skip audit format

When a duplicate is detected, audit should be readable.

Format:

Duplicate skipped: <key> — <reason>.

Examples:

Duplicate skipped: item.pickup.rusty_dagger.scene_12 — item pickup already applied.
Duplicate skipped: quest.reward.clear_mill_wolves.old_bren.15_gold — reward already paid.
Duplicate skipped: startup.import.item.iron_sword — starting item already imported.

⸻

23. AuditLogger and OperationLedger interaction

OperationLedger may call AuditLogger for duplicate skip messages.

AuditLogger must not call OperationLedger for normal audit appends.

Reason:

Avoid circular dependency where audit writes require ledger keys and ledger keys require audit writes.

Recommended dependency direction:

OperationLedger -> AuditLogger optional
AuditLogger -> no OperationLedger dependency

If circular dependency risk exists, OperationLedger can return duplicate result and caller logs through AuditLogger.

⸻

24. Ledger and reducers later

Reducers should use OperationLedger in this pattern:

1. Compute operation key.
2. Check ledger duplicate.
3. If duplicate, skip effect and audit.
4. If not duplicate, apply reducer effect.
5. Add key to ledger.
6. Mark dirty targets.
7. Audit success.

Potential stricter future pattern:

1. Check key.
2. Apply effect.
3. Add key.

If adding key after effect fails, audit critical warning.

For MVP, code should keep the pattern simple and safe.

⸻

25. Ledger and startup import

Startup import must use ledger or startup operation keys.

Example:

Setup says:

Starting equipment: iron sword.

Operation key:

startup.import.item.iron_sword

If setup import runs again:

* key already exists
* do not add second sword
* audit duplicate startup import skip

⸻

26. Ledger and command output

Commands usually do not need gameplay operation keys because they do not mutate gameplay.

But command suppression may use sourceUseKeys or runtime hashes.

Example:

source.command.inventory.hash_abc.suppressed

Do not create item/reward keys from command output.

⸻

27. Ledger and Context

Context should not create gameplay operation keys.

Context may use packet hashes later for audit spam prevention.

Context packet hashes are not gameplay operation keys.

Do not use context text to add inventory/reward/XP keys.

⸻

28. Ledger and Story Cards

Story Card operations must use cardKeys.

Story Card text must not create gameplay keys.

Examples:

Allowed:

card.update.protected.inventory_equipment.hash_abc

Forbidden:

item.add.crown_of_dawn.from_story_card_text

unless a future explicit manual import path validates it.

⸻

29. Ledger and releasecheck

ReleaseCheck must verify:

* operationLedger exists
* permanentKeys exists
* recentKeys exists
* rewardKeys exists
* cardKeys exists
* sourceUseKeys exists
* maxRecentKeys exists
* recent key cap is sane

ReleaseCheck must fail if:

* rewardKeys missing
* cardKeys missing while CardSync enabled
* recentKeys unbounded
* duplicate protection unavailable for output replay/rewards

ReleaseCheck should warn if:

* no duplicate skip audit path exists
* sourceUseKeys not used yet in early shell

⸻

30. Audit and command renderers

Command renderers use audit/ledger data.

30.1 /debuglast

Reads:

* lastTurnSummary
* recentProposals
* recentValidationResults
* runtime warnings/errors
* duplicate skip audit lines

30.2 /ledger

Reads:

* counts for permanent/recent/reward/card/sourceUse keys
* recent duplicate skip lines if available
* ledger warnings

30.3 /stateaudit

Reads:

* audit sizes
* runtime errors
* migration warnings
* cleanup warnings

30.4 /releasecheck

Reads audit and ledger health to determine readiness.

Command renderers must never mutate gameplay while showing audits/keys.

⸻

31. Audit and privacy/length safety

Audit should avoid storing full long player/AI text.

Use previews.

Recommended preview max:

160 characters

Full text can be avoided unless absolutely needed.

Do not store giant outputs in audit buckets.

Do not store full Story Card contents in audit logs.

Use hashes/previews instead.

⸻

32. Early v0.1 behavior

In early Core MVP v0.1, full parser/reducer systems may still be inactive.

AuditLogger and OperationLedger are still useful.

They should support:

* command routing audit
* command suppression audit
* duplicate output hash audit
* state repair audit
* releasecheck result storage
* card dry-run audit
* context shell audit
* ledger shell display

They do not need to wait for full gameplay automation.

⸻

33. AuditLogger implementation sequence

When coding begins, implement AuditLogger in this order:

1. safe line/preview helper
2. generic append helper
3. cap helper
4. debug/warn appenders
5. runtime warning/error appenders
6. card/context/cleanup/migration appenders
7. proposal/validation appenders
8. releasecheck storage
9. turn summary storage
10. capAll

Do not start with complex UI formatting.

Command renderers handle display.

⸻

34. OperationLedger implementation sequence

When coding begins, implement OperationLedger in this order:

1. normalizeKey
2. makeKey helper
3. ensure ledger bucket helper
4. bucket-specific has functions
5. bucket-specific add functions
6. tryAdd result object
7. recent key compaction
8. duplicate audit helper
9. summary helper for /ledger

Do not implement gameplay effects inside OperationLedger.

⸻

35. Required AuditLogger tests

BC7-001 — Debug audit append

Action:

AuditLogger.debug(root, 'test message')

Expected:

* message added to audits.debugMessages
* capped if needed
* no gameplay mutation

Fail if:

* inventory/quests/etc. change

⸻

BC7-002 — Runtime warning append

Action:

AuditLogger.runtimeWarning(root, 'duplicate output replay detected')

Expected:

* warning visible through audits/runtime as designed
* no crash

Fail if:

* warning not recorded or gameplay state changes

⸻

BC7-003 — Audit cap works

Setup:

audits.debugMessages has 100 entries
max = 50

Action:

append one more and cap.

Expected:

* length <= 50
* recent messages preserved

Fail if:

* unbounded growth

⸻

BC7-004 — Proposal audit stores compact record

Action:

Add proposal audit record.

Expected:

* record includes turn, family, outcome, reason, preview
* no full huge text dump

Fail if:

* raw giant text stored unnecessarily

⸻

BC7-005 — ReleaseCheck record stored

Action:

Store releasecheck result FAIL.

Expected:

* audits.releaseCheck updated
* meta.lastReleaseCheckResult can be updated by official path if implemented
* no gameplay mutation

Fail if:

* releasecheck storage changes inventory/quests/etc.

⸻

BC7-006 — Card audit append non-mutating

Action:

Add card audit line:

Generated cards disabled in MVP.

Expected:

* line added to cardAudit
* generatedCardsEnabled remains false
* no card created

Fail if:

* audit triggers card operation

⸻

BC7-007 — Context audit append non-mutating

Action:

Add context audit line mentioning Iron Sword custody.

Expected:

* audit added
* inventory/custody unchanged

Fail if:

* audit text changes item custody

⸻

BC7-008 — Audit append fail-soft

Simulate malformed audit bucket.

Expected:

* bucket repaired or append skipped safely
* no crash
* warning if possible

Fail if:

* uncaught audit error crashes hook

⸻

36. Required OperationLedger tests

BC7-009 — Add recent key once

Action:

addRecent('item.pickup.rusty_dagger.scene_12')

Expected:

* key added
* hasRecent true

Fail if:

* key missing

⸻

BC7-010 — Duplicate recent key blocked

Action:

Add same recent key twice.

Expected:

* second add returns duplicate result
* no second copy
* duplicate audit possible

Fail if:

* duplicate copy appears

⸻

BC7-011 — Reward key durable

Action:

addReward('quest.reward.clear_mill_wolves.old_bren.15_gold')

Expected:

* reward key added to rewardKeys
* not added only to recentKeys
* not capped by recent compaction

Fail if:

* reward key can be lost through recent cap

⸻

BC7-012 — Card key durable

Action:

addCard('card.create.protected.player_sheet')

Expected:

* card key added
* duplicate card create can be blocked

Fail if:

* card key missing or stored only as recent

⸻

BC7-013 — Permanent startup key blocks duplicate import

Action:

Add:

startup.import.item.iron_sword

twice.

Expected:

* second add duplicate
* no duplicate startup effect should be applied by caller

Fail if:

* duplicate import would not be detectable

⸻

BC7-014 — Recent key cap does not affect permanent/reward/card

Setup:

* recentKeys over max
* permanentKeys contains startup key
* rewardKeys contains reward key
* cardKeys contains card key

Action:

compact recent.

Expected:

* recentKeys capped
* permanent/reward/card keys preserved

Fail if:

* durable keys removed

⸻

BC7-015 — Normalize key stable

Input:

' Quest Reward: Clear Mill Wolves / Old Bren / 15 Gold!! '

Expected normalized key:

* lowercase
* safe separators
* no unsafe punctuation
* stable across repeated call

Fail if:

* key includes unsafe full punctuation or changes randomly

⸻

BC7-016 — Empty key rejected

Input:

''

Expected:

* add returns failure
* warning possible
* no empty key stored

Fail if:

* empty key appears in ledger

⸻

BC7-017 — hasAny checks all buckets

Setup:

Key in rewardKeys only.

Action:

hasAny(key)

Expected:

* true

Fail if:

* only recentKeys checked

⸻

BC7-018 — Ledger summary safe

Action:

Call summary for /ledger.

Expected:

* counts returned
* no full giant key dump by default
* no mutation

Fail if:

* summary mutates ledger or dumps unbounded data

⸻

37. Cross-system dedupe tests

BC7-019 — Duplicate command output suppression audit

Run /inventory, then simulate command output in Output hook.

Expected:

* command suppression audit line
* no item duplication

Fail if:

* command output creates item

⸻

BC7-020 — Duplicate output replay audit

Output repeated exactly.

Expected:

* duplicate output hash skip audit
* no repeated consequence processing

Fail if:

* no audit and duplicate consequence possible

⸻

BC7-021 — Reward duplicate key blocks second payment later

Future reducer test placeholder.

Given reward key already exists:

quest.reward.clear_mill_wolves.old_bren.15_gold

When same payment proposal appears again:

Expected:

* duplicate skip
* no second payment
* audit explains duplicate

Fail if:

* reward can pay twice

⸻

BC7-022 — Startup duplicate key blocks second starting item later

Given startup import key exists:

startup.import.item.iron_sword

When startup import runs again:

Expected:

* duplicate skip
* no second sword
* audit explains duplicate

Fail if:

* duplicate starting sword

⸻

38. ReleaseCheck requirements for Build Chunk 7

ReleaseCheck must verify:

38.1 Audit checks

* audits bucket exists
* debugMessages exists
* recentProposals exists
* recentValidationResults exists
* card/context/cleanup/migration audit buckets exist
* releaseCheck storage exists
* audit caps exist or warning if not active

38.2 Ledger checks

* operationLedger bucket exists
* permanentKeys exists
* recentKeys exists
* rewardKeys exists
* cardKeys exists
* sourceUseKeys exists
* maxRecentKeys exists
* reward/card keys are not capped as recent

38.3 Failure conditions

ReleaseCheck must fail if:

* rewardKeys missing
* cardKeys missing while CardSync present
* recentKeys unbounded
* audit writing can crash hooks
* duplicate output/reward protection unavailable

38.4 Warning conditions

ReleaseCheck may warn if:

* proposal/validation audits are shell-only
* sourceUseKeys not used yet
* duplicate skip audit is basic
* audit cap implementation is partial

⸻

39. Implementation boundaries

When coding AuditLogger and OperationLedger:

Do not also code:

* full parser
* full validators
* reducers
* card API adapter
* generated cards
* economy/crafting/investigation/domain
* manual mutation commands

This build chunk is support infrastructure only.

⸻

40. Acceptance checklist

Build Chunk 7 is complete when implementation will clearly support:

* audit append helpers
* audit caps
* debug/warning/error logging
* proposal/validation audit placeholders
* card/context/cleanup/migration audit lines
* releasecheck result storage
* last-turn summary storage
* ledger key normalization
* ledger has/add functions
* permanent/recent/reward/card/sourceUse buckets
* recent key compaction
* duplicate result objects
* duplicate skip audit
* /ledger summary support
* releasecheck audit/ledger checks
* tests BC7-001 through BC7-022

⸻
