42. What Build Chunk 8 should do next

Next build document:

Build Chunk 8 — CommandRouter and Command Renderer Implementation Plan

Build Chunk 8 should plan:

* slash command detection
* command alias routing
* active command dispatch
* disabled command responses
* unknown command handling
* command output suppression integration
* renderer function responsibilities
* exact safety checks for command non-mutation
* command tests before parser/reducer work begins

After Build Chunk 8, the first code skeleton can be planned very directly because StateManager, Runtime, AuditLogger, OperationLedger, and Commands will all be locked.

⸻

43. Final Build Chunk 7 doctrine

AuditLogger and OperationLedger are not flashy, but they protect the project from two of the most dangerous failure types: invisible behavior and repeated behavior.

Final rule:

If the script does something important, audit it.
If the script might do something twice, key it.
If a duplicate appears, skip it safely.
If an audit grows too large, cap it safely.
If a key protects a permanent outcome, never compact it away as recent noise.

With AuditLogger and OperationLedger planned, the next safe step is to plan CommandRouter and command renderers.

====================================================================================================
20_Build_Chunks_01_to_17/08_AIDRPG_Build_Chunk_08_CommandRouter_and_Command_Renderer_Implementation_Plan.txt
====================================================================================================

AIDRPG Build Chunk 8 — CommandRouter and Command Renderer Implementation Plan

0. Document status

Document name: AIDRPG Build Chunk 8 — CommandRouter and Command Renderer Implementation Plan
Build target: AIDRPG Core MVP v0.1
Purpose: Define the exact command detection, routing, alias handling, disabled-command behavior, command renderer ownership, command output suppression, and command safety tests before command code is generated.
Status: Practical build document.
Follows: Build Chunk 7 — AuditLogger and OperationLedger Implementation Plan.
This document does not contain final script code.

This chunk is useful because commands are the player’s main way to see, test, and debug the system.

If commands mutate state, command output becomes a hidden gameplay system.

If commands lie, the player cannot trust the script.

If command output is parsed as story, old duplication bugs return.

If aliases are inconsistent, old test workflows break.

If disabled commands activate deferred systems, the MVP boundary collapses.

Build Chunk 8 makes command behavior exact before coding.

⸻

1. Command doctrine

The command doctrine is:

Commands inspect state.
Commands explain state.
Commands route safely.
Commands suppress their own output.
Commands do not mutate gameplay state.
Commands do not activate deferred systems.

Commands may update runtime command metadata and diagnostic audit lines.

Commands may not create gameplay truth.

⸻

2. Why this chunk comes before parser/reducer work

Command safety must exist before parser/reducer automation.

Reason:

Once parser/reducer systems become active, command output becomes dangerous unless suppression is already locked.

Examples:

* /inventory displaying Rusty Dagger must not create another dagger.
* /quests displaying Reward: 15 gold promised must not pay the reward.
* /abilities displaying Shadow Step — candidate must not stabilize Shadow Step.
* /actors displaying Mara — elsewhere must not make Mara present.
* /location displaying Travel Intent: chapel must not move the player to chapel.

Build Chunk 8 protects those cases before automation starts.

⸻

3. Placement in final script

CommandRouter belongs in:

Section 09 — Command Router

Command renderers belong in:

Section 10 — Command Renderers

They depend on:

Constants
Utils
StateManager
Runtime suppression helpers
AuditLogger
OperationLedger summary helpers
ReleaseCheckLite for /releasecheck

They must not depend on:

EventScannerLite for command output parsing
ProposalBuilder for command output
CanonValidator for command mutation
ReducerRegistry for MVP gameplay mutation
CardSync live writes
Cleanup destructive actions
Migration aggressive import

⸻

4. CommandRouter responsibilities

CommandRouter owns:

* slash command detection
* command text normalization
* command argument parsing
* command alias resolution
* active command dispatch
* disabled command dispatch
* unknown command handling
* command output suppression integration
* command audit line if desired
* safe command error handling

CommandRouter does not own:

* gameplay mutation
* state schema repair beyond StateManager call
* command output formatting details beyond dispatch
* releasecheck logic itself
* card sync live writes
* cleanup destructive actions
* manual mutation behavior

⸻

5. Command renderer responsibilities

Command renderers own:

* readable command output text
* state summaries
* empty-state display
* warning display
* disabled system message formatting
* alias note display
* output truncation/summarization

Command renderers do not own:

* command detection
* alias routing
* gameplay mutation
* StateManager repairs
* OperationLedger mutation
* parser/reducer calls
* card API calls
* quarantine resolution

⸻

6. Required CommandRouter functions

The final script should provide these functions or equivalent section-scoped functions.

CommandRouter.isCommand(text)
CommandRouter.parse(text)
CommandRouter.normalizeCommandName(name)
CommandRouter.resolveAlias(commandName)
CommandRouter.route(root, inputText)
CommandRouter.dispatchActive(root, parsedCommand)
CommandRouter.dispatchDisabled(root, parsedCommand, disabledInfo)
CommandRouter.renderUnknown(root, parsedCommand)
CommandRouter.safeRoute(root, inputText)
CommandRouter.getDisabledInfo(commandName)
CommandRouter.isActiveCommand(commandName)
CommandRouter.isDisabledCommand(commandName)
CommandRouter.markCommandAudit(root, commandName, outcome)

Names may change in code, but responsibilities must exist.

⸻

7. Required command renderer functions

The final script should provide renderers for every active MVP command.

Commands.renderHelp(root, context)
Commands.renderVersion(root, context)
Commands.renderSheet(root, context)
Commands.renderStatus(root, context)
Commands.renderInventory(root, context)
Commands.renderAbilities(root, context)
Commands.renderQuests(root, context)
Commands.renderActors(root, context)
Commands.renderLocation(root, context)
Commands.renderTime(root, context)
Commands.renderDebugLast(root, context)
Commands.renderCardAudit(root, context)
Commands.renderContextAudit(root, context)
Commands.renderStateAudit(root, context)
Commands.renderLedger(root, context)
Commands.renderDirty(root, context)
Commands.renderMigration(root, context)
Commands.renderQuarantine(root, context)
Commands.renderReleaseCheck(root, context)
Commands.renderStartupAudit(root, context)
Commands.renderDisabled(root, disabledInfo, context)
Commands.renderUnknown(root, parsedCommand, context)
Commands.withHeader(title, status, bodyLines)
Commands.truncateOutput(text, maxChars)

Renderer function names may vary, but every output from Build Chunk 4 must be supported.

⸻

8. Command detection rules

8.1 Command prefix

A command is an input beginning with:

/

followed by at least one command character.

Examples:

/help
/inventory
/releasecheck
/craft sword
/cardsync live on

8.2 Non-command examples

These are not commands:

I swing my sword.
Mara says /help as a joke.
The sign reads: /inventory.
I type the symbol / in the dust.

Only player input beginning with / at the start of the input is a command.

8.3 Whitespace

Leading whitespace may be trimmed for command detection.

Examples:

  /help

should route as /help.

8.4 Case

Commands should be case-insensitive.

Examples:

/HELP
/Inventory
/ReLeAsEcHeCk

should normalize to:

/help
/inventory
/releasecheck

⸻

9. Command parse result schema

CommandRouter should parse command input into a structured object.

ParsedCommand {
  rawText: string,
  commandName: string,
  normalizedName: string,
  argsText: string,
  args: string[],
  isCommand: boolean,
  aliasOf: string,
  routeStatus: 'active' | 'disabled' | 'unknown' | 'error',
  notes: string[]
}

9.1 Example

Input:

/questlog active

Parsed:

rawText = '/questlog active'
commandName = 'questlog'
normalizedName = 'quests'
argsText = 'active'
args = ['active']
isCommand = true
aliasOf = 'questlog'
routeStatus = 'active'

9.2 Args in MVP

Most MVP commands ignore args or treat them as informational.

MVP should not implement mutation args like:

/additem Crown
/setlevel 99

Those remain disabled.

⸻

10. Active MVP command set

The active MVP commands are:

/help
/version
/sheet
/status
/inventory
/abilities
/quests
/actors
/location
/time
/debuglast
/cardaudit
/contextaudit
/stateaudit
/ledger
/dirty
/migration
/quarantine
/releasecheck
/startupaudit

These commands are inspect-only or diagnostic-only.

No active MVP command may directly mutate gameplay state.

⸻

11. Active command dispatch map

The command router should use a dispatch map.

help -> renderHelp
version -> renderVersion
sheet -> renderSheet
status -> renderStatus
inventory -> renderInventory
abilities -> renderAbilities
quests -> renderQuests
actors -> renderActors
location -> renderLocation
time -> renderTime
debuglast -> renderDebugLast
cardaudit -> renderCardAudit
contextaudit -> renderContextAudit
stateaudit -> renderStateAudit
ledger -> renderLedger
dirty -> renderDirty
migration -> renderMigration
quarantine -> renderQuarantine
releasecheck -> renderReleaseCheck
startupaudit -> renderStartupAudit

Avoid a large tangled if/else chain if a map/object is safer in the target environment.

A clear switch statement is acceptable if simpler.

⸻

12. Active alias map

The router must support old/common aliases safely.

questlog -> quests
finalcheck -> releasecheck
finalrelease -> releasecheck
rc -> releasecheck
cardedits -> cardaudit
tombstones -> cardaudit
startup -> startupaudit
continuity -> stateaudit or contextaudit, MVP preference: stateaudit
memory -> contextaudit
selftest -> releasecheck

12.1 Alias output note

When alias is used, command output may include:

Alias: /questlog -> /quests

This helps the user understand what happened.

12.2 Alias safety

Aliases must not bypass disabled system gates.

Example:

/cardsync live on

must not enable live CardSync through an alias or args.

⸻

13. Disabled command families

The router must recognize disabled command families and return an honest disabled message.

13.1 Economy disabled commands

/shop
/buy
/sell
/economy
/price
/barter
/debt

Disabled reason:

Economy / Transaction Ledger Mini-Spec required.

13.2 Crafting disabled commands

/craft
/crafting
/recipes
/materials
/forge
/alchemy

Disabled reason:

Crafting / Materials Mini-Spec required.

13.3 Investigation disabled commands

/cases
/case
/clues
/clue
/evidence
/investigation

Disabled reason:

Investigation / Evidence Mini-Spec required.

13.4 Reputation/faction disabled commands

/reputation
/rep
/factions
/faction
/titles

Disabled reason:

Reputation / Faction / Titles Mini-Spec required.

Note:

/sheet may display titles already stored, but title automation is deferred.

13.5 Domain/labor disabled commands

/domain
/workers
/labor
/projects
/base
/settlement

Disabled reason:

Domain / Labor Mini-Spec required.

13.6 Absorption/evolution disabled commands

/absorb
/evolve
/devour
/consume_power
/evolution

Disabled reason:

Absorption / Evolution Mini-Spec required.

13.7 Party/summon/multi-protagonist disabled or limited commands

/party
/followers
/summons
/summon
/protagonists
/players
/groups

MVP behavior:

* /party may map to /actors if it only displays companions.
* advanced party controls are disabled.
* multi-protagonist commands are disabled.

Disabled reason:

Party / Followers / Summons / Multi-Protagonist Spec required.

13.8 Scale/warfare disabled commands

/scale
/warfare
/battlefield
/army
/armies
/groupcombat
/threat
/combat

MVP behavior:

* /combat may return disabled or status-only message.
* full combat automation is not active.

Disabled reason:

Combat/Progression v0.2 or Scale / Group Combat Spec required.

13.9 CardSync live/generated disabled commands

/cardsync
/generatecards
/cardlive
/cards live
/cardsync live on

MVP behavior:

* /cardaudit is active diagnostic.
* live generated cards remain disabled.
* live CardSync write toggles are disabled.

Disabled reason:

Story Card API Adapter tests and Generated CardSync Beta Spec required.

13.10 Manual mutation disabled commands

/additem
/removeitem
/sethp
/setmp
/setep
/setlevel
/addxp
/setability
/completequest
/payreward
/setactor
/setlocation
/settime
/importcard
/resolvequarantine

Disabled reason:

Manual Override Spec required.

⸻

14. Unknown command behavior

If a command starts with / but is not active, aliased, or recognized as disabled, return unknown command output.

Required behavior:

* no crash
* no gameplay mutation
* command output suppression active
* suggest /help

Example:

[AIDRPG] Unknown Command
Status: Not Recognized
Command: /banana
Use /help to see active MVP commands.
No gameplay state was changed.

⸻

15. Command routing flow

Command routing should follow this order:

1. Receive input text from Input hook.
2. Parse command.
3. Normalize command name.
4. Resolve alias.
5. If active command: dispatch active renderer.
6. Else if disabled command/family: render disabled message.
7. Else: render unknown command message.
8. Truncate output if needed.
9. Return command output to Runtime.
10. Runtime marks command suppression.

CommandRouter itself should not return raw undefined/null.

If renderer fails, CommandRouter safeRoute should return fail-soft command error text.

⸻

16. Command output suppression integration

CommandRouter returns command output.

Runtime marks suppression.

Recommended integration:

const output = CommandRouter.safeRoute(root, inputText)
Runtime.setCommandSuppression(root, parsedCommand.normalizedName, output)
return output

16.1 Command output prefix

Every command output should begin with:

[AIDRPG]

Runtime may use this as secondary suppression detection.

16.2 Command output hash

Runtime should hash the final command output after truncation.

This becomes:

runtime.lastCommandHash

16.3 Command output must not be scanned

The Output hook must skip parser/proposal/reducer processing for suppressed command output.

⸻

17. Renderer safety rules

Every renderer must:

* tolerate missing/malformed bucket values
* use safe defaults/display
* show none for empty lists
* avoid full raw JSON dumps
* cap long output
* not call reducers
* not call parser
* not call CardSync live writes
* not call cleanup destructive actions
* not enable feature flags
* not resolve quarantine

Renderers can call read-only helpers.

⸻

18. Renderer formatting helpers

Command renderers should share small formatting helpers.

Recommended helpers:

Commands.header(title, status)
Commands.section(title, lines)
Commands.line(label, value)
Commands.noneIfEmpty(list)
Commands.renderList(list, formatter, maxItems)
Commands.renderWarnings(list, maxItems)
Commands.truncate(text, maxChars)
Commands.aliasNote(aliasOf, normalizedName)
Commands.safetyNote(lines)
Commands.safeJoin(list, fallback)

These helpers must not mutate gameplay state.

⸻

19. Output caps by command

Use Build Chunk 4 caps.

/help: 2000
/version: 1200
/sheet: 1500
/status: 1200
/inventory: 1800
/abilities: 1800
/quests: 2200
/actors: 1800
/location: 1500
/time: 900
/debuglast: 2500
/cardaudit: 2500
/contextaudit: 2200
/stateaudit: 2500
/ledger: 1800
/dirty: 1800
/migration: 1800
/quarantine: 2200
/releasecheck: 3000
/startupaudit: 1800

If output is too long:

* keep high-priority sections
* summarize remaining records
* include omitted count

⸻

20. Active command renderer requirements

20.1 /help

Must include:

* active player commands
* diagnostic commands
* disabled systems
* safety notes
* generated cards disabled

Must not claim advanced systems are active.

20.2 /version

Must include:

* project/build/schema
* build stage
* authority
* feature flags
* migration status
* last releasecheck result

20.3 /sheet

Must include:

* identity
* build
* progression
* stats
* temporary identity effects

Must separate name/title/disguise/illusion.

20.4 /status

Must include:

* HP/MP/EP
* fatigue
* death boundary
* body traits
* injuries
* conditions

Must not heal/damage.

20.5 /inventory

Must separate:

* currency
* equipped
* owned inventory
* available nearby
* unavailable/custody
* lost/sold/destroyed/consumed

Must not move items.

20.6 /abilities

Must separate:

* stable
* unstable
* candidate/hinted
* passives/talents
* sealed/unavailable

Must not stabilize abilities.

20.7 /quests

Must separate:

* rumored/offered
* active
* blocked
* ready to turn in
* completed/rewarded
* failed/archived

Must distinguish promised/ready/paid/disputed rewards.

20.8 /actors

Must separate:

* companions/familiars
* important actors
* scene-only actors
* custody notes
* uncertain/claimed information

Must not promote scene actors.

20.9 /location

Must show:

* current location
* current scene
* present actors
* visible/available items
* hazards
* exits
* travel intent separately

Must not change location.

20.10 /time

Must show:

* time text
* turn
* last advance
* deadlines

Must not advance time.

20.11 /debuglast

Must show:

* last turn summary
* recent proposal summaries
* recent validation summaries
* runtime warnings/errors
* parser shell status if inactive

Must not replay anything.

20.12 /cardaudit

Must show:

* dry-run/safe mode
* live writes status
* generated card status
* API status
* protected registry status
* dirty cards
* duplicate warnings
* tombstones
* recent card audit

Must not write cards.

20.13 /contextaudit

Must show:

* ContextBuilder mode
* read-only guard
* last context hash
* budget
* selected/skipped packets if available

Must not generate gameplay.

20.14 /stateaudit

Must show:

* schema status
* integrity warnings/errors
* state size/caps
* migration/quarantine
* cleanup
* important warnings

Must not dump full state.

20.15 /ledger

Must show:

* key bucket counts
* recent duplicate skips if available
* ledger warnings

Must not add/remove keys.

20.16 /dirty

Must show:

* total targets
* queued/processed/blocked/error counts
* highest priority targets
* dirty protected cards
* context dirty reasons

Must not process dirty targets.

20.17 /migration

Must show:

* migration status
* current migration version
* counts
* warnings/errors
* old card prose non-canon note

Must not run aggressive migration.

20.18 /quarantine

Must show:

* unresolved count
* records by severity
* preview/reason/suggested action
* non-gameplay note

Must not resolve/import/delete quarantine.

20.19 /releasecheck

Must run ReleaseCheckLite and render result.

May update releasecheck audit result through official ReleaseCheck/Audit path.

Must not fix gameplay state or enable features.

20.20 /startupaudit

Must show:

* setup status
* setup imported
* gameplay started
* setup locked turn
* imported fields
* rejected fields
* warnings

Must not rerun startup import.

⸻

21. Disabled command renderer requirements

Disabled command output must include:

[AIDRPG] <System Name>
Status: Disabled in Core MVP v0.1
<system> is not active yet.
Required before activation: <mini-spec/build phase>.
No gameplay state was changed.

21.1 Disabled command must not

* create placeholder project
* create item
* change currency
* toggle feature flag
* resolve quarantine
* write cards
* add ability
* advance time

⸻

22. Command non-mutation enforcement

Because JavaScript-like environments do not automatically enforce read-only renderers, the plan must rely on boundaries and tests.

22.1 Required enforcement methods

* renderers do not call reducers
* renderers do not call parser
* renderers do not call CardSync live writes
* renderers do not call Cleanup destructive actions
* commands have tests comparing gameplay buckets before/after
* ReleaseCheck verifies command mutation risk

22.2 Optional future enforcement

A future test harness may snapshot gameplay buckets before/after command rendering.

If any gameplay bucket changes from command rendering, command test fails.

⸻

23. Command audit behavior

CommandRouter may add light audit lines.

Examples:

Command routed: /questlog -> /quests.
Command disabled: /craft requires Crafting Mini-Spec.
Unknown command: /banana.

Command audit must be capped.

Do not audit every command forever unbounded.

Command audit is diagnostic only.

⸻

24. Command error handling

If command rendering throws an error:

CommandRouter safe route should return:

[AIDRPG] Command Error
Status: Failed Safely
The command failed safely. Gameplay state was not intentionally changed.
Run /debuglast or /stateaudit for details.

Runtime should also record runtime error through fail-soft if the exception escapes router.

Command failure must not erase state.

⸻

25. Command output and parser interaction

Command output is explicitly non-canon.

25.1 Parser must not scan command output

Output hook suppression must happen before EventScannerLite.

25.2 Command output prefix

[AIDRPG] is a non-story marker.

Parser should reject text beginning with [AIDRPG] as non-canon source even if suppression flag fails.

25.3 Commands that display dangerous words

Commands may display dangerous words like:

* gold
* XP
* stable ability
* candidate ability
* active quest
* Crown of Dawn
* Mara
* dead
* reward paid

These are display only.

They must not create or modify state.

⸻

26. ReleaseCheck requirements for commands

ReleaseCheck must verify:

* active command list exists
* command output prefix is used
* suppression integration exists
* disabled command families are recognized
* unknown command handler exists
* commands are inspect-only in MVP
* manual mutation commands disabled
* generated CardSync commands disabled
* /help honesty requirements satisfied
* /releasecheck command exists

ReleaseCheck must fail if:

* command output can be scanned as story
* /help claims disabled systems are active
* manual mutation commands are enabled
* generated cards can be enabled by command
* disabled commands mutate gameplay

ReleaseCheck may warn if:

* alias coverage is incomplete
* command output caps are not fully implemented
* some diagnostic commands are shell-only

⸻

27. Command implementation sequence

When coding begins, implement commands in this order:

1. command detection
2. parse result object
3. active command set
4. alias map
5. disabled command family map
6. unknown command renderer
7. disabled command renderer
8. shared renderer formatting helpers
9. /help
10. /version
11. /sheet, /status, /inventory, /abilities, /quests
12. /actors, /location, /time
13. diagnostic renderers
14. /releasecheck renderer integration
15. suppression integration with Runtime
16. command tests

Do not start by implementing manual mutation commands.

⸻

28. Required command tests

BC8-001 — Slash command detected

Input:

/help

Expected:

* parsed as command
* normalized name help

Fail if:

* treated as story input

⸻

BC8-002 — Non-command not detected

Input:

Mara says /help as a joke.

Expected:

* not a command because input does not begin with command token

Fail if:

* routed as /help

⸻

BC8-003 — Leading whitespace command detected

Input:

   /inventory

Expected:

* routed to inventory

Fail if:

* treated as story

⸻

BC8-004 — Case-insensitive command

Input:

/ReLeAsEcHeCk

Expected:

* normalized to releasecheck

Fail if:

* unknown due to case

⸻

BC8-005 — Alias /questlog maps to /quests

Input:

/questlog

Expected:

* routes to quests renderer
* optional alias note
* no quest mutation

Fail if:

* unknown or mutates quest

⸻

BC8-006 — Alias /finalcheck maps to /releasecheck

Input:

/finalcheck

Expected:

* routes to releasecheck
* no gameplay mutation

Fail if:

* unknown or false PASS without checks

⸻

BC8-007 — Unknown command safe

Input:

/banana

Expected:

* unknown command output
* starts with [AIDRPG]
* no gameplay mutation
* suppression will be set by Runtime

Fail if:

* crash or parser story processing

⸻

BC8-008 — Disabled crafting safe

Input:

/craft iron sword

Expected:

* disabled message
* no crafting project
* no item created
* no feature flag enabled

Fail if:

* crafting activates

⸻

BC8-009 — Disabled economy safe

Input:

/buy potion

Expected:

* disabled economy message
* currency unchanged
* no potion added

Fail if:

* transaction occurs

⸻

BC8-010 — Disabled generated cards safe

Input:

/cardsync live on

Expected:

* disabled CardSync live message
* generatedCardsEnabled false
* liveWritesEnabled false

Fail if:

* live/generate toggles enabled

⸻

BC8-011 — Disabled manual mutation safe

Input:

/additem Crown of Dawn

Expected:

* manual mutation disabled message
* no Crown added

Fail if:

* item added

⸻

BC8-012 — /help honest

Run /help.

Expected:

* active commands listed
* disabled systems listed
* safety notes included
* no false active claims

Fail if:

* economy/crafting/generated cards claimed active

⸻

BC8-013 — /inventory non-mutating

State before:

* one Rusty Dagger

Run /inventory.

Expected:

* one Rusty Dagger still
* no item duplicated

Fail if:

* command changes inventory

⸻

BC8-014 — /quests non-mutating

State before:

* quest reward promised 15 gold
* currency 0 gold

Run /quests.

Expected:

* reward remains promised
* currency remains 0 gold

Fail if:

* command pays reward

⸻

BC8-015 — /abilities non-mutating

State before:

* Shadow Step candidate

Run /abilities.

Expected:

* Shadow Step remains candidate

Fail if:

* command stabilizes ability

⸻

BC8-016 — /actors non-mutating

State before:

* Mara elsewhere

Run /actors.

Expected:

* Mara remains elsewhere

Fail if:

* command makes Mara present

⸻

BC8-017 — /location non-mutating

State before:

* current location Old Mill
* travel intent chapel

Run /location.

Expected:

* current location remains Old Mill
* chapel remains travel intent only

Fail if:

* command moves player

⸻

BC8-018 — Command output prefix present

Run every active command.

Expected:

* each output begins with [AIDRPG]

Fail if:

* any command lacks prefix

⸻

BC8-019 — Command output cap works

Setup:

* inventory has many records

Run /inventory.

Expected:

* output capped/summarized
* no crash
* omitted count if needed

Fail if:

* huge unbounded output

⸻

BC8-020 — Renderer handles malformed bucket safely

Setup:

* abilities.records malformed

Run /abilities.

Expected:

* safe warning output
* no crash
* StateManager may repair structural issue if designed

Fail if:

* command crashes

⸻

BC8-021 — Command renderer error fail-soft

Simulate renderer throw.

Expected:

* command error output
* no gameplay mutation
* runtime/audit error recorded if possible

Fail if:

* uncaught crash

⸻

BC8-022 — Command suppression integration

Run /inventory, then simulate its output through Output hook.

Expected:

* Output hook suppresses scan
* no duplicated items

Fail if:

* command output scanned as story

⸻

BC8-023 — Command audit capped

Run many commands.

Expected:

* command/debug audit capped
* no unbounded audit growth

Fail if:

* debugMessages grows endlessly

⸻

BC8-024 — /releasecheck catches command hazards

Test unsafe config:

* manualMutationCommandsEnabled true

Run /releasecheck.

Expected:

* FAIL
* clear reason

Fail if:

* releasecheck passes

⸻

29. Command acceptance checklist

Build Chunk 8 is complete when implementation will clearly support:

* command detection
* command parsing
* command normalization
* active command dispatch
* alias routing
* disabled command family recognition
* unknown command handling
* command output prefix
* command output suppression integration
* shared renderer helpers
* all active command renderers
* disabled command renderer
* command caps/summarization
* renderer fail-soft handling
* command non-mutation boundaries
* releasecheck command safety checks
* tests BC8-001 through BC8-024

⸻

30. Relationship to previous build chunks

Build Chunk 8 depends on:

* Build Chunk 1 for active and forbidden systems
* Build Chunk 2 for section placement and command renderer ownership
* Build Chunk 3 for state bucket names
* Build Chunk 4 for exact command output formats
* Build Chunk 5 for StateManager safety before command rendering
* Build Chunk 6 for Runtime command suppression
* Build Chunk 7 for audit/ledger command summaries
* Gap Chunk E for command honesty and disabled behavior

⸻

31. What Build Chunk 9 should do next

Next build document:

Build Chunk 9 — EventScannerLite Parser Shell Plan

Build Chunk 9 should plan the safe parser shell and the first limited event scanning boundaries:

* non-canon source suppression
* parser hit schema
* safety flags
* quote/negation/hypothetical/dream detection shell
* input intent only
* output parser gating
* no direct state mutation
* parser tests before proposal/validator activation

Build Chunk 9 should not yet build full reducers.

⸻

32. Final Build Chunk 8 doctrine

Commands are not gameplay.

Commands are a safe inspection interface.

They must be predictable before parser and reducer automation becomes active.

Final rule:

Detect commands only at the input command surface.
Route aliases safely.
Disable deferred systems honestly.
Render state without changing it.
Prefix and suppress every command output.
Never let command text become story canon.

With CommandRouter and command renderers planned, the next safe step is the parser shell.

====================================================================================================
20_Build_Chunks_01_to_17/09_AIDRPG_Build_Chunk_09_EventScannerLite_Parser_Shell_Plan.txt
====================================================================================================

AIDRPG Build Chunk 9 — EventScannerLite Parser Shell Plan

0. Document status

Document name: AIDRPG Build Chunk 9 — EventScannerLite Parser Shell Plan
Build target: AIDRPG Core MVP v0.1
Purpose: Define the exact safe parser shell, parser source rules, parser hit schema, safety-flag detection, family boundaries, and parser tests before parser code is generated.
Status: Practical build document.
Follows: Build Chunk 8 — CommandRouter and Command Renderer Implementation Plan.
This document does not contain final script code.

This chunk is useful because the parser is where many old bugs begin.

If the parser is too aggressive, visible items become inventory, promises become payments, rumors become facts, failed abilities become stable abilities, and dreams become canon.

If the parser mutates state directly, CanonValidator and reducers become useless.

If the parser scans commands, context, cards, or audit text, the script corrupts itself.

Build Chunk 9 locks the parser as a safe detector only.

⸻

1. Parser shell doctrine

The parser doctrine is:

The parser detects possible events.
The parser does not create truth.
The parser does not mutate gameplay state.
The parser does not decide canon.
The parser does not bypass validation.

The parser output is a ParserHit.

A ParserHit is not an accepted event.

A ParserHit is only evidence that later systems may turn into an EventProposal.

⸻

2. Why this chunk is a shell, not full parser automation

Build Chunk 9 should not activate the full game parser all at once.

The first parser implementation should be conservative and structurally correct.

It should prove:

* source suppression works
* command output is ignored
* context/card/debug text is ignored
* safety flags are detected
* input is intent only
* output can produce candidate hits
* parser hits have the right shape
* parser hits do not mutate state
* parser output is capped and auditable

Full event interpretation belongs to later proposal, validator, and reducer chunks.

⸻

3. Placement in final script

EventScannerLite belongs in:

Section 12 — Event Scanner Lite Shell

It depends on:

Constants
Utils
Runtime read-only suppression status
AuditLogger for debug/proposal-shell audit if needed

It must not depend on:

ReducerRegistry
InventoryReducer
QuestReducerLite
AbilityReducer
ProgressionReducerLite
CardSyncLite
ContextBuilderLite mutation
StateManager gameplay repair

EventScannerLite may read state lightly for source suppression or current parser mode, but should avoid heavy world scans.

⸻

4. Parser responsibilities

EventScannerLite owns:

* deciding whether a text source is eligible to scan
* splitting current text into safe windows/clauses
* detecting safety flags
* detecting broad event-family hints
* emitting ParserHit records
* capping parser hits
* recording parser shell audit if useful

EventScannerLite does not own:

* creating EventProposal objects
* resolving exact canonical targets
* validating events
* mutating inventory
* mutating abilities
* mutating quests
* mutating actors
* mutating places
* mutating time
* awarding XP
* paying rewards
* creating Story Cards

⸻

5. Required EventScannerLite public functions

The final script should provide these functions or equivalents.

EventScannerLite.shouldScanSource(sourceInfo, root)
EventScannerLite.scanInput(text, root)
EventScannerLite.scanOutput(text, root)
EventScannerLite.scanText(text, sourceHook, root)
EventScannerLite.splitIntoWindows(text)
EventScannerLite.detectSafetyFlags(windowText)
EventScannerLite.detectFamilyHints(windowText, flags, sourceHook)
EventScannerLite.makeHit(data)
EventScannerLite.capHits(hits, maxHits)
EventScannerLite.rejectNonCanonSource(reason)
EventScannerLite.isCommandOutput(text, root)
EventScannerLite.isAidRpgDiagnosticText(text)
EventScannerLite.auditScan(root, summary)

Names may change in code, but the responsibilities must exist.

⸻

6. Parser source authority

Parser must respect source authority.

Allowed scan sources in MVP shell:

input
output

Disallowed scan sources:

command_output
context
story_card
protected_card
generated_card
debug
audit
help
releasecheck
migration_log
old_log
archive
unknown_noncanon

6.1 Input source rule

Input can create intent/attempt hits only.

Input must not create completed success hits.

Example:

I take the sword.

Input hit may be:

item_take_attempt
completedAction = false
attemptOnly = true

Input must not add sword to inventory.

6.2 Output source rule

Output can create possible completed consequence hits if wording is clear and safe.

Example:

You pick up the sword.

Output hit may be:

inventory.item_pickup
completedAction = true

But the parser still does not commit.

6.3 Command output rule

Command output is never a gameplay source.

Any text beginning with:

[AIDRPG]

should be treated as non-canon diagnostic/command text unless explicitly proven otherwise by the hook contract.

⸻

7. SourceInfo schema

Parser source checks should use a source info object when possible.

SourceInfo {
  sourceHook: 'input' | 'output' | 'context' | 'command' | 'manual' | 'migration' | 'setup' | 'system' | 'unknown',
  textHash: string,
  isCommandOutput: boolean,
  isContextText: boolean,
  isCardText: boolean,
  isAuditText: boolean,
  isDebugText: boolean,
  isHelpText: boolean,
  scanAllowed: boolean,
  reason: string
}

For early v0.1, the sourceInfo may be simple, but the distinction must be planned.

⸻

8. ParserHit schema

Every parser hit must use the universal ParserHit schema from Gap Chunk D.

ParserHit {
  hitId: string,
  sourceHook: 'input' | 'output' | 'manual' | 'migration' | 'setup',
  rawText: string,
  normalizedText: string,
  matchedText: string,
  eventFamily: string,
  eventType: string,
  subjectText: string,
  objectText: string,
  actorText: string,
  itemText: string,
  placeText: string,
  questText: string,
  abilityText: string,
  completedAction: boolean,
  attemptOnly: boolean,
  negated: boolean,
  hypothetical: boolean,
  quotedSpeech: boolean,
  dreamOrVision: boolean,
  rumorOrClaim: boolean,
  failedOrRevoked: boolean,
  conditional: boolean,
  futureIntent: boolean,
  confidence: number,
  truthStatus: string,
  evidenceType: string,
  parserNotes: string[]
}

8.1 Required safe defaults

If a field is unknown, use safe defaults:

completedAction = false
attemptOnly = true if sourceHook is input or wording is unclear
negated = false unless detected
hypothetical = false unless detected
quotedSpeech = false unless detected
dreamOrVision = false unless detected
rumorOrClaim = false unless detected
failedOrRevoked = false unless detected
conditional = false unless detected
futureIntent = false unless detected
confidence = low/moderate, not high by default
truthStatus = 'unknown' or 'observed' depending family
evidenceType = 'parser_shell'
parserNotes = []

8.2 Hit ID rule

Hit IDs should be stable enough for audit, not random-only.

Recommended pattern:

hit.<sourceHook>.<eventFamily>.<hash(windowText)>.<index>

⸻

9. Text normalization rules

Parser normalization should be safe and meaning-preserving.

9.1 Normalize for matching

Allowed:

* convert to safe string
* trim leading/trailing whitespace
* collapse repeated whitespace
* create lowercase matching copy
* normalize curly quotes to plain quotes for quote detection

9.2 Do not strip safety words

Never remove or ignore safety words like:

not
never
no
don't
didn't
can't
failed
almost
could
might
would
if
unless
dream
vision
rumor
says
claims
will
later

These words often decide whether a hit is safe.

9.3 Raw text preservation

Every hit must keep a raw/matched text preview for audit.

Do not store huge full output text in every hit.

Use window text and short previews.

⸻

10. Window splitting rules

Parser should scan current input/output text in windows, not the whole campaign history.

10.1 Window sources

Windows may be:

* sentences
* clauses
* short line segments
* command-free text chunks

10.2 Why windows matter

Output:

You reach for the sword, but Mara stops you. The dagger falls to the ground.

The parser should not treat the whole paragraph as a successful sword pickup.

Each candidate should be considered in its clause/window.

10.3 MVP window limit

Recommended early limits:

maxInputTextLengthToParse = 3000 characters
maxOutputTextLengthToParse = 5000 characters
maxWindows = 30
maxWindowLength = 500 characters

If exceeded:

* scan first safe windows or highest relevance windows
* audit skip if needed
* do not crash

⸻

11. Safety flag detection shell

EventScannerLite must detect key safety flags before family-specific parsing.

Required flags:

negated
hypothetical
attemptOnly
failedOrRevoked
quotedSpeech
rumorOrClaim
dreamOrVision
conditional
futureIntent

These flags must be included in every ParserHit.

⸻

12. Negation detection

12.1 Markers

Detect negation markers such as:

not
never
no longer
doesn't
didn't
do not
don't
cannot
can't
without taking
refuse
refuses
decline
declines
avoid
avoids
stop short of
fail to
fails to

12.2 Examples

You do not take the sword.

Hit may be item-related, but:

negated = true
completedAction = false for pickup success
Old Bren refuses to pay you.

Hit may be reward-related, but:

negated/failedOrRevoked = true
no payment success

⸻

13. Hypothetical detection

13.1 Markers

could
might
may
would
should
if you wanted
possible
possibly
perhaps
maybe
imagine
suppose
in theory

13.2 Examples

You could take the ring.

Result:

hypothetical = true
completedAction = false
Perhaps Old Bren would pay you later.

Result:

hypothetical = true
futureIntent = true maybe
no payment success

⸻

14. Attempt detection

14.1 Markers

try to
tries to
attempt to
attempts to
reach for
start to
begin to
prepare to
move to
aim to
plan to
intend to
head toward
set out for

14.2 Examples

You try to pick up the gem.

Result:

attemptOnly = true
completedAction = false
I head toward the chapel.

Input result:

place.location_intent
attemptOnly = true
completedAction = false

⸻

15. Failure/revocation detection

15.1 Markers

fails
failed
fizzles
nothing happens
no effect
collapses
breaks apart
slips away
drops before you can take it
vanishes before you touch it
refuses
backfires
is interrupted
is stopped
is blocked
is denied
almost
pulls it back

15.2 Examples

The spell fizzles.

Result:

failedOrRevoked = true
no stable ability success
Mara almost hands you the pouch, then pulls it back.

Result:

failedOrRevoked = true
no item transfer success

⸻

16. Quoted speech detection

16.1 Quote markers

Detect spans inside:

"..."
'...'
“...”
‘...’

Also detect reported speech frames:

says
said
tells you
asks
commands
orders
reads
is written

16.2 Examples

Mara says, "Take the key."

Result:

quotedSpeech = true
actor speech/request, not item acquisition
The scroll reads, "Shadow Step bends space."

Result:

quotedSpeech = true
information/hint only, not stable ability

⸻

17. Rumor/claim detection

17.1 Markers

rumor
people say
it is said
claims
claimed
says
said
tells you
mentions
heard that
supposedly
allegedly
believed to be

17.2 Examples

People say Lord Veyr killed Dalen.

Result:

rumorOrClaim = true
truthStatus = 'rumored'
no confirmed murder fact
Mara claims she owns the sword.

Result:

rumorOrClaim = true
truthStatus = 'claimed'
no automatic custody transfer

⸻

18. Dream/vision/illusion detection

18.1 Markers

dream
vision
hallucination
memory flash
prophecy
nightmare
illusion
imagines
in your mind
symbolic

18.2 Examples

In your dream, you receive the Crown of Dawn.

Result:

dreamOrVision = true
no real inventory item
A vision shows you standing in the Ash Shrine.

Result:

dreamOrVision = true
no current location change

⸻

19. Conditional and future intent detection

19.1 Conditional markers

if
unless
when you
once you
provided that
as long as
should you

19.2 Future markers

will
shall
going to
later
tomorrow
soon
next time

19.3 Examples

Old Bren will pay you 15 gold if you clear the wolves.

Result:

conditional = true
futureIntent = true
reward promise, not payment
Mara says she will give you the sword tomorrow.

Result:

futureIntent = true
no item acquisition

⸻

20. Event family hint detection

EventScannerLite should identify broad event families without committing state.

Initial families:

item
handoff
ability
body
quest
reward
actor
place
time
progression
unknown

20.1 Family hint is not proposal ownership

A family hint only says:

This text might matter to that system.

It does not decide:

* target entity
* final event type
* validity
* state mutation

⸻

21. Item family shell

21.1 Detect possible item mentions

Item family shell may detect patterns like:

you see X
there is X
X lies nearby
X drops Y
you pick up X
you take X
you collect X
you drop X
you lose X

21.2 Output hit examples

You see a silver ring on the table.

Hit:

eventFamily = 'item'
eventType = 'item_visible_candidate'
completedAction = true
truthStatus = 'observed'
You pick up the rusty dagger.

Hit:

eventFamily = 'item'
eventType = 'item_pickup_candidate'
completedAction = true

21.3 Input hit examples

I take the rusty dagger.

Hit:

eventFamily = 'item'
eventType = 'item_pickup_attempt'
attemptOnly = true
completedAction = false

21.4 Must not

Item parser shell must not:

* add item to inventory
* move scene item to owned
* equip item
* consume item
* create currency

⸻

22. Handoff family shell

22.1 Detect possible handoff language

Patterns:

hands you
gives you
passes you
places X in your hand
slips X into your pouch
returns X to you
you accept X from
receive X from

22.2 Rejected handoff patterns should still flag safety

Examples:

Mara will give you the sword tomorrow.
Mara says, "Take the key."
Mara almost hands you the pouch.
Mara offers the ring, but you refuse.

These may produce handoff-related hits but with safety flags that prevent success later.

22.3 Must not

Handoff shell must not transfer custody.

It only records possible handoff text.

⸻

23. Ability family shell

23.1 Detect possible ability mentions

Patterns:

you cast X
you use X
X activates
X fizzles
nothing happens
ability
spell
skill
power
passive
talent

23.2 Input attempt

I try to use Shadow Step.

Hit:

eventFamily = 'ability'
eventType = 'ability_attempt_candidate'
attemptOnly = true
completedAction = false

23.3 Output success/failure candidates

Shadow Step fizzles.

Hit:

eventFamily = 'ability'
eventType = 'ability_failure_candidate'
failedOrRevoked = true
completedAction = false
Mana Slash erupts from your blade.

Hit:

eventFamily = 'ability'
eventType = 'ability_success_candidate'
completedAction = true

23.4 Must not

Ability shell must not:

* create stable abilities
* spend MP/EP
* create generated ability card
* unseal ability
* add permanent stat/trait

⸻

24. Body family shell

24.1 Detect body/status hints

Patterns:

you are hit
you are struck
you are wounded
you bleed
you are poisoned
you recover
you heal
you regain MP
you are exhausted

24.2 Must not

Body shell must not:

* change HP/MP/EP
* add conditions
* heal injuries
* kill player

It only emits body-related hits.

⸻

25. Quest/reward family shell

25.1 Quest patterns

asks you to
requests that you
job
quest
contract
objective
complete
completed
clear the wolves
deliver the letter

25.2 Reward patterns

promises X gold
offers X if
will pay
pays you
hands you your reward
gives you the promised coins

25.3 Must distinguish promise vs payment flags

Old Bren promises to pay you 15 gold.

Hit:

eventFamily = 'reward'
eventType = 'reward_promise_candidate'
futureIntent/conditional maybe true
completedAction = false for payment
Old Bren pays you 15 gold.

Hit:

eventFamily = 'reward'
eventType = 'reward_payment_candidate'
completedAction = true

Still not committed.

25.4 Must not

Quest/reward shell must not:

* activate quests
* complete objectives
* add currency
* pay rewards
* award XP

⸻

26. Actor family shell

26.1 Detect actor hints

Patterns:

named actor appears
Mara says
Captain Mora enters
Old Bren asks
A guard approaches

26.2 Generic actor rule

Generic actors like:

a guard
a stranger
a merchant
someone

may be scene-actor candidates only later.

Parser shell should not promote them.

26.3 Relation phrase caution

If text includes:

Mara's sister
Joren's son
Captain Mora's guard

parser should preserve full phrase and note relation risk.

Do not resolve as base actor in parser.

26.4 Must not

Actor shell must not:

* create persistent actors
* change presence
* confirm claims
* kill actors
* transfer item custody

⸻

27. Place/time/progression family shells

27.1 Place patterns

head toward X
set out for X
arrive at X
reach X
enter X

Input:

I head toward chapel.

Hit:

place.location_intent_candidate
completedAction = false

Output:

You arrive at the chapel.

Hit:

place.location_arrival_candidate
completedAction = true

Parser still does not move player.

27.2 Time patterns

an hour passes
minutes later
morning comes
you sleep through the night

Parser shell emits time candidates only.

It does not advance time.

27.3 Progression patterns

you gain 20 XP
Strength increases by 1
you level up

Parser shell may emit progression candidates only if explicit.

It does not award XP or levels.

27.4 Must not

These shells must not:

* change current location
* advance time
* age player
* add XP
* increase stats

⸻

28. Confidence defaults

Parser confidence is preliminary.

Suggested initial confidence:

clear output completed action = 0.70 to 0.75
player input intent = 0.45 to 0.55
quoted speech = 0.25 to 0.35
rumor = 0.20 to 0.30
hypothetical = 0.15 to 0.25
dream/vision = 0.15 to 0.25
negated success = 0.05
command/context/card/audit source = 0.00 and scan rejected

28.1 Confidence cannot override flags

A hit with strong words but bad safety flags must not become canon later.

Example:

In a dream, you receive the Crown of Dawn.

Even though the phrase says receive, dream flag blocks real acquisition.

⸻

29. Parser output caps

Recommended caps:

maxInputParserHits = 10
maxOutputParserHits = 20
maxWindows = 30
maxHitPreviewLength = 160
maxParserAuditLines = 10 per turn

If caps are exceeded:

* keep higher-confidence/high-safety hits first if cheap
* otherwise keep first hits and audit skip
* do not crash
* do not scan entire history to compensate

⸻

30. Parser audit summary

Parser audit should be compact.

Possible summary:

Parser shell scanned output: 4 windows, 2 hits, 1 skipped by command/context suppression.

Possible hit summary:

Parser hit: item_pickup_candidate from output, matched "pick up the rusty dagger", completedAction=true.

Do not store huge full text.

⸻

31. Parser and Runtime relationship

Runtime decides when parser may run.

Parser must only run after Runtime confirms:

* source is Output hook or Input intent shell
* command output suppression did not trigger
* duplicate output replay did not trigger
* text is not [AIDRPG] diagnostic output
* parser feature/shell mode allows scanning

Parser should still have its own non-canon source rejection as a backup.

⸻

32. Parser and CommandRouter relationship

CommandRouter handles commands in Input hook.

Parser must not parse command text.

Input:

/inventory

must never produce item hits.

Command output beginning with [AIDRPG] must never produce hits.

⸻

33. Parser and ProposalBuilder relationship

Parser produces ParserHits.

ProposalBuilder later converts ParserHits to EventProposals.

Forbidden path:

ParserHit -> Reducer

Allowed path:

ParserHit -> ProposalBuilder -> CanonValidator -> ReducerRegistry -> OwnerReducer

Build Chunk 9 stops at ParserHit.

⸻

34. Parser and AuditLogger relationship

Parser may call AuditLogger for summary/debug only.

Parser audit must not become gameplay source.

Parser should not over-audit every tiny hit forever.

Use caps.

⸻

35. Parser and StateManager relationship

Parser assumes StateManager already initialized state.

Parser must not repair state.

Parser must not use StateManager as a hidden mutation path.

If parser detects malformed state dependency, it should return no hits and audit warning, not repair gameplay.

⸻

36. Parser and Story Cards

Parser must not scan Story Card text.

Parser must not scan protected card text.

Parser must not scan generated card text.

Parser must not create Story Cards.

Generated cards remain disabled.

⸻

37. Early v0.1 activation level

For Core MVP v0.1 foundation, parser can be in one of two acceptable modes.

37.1 Inert shell mode

EventScannerLite exists but returns no hits.

It reports:

Parser shell present; full scanning disabled.

This is acceptable only before parser tests are needed.

37.2 Safe hit shell mode

EventScannerLite detects safety flags and a limited set of family hints, returning ParserHits.

It still does not create proposals or mutate state.

Preferred for Build Chunk 9 implementation.

⸻

38. Parser releasecheck requirements

ReleaseCheck must verify:

* parser exists or is honestly marked shell-only
* parser cannot mutate state directly
* parser source suppression exists
* command output cannot be parsed
* context/card/audit/help text cannot be parsed
* ParserHit schema includes safety flags
* input parser produces intent/attempt only
* output parser gated by Runtime duplicate/suppression checks
* parser hit caps exist

ReleaseCheck must fail if:

* parser writes inventory/quests/abilities/XP directly
* parser scans command output
* parser scans Story Card text as canon
* parser omits safety flags
* parser treats player input as completed success

ReleaseCheck may warn if:

* parser shell is present but not fully active
* family detection is limited
* resolver/proposal/validator not active yet

⸻

39. Required parser tests

BC9-001 — Parser does not mutate state

Input/output text:

You pick up the rusty dagger.

Action:

Run parser shell only.

Expected:

* ParserHit may be emitted
* inventory unchanged

Fail if:

* dagger added to inventory

⸻

BC9-002 — Command input not parsed

Input:

/inventory

Expected:

* CommandRouter handles it
* EventScannerLite not called or returns no hits

Fail if:

* parser treats /inventory as story

⸻

BC9-003 — Command output prefix suppressed

Text:

[AIDRPG] Inventory
- Rusty Dagger

Expected:

* parser rejects as diagnostic/command output
* no item hit

Fail if:

* Rusty Dagger hit emitted from command output

⸻

BC9-004 — Context text not parsed

Context text:

The Iron Sword is in Mara's custody.

Source:

context

Expected:

* scan rejected
* no item/custody hit

Fail if:

* context reminder creates hit for gameplay commit

⸻

BC9-005 — Story Card text not parsed

Card text:

Player owns Crown of Dawn.

Source:

story_card

Expected:

* scan rejected
* no Crown hit

Fail if:

* card prose creates parser hit for acquisition

⸻

BC9-006 — Input is attempt only

Input:

I take the jeweled crown.

Expected:

* item attempt hit may be emitted
* completedAction=false
* attemptOnly=true
* inventory unchanged

Fail if:

* completedAction=true from input alone or item added

⸻

BC9-007 — Output pickup candidate

Output:

You pick up the rusty dagger.

Expected:

* item_pickup_candidate hit
* completedAction=true
* no inventory mutation

Fail if:

* no hit in active safe-hit mode, or state mutates

⸻

BC9-008 — Visible item not pickup

Output:

You see a silver ring on the table.

Expected:

* item_visible_candidate hit
* not item_pickup_candidate
* inventory unchanged

Fail if:

* pickup hit or owned item created

⸻

BC9-009 — Available loot not pickup

Output:

The goblin drops a rusty dagger.

Expected:

* item_available_loot_candidate hit
* not owned inventory

Fail if:

* item pickup success emitted/committed

⸻

BC9-010 — Negated pickup flagged

Output:

You do not take the sword.

Expected:

* item-related hit may exist
* negated=true
* completedAction=false for pickup success

Fail if:

* successful pickup candidate without negation

⸻

BC9-011 — Hypothetical flagged

Output:

You could take the jeweled cup if you wanted.

Expected:

* hypothetical=true
* completedAction=false

Fail if:

* successful pickup candidate

⸻

BC9-012 — Dream item flagged

Output:

In a dream, you receive the Crown of Dawn.

Expected:

* dreamOrVision=true
* no real pickup success

Fail if:

* acquisition candidate lacks dream flag

⸻

BC9-013 — Quoted handoff flagged

Output:

Mara says, "Take the iron key."

Expected:

* quotedSpeech=true
* actor speech/request hit possible
* no item handoff success

Fail if:

* item acquisition success hit

⸻

BC9-014 — Future handoff flagged

Output:

Mara says she will give you the sword tomorrow.

Expected:

* futureIntent=true
* no current handoff success

Fail if:

* current transfer hit without future flag

⸻

BC9-015 — Failed ability flagged

Output:

You attempt Shadow Step, but nothing happens.

Expected:

* ability hit
* attemptOnly=true or failedOrRevoked=true
* no stable ability creation

Fail if:

* ability success/stable implication

⸻

BC9-016 — Rumor flagged

Output:

People say Lord Veyr killed Dalen.

Expected:

* actor/claim hit possible
* rumorOrClaim=true
* truthStatus rumored/claimed

Fail if:

* confirmed fact hit

⸻

BC9-017 — Travel intent not arrival

Input:

I head toward the chapel.

Expected:

* place intent hit
* completedAction=false

Fail if:

* arrival candidate from input alone

⸻

BC9-018 — Confirmed arrival candidate

Output:

After a short walk, you arrive at the chapel.

Expected:

* place arrival candidate
* completedAction=true
* no state mutation

Fail if:

* current location changed by parser

⸻

BC9-019 — Explicit XP candidate only

Output:

You gain 20 XP.

Expected:

* progression XP candidate hit if safe-hit mode active
* no XP added

Fail if:

* XP mutates progression directly

⸻

BC9-020 — Vague experience not XP

Output:

This was a learning experience.

Expected:

* no XP candidate or low-confidence non-XP note

Fail if:

* XP gain candidate emitted

⸻

BC9-021 — Hit cap enforced

Output contains many repeated candidate phrases.

Expected:

* hits capped at maxOutputParserHits
* audit skip if needed
* no crash

Fail if:

* unbounded hits

⸻

BC9-022 — Parser does not scan full history

Given long campaign history, parser receives current output only.

Expected:

* scans current text/window only
* no full-history parse

Fail if:

* parser loops over entire history every output

⸻

BC9-023 — ReleaseCheck catches parser mutation risk

Test unsafe parser direct-write flag/path.

Expected:

* releasecheck FAIL

Fail if:

* releasecheck passes unsafe parser

⸻

BC9-024 — Parser shell honesty

If parser is inert shell mode:

Expected:

* /debuglast or /releasecheck says parser shell inactive/limited
* no false claim of full parser

Fail if:

* system claims full parsing active while returning no hits

⸻

40. Parser acceptance checklist

Build Chunk 9 is complete when implementation will clearly support:

* source authority checks
* command output rejection
* context/card/audit/help text rejection
* ParserHit schema
* safety flags on every hit
* safe normalization
* window splitting
* hit caps
* input intent-only scanning
* output candidate scanning
* item/handoff/ability/body/quest/reward/actor/place/time/progression family shells
* no direct state mutation
* audit summary
* releasecheck parser safety checks
* tests BC9-001 through BC9-024

⸻

Build Chunk 9 depends on:

* Build Chunk 1 for MVP scope and deferred systems

* Build Chunk 4 for command output non-canon formats
* Build Chunk 5 for StateManager-first initialization
