30. Final Build Chunk 11 doctrine

CanonValidator is the final gate before truth.

It must be strict, explainable, and family-aware.

Final rule:

No unsafe source becomes canon.
No unsafe clause becomes canon.
No ambiguous target becomes durable truth without validation.
No duplicate effect repeats.
No reducer receives anything except accepted validated results.

With CanonValidator planned, the next safe step is Resolver and owner-specific sanitizers.

====================================================================================================
20_Build_Chunks_01_to_17/12_AIDRPG_Build_Chunk_12_Resolver_and_Owner_Specific_Sanitizer_Plan.txt
====================================================================================================

AIDRPG Build Chunk 12 — Resolver and Owner-Specific Sanitizer Plan

0. Document status

Document name: AIDRPG Build Chunk 12 — Resolver and Owner-Specific Sanitizer Plan
Build target: AIDRPG Core MVP v0.1 / final-core architecture path
Purpose: Define the exact resolver, target-binding, name-cleanup, head-noun classification, compound suppression, card trigger hygiene, and owner-specific sanitizer rules before ReducerRegistry and owner reducers are planned.
Status: Practical build document.
Follows: Final Build Control Map v1, Build Chunk 10, and Build Chunk 11.
This document does not contain final script code.

This chunk exists because previous versions repeatedly used broad cleanup patches that fixed one system and damaged another.

The final build must not use one aggressive shared cleaner for every kind of target.

Ability names, item names, place names, actor names, quest objectives, card titles, and card triggers have different rules.

The correct solution is:

shared cleanup stays conservative
owner-specific sanitizers do the aggressive work
resolvers bind cleaned names to canonical targets
audits explain what was changed or rejected
validators decide canon after resolver output

⸻

1. Resolver doctrine

The doctrine is:

Clean locally.
Resolve conservatively.
Never let a sanitizer create canon.
Never let one family's cleanup damage another family.
Prefer explicit owner-specific rules over broad text surgery.

Resolvers and sanitizers prepare target identity.

They do not commit gameplay state.

They do not decide canon.

They do not bypass CanonValidator.

⸻

2. Why this chunk matters

Old versions already proved the danger.

A fix for ability names stripped phrases like:

Ember Ward to protect allies
Frost Step by moving across slick stones
Stoneguard until my stance holds under impact

That cleanup was useful for abilities.

But if the same aggressive cleanup lives in a shared helper, it risks damaging:

I travel to the smithy
I inspect the old wooden door
I use the iron key on the cellar door
After the road, you enter The Drunken Huntsman
Mara refuses the crown but hands you the potion

So the final build must have separate sanitizer ownership.

⸻

3. Placement in final script

Resolver and sanitizer logic belongs after utilities and before/facing ProposalBuilder and CanonValidator.

Recommended section placement:

Section 12.5 — Resolver and Owner-Specific Sanitizers

or within the planned parser/proposal area as:

ResolverAndSanitizer

It is used by:

EventScannerLite
ProposalBuilder
CanonValidator read checks
Command renderers for display helpers only
CardSyncLite for card title/trigger hygiene
ReleaseCheck for resolver gate tests

It must not call:

InventoryReducer
AbilityReducer
QuestReducer
BodyReducer
PlaceReducer
TimeReducer
ActorReducer
CardSync live writes
ContextBuilder mutation

⸻

4. Resolver responsibilities

Resolver layer owns:

* conservative shared text normalization
* owner-specific name sanitization
* canonical key generation
* display name generation
* target family identification
* compound actor suppression
* route/interstitial place handling
* head-noun classification
* card title and trigger hygiene
* confidence scoring for target binding
* resolver notes for audits
* ambiguity marking
* known-target matching when state contains existing records

Resolver layer does not own:

* canon approval
* gameplay mutation
* item ownership
* ability stability
* reward payment
* quest activation
* actor persistence
* place movement
* time advancement
* Story Card writes

⸻

5. Required resolver public functions

The final script should provide these functions or equivalents.

Resolver.normalizeShared(text)
Resolver.makeCanonicalKey(family, displayName)
Resolver.makeDisplayName(cleanedText)
Resolver.resolveTarget(family, rawText, context, root)
Resolver.resolveItem(rawText, context, root)
Resolver.resolveAbility(rawText, context, root)
Resolver.resolveActor(rawText, context, root)
Resolver.resolvePlace(rawText, context, root)
Resolver.resolveQuest(rawText, context, root)
Resolver.resolveReward(rawText, context, root)
Resolver.resolveCard(rawText, context, root)
Resolver.classifyHeadNoun(rawText, family)
Resolver.detectAmbiguity(rawText, family, context)
Resolver.scoreTarget(rawText, cleanedText, family, context)
Resolver.buildTargetBinding(family, rawText, context, root)
Resolver.auditResolution(root, result)

Names may change in code, but these responsibilities must exist.

⸻

6. Required sanitizer public functions

The final script should provide owner-specific sanitizer functions or equivalents.

SharedHintCleaner.clean(text)
AbilityNameSanitizer.clean(rawText, context)
ItemNameSanitizer.clean(rawText, context)
PlaceNameSanitizer.clean(rawText, context)
ActorNameSanitizer.clean(rawText, context)
QuestObjectiveSanitizer.clean(rawText, context)
RewardNameSanitizer.clean(rawText, context)
CardNameSanitizer.cleanTitle(rawText, context)
CardTriggerSanitizer.cleanTriggers(rawTriggers, context)

The exact function names can differ, but the ownership separation must exist.

⸻

7. SharedHintCleaner rules

SharedHintCleaner must stay conservative.

Allowed shared cleanup:

safe string conversion
trim whitespace
collapse repeated whitespace
normalize curly quotes for matching
remove obvious leading/trailing punctuation
strip control characters
limit length

Forbidden shared cleanup:

strip after to/by/from/for/until/across globally
remove prepositional phrases globally
remove actor titles globally
remove route words globally
remove item custody phrases globally
remove card trigger phrases globally
assume ability syntax globally

7.1 Reason

A phrase like:

Frost Step by moving across slick stones

should become an ability name Frost Step only in AbilityNameSanitizer.

But:

I travel across the bridge

must not become I travel because a shared cleaner stripped across the bridge.

⸻

8. Canonical key rules

Canonical keys must be stable and family-scoped.

Recommended format:

<family>:<normalized_name>

Examples:

ability:ember_ward
item:red_healing_potion
actor:veteran_guard
place:the_drunken_huntsman
quest:repair_north_wall
card:protected_player_sheet

8.1 Key normalization

Normalize by:

lowercase
trim
collapse whitespace to underscores
remove unsafe punctuation
preserve meaningful alphanumeric words
limit length
hash only overflow/detail when needed

8.2 Key safety

Keys must not contain:

full raw output paragraphs
quotes/dream text
entire command output
unstable timestamps only
internal object dumps

⸻

9. TargetBinding output standard

Resolver output must support the TargetBinding schema from Build Chunk 10.

TargetBinding {
  targetFamily,
  rawTargetText,
  cleanedTargetText,
  canonicalKey,
  displayName,
  targetId,
  targetStatus,
  targetConfidence,
  sanitizerUsed,
  resolverNotes
}

If target cannot be safely resolved, use:

targetStatus = 'ambiguous' | 'generic' | 'reference_only' | 'unknown'

Do not invent durable identity when target is unsafe.

⸻

10. AbilityNameSanitizer rules

Ability names need the most aggressive phrase cleanup, but only inside ability context.

10.1 Ability context examples

Ability context exists when local clause includes:

train/practice/drill/refine/work on/meditate on/spar with
use/cast/channel/activate/manifest
spell/skill/technique/power/ability/talent/passive
learn/unlock/master/stabilize

10.2 Ability cleanup may strip purpose/training suffixes

Strip suffixes after the ability name when they are clearly purpose or method phrases:

to protect allies
from heat
by moving across slick stones
until my stance holds
under impact
against shield-cracking blows
for defense
with careful crescent cuts
through repeated practice

10.3 Ability cleanup must preserve real ability names

Do not destroy names that naturally include common words.

Examples:

Shield Against Dawn
Step Across Stars
Song for the Dead
Blade of the North
Ward from Below

If unsure, lower confidence and keep more text rather than over-strip.

10.4 Ability name examples

I train Ember Ward to protect allies from heat -> Ember Ward
I practice Frost Step by moving across slick stones -> Frost Step
I drill Stoneguard until my stance holds under impact -> Stoneguard
I train Moonblade by practicing careful crescent cuts -> Moonblade
I refine Crimson Lotus Fang through repeated strikes -> Crimson Lotus Fang

10.5 Non-ability input must not create ability names

I travel to the smithy -> no smithy ability
I inspect the old wooden door -> no door ability
I use the iron key on the cellar door -> no key/door ability
I ask Mara about Moonblade -> possible ability mention/hint only, not stable/training unless training context exists

⸻

11. ItemNameSanitizer rules

Item names must preserve concrete object identity while removing action/custody/failure fragments.

11.1 Item cleanup may strip

leading articles: a, an, the
quantity text when separately stored
trailing failure clauses
custody/action fragments
source actor phrases when separately stored
location phrases when separately stored

Examples:

the rusted iron key, but the goblin snatches it away -> rusted iron key
red healing potion from Mira -> red healing potion
three silver arrows on the table -> silver arrows, quantity 3
jeweled crown that Mira refuses to give you -> jeweled crown

11.2 Item cleanup must not strip meaningful item modifiers

Preserve:

rusted
iron
black gate
red healing
jeweled
silver
old wooden
cellar
masterwork
cracked

11.3 Abstract item detection

Flag abstract/metaphorical item phrases:

idea of a brass key
memory of a sword
title of Crown of Dawn
concept of a lockpick
promise of gold
symbol of a blade

These may produce references but not acquired items.

11.4 Item ownership is not resolver’s job

ItemNameSanitizer can clean rusted iron key.

It cannot decide the player owns it.

CanonValidator and InventoryReducer decide acquisition.

⸻

12. PlaceNameSanitizer rules

Place names must distinguish current location, named destination, route/interstitial, visible/nearby references, and generic places.

12.1 Strong named places

Preserve named place titles:

The Drunken Huntsman
The Silver Hart
Ash Shrine
North Wall
Old Mill
Market Lane

12.2 Route/interstitial words

Route/interstitial words should not outrank strong named arrivals.

Examples:

road
path
route
street
hallway
corridor
trail
bridge
lane as route segment

In:

After the road, you enter The Drunken Huntsman.

resolve target place as:

The Drunken Huntsman

not:

road

12.3 Weak reference markers

Flag weak references:

visible from
nearby
down the street
beyond
in the distance
road to
path toward
consider going
might head to

These should not resolve as current-scene arrival.

12.4 Current scene confirmation markers

Recognize current scene confirmation:

remains
still in
around you
you stand in
the market lane remains busy
the square buzzes around you

12.5 Generic place caution

Generic rooms/doors/roads should not become durable major places by default.

Examples:

a room
the street
a road
a door
the hallway

Mark as generic/scene-only unless named or important.

⸻

13. ActorNameSanitizer rules

Actor names must preserve meaningful titles and compound roles while suppressing duplicate generic heads.

13.1 Actor title preservation

Preserve titles and role modifiers:

Veteran Guard
High Priestess
Orc Warrior
City Captain
Old Bren
Captain Mora
Lord Veyr
Blacksmith Tomas

13.2 Compound-shorter suppression

If one window contains:

Veteran Guard

then do not also produce:

Guard

for the same span.

Same for:

High Priestess -> not Priestess separately
Orc Warrior -> not Warrior separately
City Captain -> not Captain separately if same span

13.3 Generic actor heads

Recognize common actor heads but mark generic actors appropriately:

guard
blacksmith
farmer
merchant
skeleton
zombie
troll
ogre
captain
general
acolyte
priestess
high priestess
wolf
goblin
orc warrior
golem

Generic actors become scene-only candidates unless named, repeated, important, or validated.

13.4 Reference-only actor markers

Flag reference-only or absent actors:

not here
definitely not here
elsewhere
absent
mentioned
named after
training dummy named after Mira
rumor of
portrait of
statue of

Reference-only actors must not become present actors.

⸻

14. QuestObjectiveSanitizer rules

Quest objectives need broad action support without over-confirming completion.

14.1 Objective verbs

Recognize and preserve objective verbs:

recover
find
repair
escort
clear
deliver
investigate
protect
bring
retrieve
rescue
defeat
survey
track
report to
speak with

14.2 Quest title cleanup

Quest titles should be readable and stable.

Examples:

repair the north wall -> Repair the North Wall
escort Bram to the chapel -> Escort Bram to the Chapel
investigate the burned tavern -> Investigate the Burned Tavern
clear the mill wolves -> Clear the Mill Wolves

14.3 Quest acceptance not implied by mention

Sanitizer can clean an objective.

It cannot activate the quest.

CanonValidator decides offer/acceptance/active status.

⸻

15. RewardNameSanitizer rules

Reward names must separate amount, currency, item reward, promise, and payment.

15.1 Currency parsing

Recognize:

15 gold
fifteen gold
3 silver
one hundred copper

But do not pay it.

15.2 Promise/payment markers

Flag promise markers:

promises to pay
will pay
offers if
reward will be

Flag payment markers:

pays you
hands you the promised coins
gives you 15 gold
places the reward in your hand

15.3 Reward sanitizer output

The sanitizer should output:

reward description
amount if clear
currency type if clear
reward item if clear
promise/payment marker

CanonValidator decides payment.

⸻

16. CardNameSanitizer rules

Card names and triggers are dangerous because broad triggers and dirty card names can activate constantly.

16.1 Protected card titles

Protected card titles should be stable:

AIDRPG: Player Sheet
AIDRPG: Body Status
AIDRPG: Abilities
AIDRPG: Inventory & Equipment
AIDRPG: Quest Log
AIDRPG: Social Memory
AIDRPG: Current Location

Internal keys:

protected:player_sheet
protected:body_status
protected:abilities
protected:inventory_equipment
protected:quest_log
protected:social_memory
protected:current_location

16.2 Generated card names

Generated cards are disabled in MVP.

If later enabled, generated card titles must be family-scoped:

AIDRPG Actor: Mara
AIDRPG Place: Old Mill
AIDRPG Item: Crown of Dawn
AIDRPG Quest: Clear the Mill Wolves

16.3 Trigger hygiene

Reject broad/common triggers such as:

street
road
town
village
forest
market
shop
sword
key
guard
man
woman
he
she
they

unless protected card context requires controlled trigger behavior.

Prefer specific triggers:

mara thorn
old bren
crown of dawn
ash shrine
clear the mill wolves

16.4 Connector pollution

Strip connector pollution from card/actor targets:

AND_MIRA -> MIRA
ACTOR:AND_MIRA -> ACTOR:MIRA

But do not over-strip legitimate names containing and.

⸻

17. Head noun classification

Head noun classification is required for places, actors, items, and world structure later.

17.1 Place/world examples

village well -> object/facility, not settlement
town gate -> gate/facility, not settlement
city wall -> wall/structure, not settlement
north wall -> wall/structure/project target, not settlement
Old Mill -> place/building
Ash Shrine -> place/shrine

17.2 Actor examples

veteran guard -> actor role guard with modifier veteran
blacksmith -> actor role/profession
skeleton -> undead actor/creature
ogre -> creature/enemy actor
high priestess -> religious actor/title

17.3 Item examples

iron key -> item key
healing potion -> consumable
jeweled crown -> item/treasure
old wooden door -> object/environment, not inventory unless detached/acquired

⸻

18. Ambiguity rules

Resolver must mark ambiguous targets instead of guessing.

Ambiguity cases:

multiple known actors named Mara
key without identifying which key when several exist
the guard when multiple guards present
the chapel when several chapels known
it / him / her / that without stable referent

Ambiguous proposals should go to CanonValidator as pending or rejected unless family allows weak reference.

Do not create durable new canon from ambiguous targets.

⸻

19. Existing state matching

Resolvers may read existing state to match known targets.

Examples:

Moonblade already exists as candidate ability -> bind to existing ability:moonblade
Mara exists as actor -> bind to actor:mara if local text clearly refers to Mara
Old Mill current location -> bind place:old_mill

Read-only matching is allowed.

Mutation is forbidden.

If match confidence is low, mark ambiguous.

⸻

20. Resolver confidence scoring

Recommended confidence bands:

0.90+ exact known target match with clear family
0.75 direct named target with clean family context
0.60 plausible new candidate with clear local clause
0.40 generic or weakly referenced target
0.20 quoted/dream/rumor/reference-only target
0.00 unsafe non-canon source

Confidence does not override safety flags.

A dream target with a clean name is still dream/non-canon for gameplay acquisition.

⸻

21. Resolver audit format

Resolver audit should be compact.

Examples:

Resolved ability target: raw="Ember Ward to protect allies from heat" clean="Ember Ward" sanitizer=AbilityNameSanitizer confidence=0.82.
Suppressed actor duplicate: kept "Veteran Guard", suppressed contained "Guard" in same window.
Place resolver: "road" marked route/interstitial; arrival target "The Drunken Huntsman" selected.
Card trigger rejected: "street" is too broad for generated card trigger.

Do not dump full raw output.

⸻

22. Resolver and ProposalBuilder relationship

ProposalBuilder uses Resolver to build TargetBinding.

The flow is:

ParserHit
-> ProposalBuilder identifies family/event type
-> Resolver.buildTargetBinding(family, raw target, local context, root)
-> EventProposal carries TargetBinding
-> CanonValidator validates target and safety

Resolver output is evidence, not canon.

⸻

23. Resolver and CanonValidator relationship

CanonValidator reads resolver output to decide if target is safe enough.

CanonValidator may reject/pending if:

targetStatus ambiguous
targetStatus generic when durable identity required
targetStatus reference_only when presence required
sanitizerUsed wrong family
targetConfidence too low
resolverNotes contain blocked marker

Resolver does not make the final canon decision.

⸻

24. Resolver and CardSync relationship

CardSyncLite uses CardNameSanitizer and CardTriggerSanitizer.

Rules:

protected cards have stable titles/keys
generated cards disabled in MVP
broad triggers rejected
manual card title edits do not become canon
card text never goes through gameplay resolver as source truth

CardSync may use resolver for display/trigger hygiene only, not gameplay import.

⸻

25. Resolver and ReleaseCheck relationship

ReleaseCheck must verify:

shared cleaner is conservative
owner-specific sanitizers exist or are honestly shell-only
ability cleanup is not global
item cleanup does not create acquisition
place resolver separates weak references and strong arrivals
actor resolver suppresses compound duplicates
card trigger sanitizer rejects broad triggers
target binding includes sanitizerUsed and confidence
ambiguous targets are represented, not guessed

⸻

26. Required resolver tests

BC12-001 — Shared cleaner conservative

Input:

I travel to the smithy.

Expected:

SharedHintCleaner does not strip to "I travel"
no ability target created

⸻

Input:

Expected:

clean ability name = Ember Ward
sanitizerUsed = AbilityNameSanitizer

⸻

Input:

I practice Frost Step by moving across slick stones.

Expected:

clean ability name = Frost Step

⸻

Input:

I drill Stoneguard until my stance holds under impact.

Expected:

clean ability name = Stoneguard

⸻

Input:

I train Shield Against Dawn.

Expected:

clean ability name = Shield Against Dawn
not Shield

⸻

Input:

Expected:

no ability target

⸻

item target iron key

item target rusted iron key

separate item targets jeweled crown and red healing potion

place target The Drunken Huntsman

Output:

The market lane remains busy with merchants.

market lane target with current-scene confirmation marker

A veteran guard stands near the gate.

one actor target Veteran Guard
no separate Guard target for same span

⸻

The high priestess enters the shrine.

A guard watches the gate.

guard targetStatus generic or scene-only candidate
not persistent named actor by default

Mira target reference_only

⸻

Output:

The goblin is definitely not here.

goblin marked absent/reference_only

quest objective Repair the North Wall

quest objective Escort Bram to the Chapel

Output:

Old Bren promises to pay you 15 gold.

reward target 15 gold
promise marker true

Trigger candidate:

street

rejected too broad

ACTOR:MIRA

with connector cleanup note.

The village well sits beside the square.

village well classified facility/object, not settlement

⸻

State has multiple known keys.

State has ability candidate Moonblade.

27. ReleaseCheck requirements for Build Chunk 12

ReleaseCheck must eventually verify:

SharedHintCleaner exists and is conservative
AbilityNameSanitizer exists
ItemNameSanitizer exists
PlaceNameSanitizer exists
ActorNameSanitizer exists
QuestObjectiveSanitizer exists
CardNameSanitizer / CardTriggerSanitizer exists or generated cards disabled
TargetBinding includes sanitizerUsed
TargetBinding includes confidence
compound actor suppression exists
route/interstitial place handling exists
weak place reference handling exists
ability suffix cleanup is owner-specific
broad card trigger rejection exists
ambiguous targets do not become durable canon by default

ReleaseCheck must fail if:

aggressive ability cleanup lives in shared cleaner
travel/inspect/use-object text can create junk abilities
compound actor shorter duplicate can persist as separate actor for same span
weak place reference can overwrite current scene
broad generated card triggers can be accepted while generated cards are enabled
ambiguous target can become durable truth without validation

⸻

28. Relationship to Final Build Control Map v1

This chunk directly implements Control Map requirements:

F001 broad helper patch broke unrelated systems
F002 explicit training intent lost to defensive words
F006 inventory false-grant prevention through local target cleanup
F008 named location arrival priority over road/interstitial
F012 compound actor duplicate suppression
F013 actor presence/reference-only safety support
F018 WorldStructure head-noun failures as deferred tests
F019 CardSync pollution, duplicate cards, broad triggers
N002 owner-specific sanitizer family
N003 local target binding

⸻

29. What Build Chunk 13 should do next

Next build document:

Build Chunk 13 — ReducerRegistry and Owner Reducer Contract Plan

Build Chunk 13 must define:

* reducer routing
* accepted validation result handoff
* owner reducer boundaries
* one owner per truth family
* no reducer for rejected/pending/deferred proposals
* operation key commit ordering
* dirty target creation
* reducer audit requirements
* reducer non-overlap rules
* tests proving parser/proposal/validator cannot mutate without reducers

⸻

30. Final Build Chunk 12 doctrine

The final build must never again rely on vague cleanup phrases like “clean names” or “sanitize cards.”

Every cleanup must belong to a specific owner.

Final rule:

Shared cleanup is conservative.
Ability cleanup belongs to abilities.
Item cleanup belongs to items.
Place cleanup belongs to places.
Actor cleanup belongs to actors.
Quest cleanup belongs to quests.
Card cleanup belongs to cards.
Resolvers bind targets but do not create truth.
Validators decide canon.
Reducers commit state.

With Resolver and owner-specific sanitizers planned, the next safe step is the ReducerRegistry and owner reducer contract.

====================================================================================================
20_Build_Chunks_01_to_17/13_AIDRPG_Build_Chunk_13_ReducerRegistry_and_Owner_Reducer_Contract_Plan.txt
====================================================================================================

AIDRPG Build Chunk 13 — ReducerRegistry and Owner Reducer Contract Plan

0. Document status

Document name: AIDRPG Build Chunk 13 — ReducerRegistry and Owner Reducer Contract Plan
Build target: AIDRPG Core MVP v0.1 / final-core architecture path
Purpose: Define the exact ReducerRegistry behavior, owner reducer contracts, accepted-result handoff, operation key ordering, dirty target creation, audit requirements, and reducer safety tests before individual reducer plans are written.
Status: Practical build document.
Follows: Build Chunk 11 — CanonValidator and Build Chunk 12 — Resolver and Owner-Specific Sanitizer Plan.
This document does not contain final script code.

This chunk exists because old versions repeatedly failed when multiple systems could mutate the same truth, when parser patches mutated state directly, or when late repair layers tried to fix already-corrupted state.

The reducer layer is the first layer allowed to commit gameplay state.

Only accepted CanonValidator results may reach reducers.

Each truth family must have exactly one reducer owner.

⸻

1. Reducer doctrine

The reducer doctrine is:

Parser detects.
ProposalBuilder structures.
CanonValidator decides eligibility.
ReducerRegistry routes.
OwnerReducers commit.
OperationLedger dedupes.
DirtyQueue marks mirrors.
AuditLogger explains.

Reducers are not parsers.

Reducers are not validators.

Reducers are not command renderers.

Reducers are not card writers.

Reducers only commit accepted, validated, owner-routed changes.

⸻

2. Why this chunk matters

Previous versions fell into patch loops because different layers could mutate truth:

parser directly added items
ability cleanup created abilities
command output was parsed as story
context reminders became state
Story Cards became canon
late patches repaired old bugs by overwriting state
multiple systems could update actors, scenes, cards, or abilities

This build stops that by enforcing reducer ownership.

If the InventoryReducer owns inventory, no other module directly adds items.

If the AbilityReducer owns abilities, no parser/sanitizer/card/context layer creates stable abilities.

If the PlaceReducer owns current location, weak place mentions cannot update state outside that path.

⸻

3. Placement in final script

ReducerRegistry belongs in:

Section 15 — Reducer Registry Shell

Owner reducers belong in:

Section 16 — MVP Reducer Shells

ReducerRegistry follows:

EventScannerLite
ProposalBuilder
CanonValidator
Resolver/Sanitizer
OperationLedger
AuditLogger

ReducerRegistry comes before:

ContextBuilderLite
CardSyncLite
CleanupManagerLite
Hook entry point final orchestration

⸻

4. ReducerRegistry responsibilities

ReducerRegistry owns:

* receiving accepted validation results
* rejecting non-accepted results from commit path
* mapping family/eventType to owner reducer
* enforcing one-owner-per-truth-family
* invoking owner reducer apply functions
* handling reducer result objects
* coordinating operation key commit order policy
* coordinating dirty target creation through owner reducer result
* collecting reducer audit summaries
* fail-softing unknown reducer routes
* preventing reducer overlap

ReducerRegistry does not own:

* parsing text
* proposal construction
* canon validation
* target sanitization
* command rendering
* Story Card writing
* context generation
* cleanup deletion
* feature activation

⸻

5. Owner reducer responsibilities

Owner reducers own actual state mutation for their truth family.

Each owner reducer must:

* accept only accepted ValidationResults
* verify the required owner/family before commit
* check the operation key if repeat-sensitive
* mutate only its owned state bucket(s)
* preserve valid existing data
* create dirty targets for mirror/context updates
* record concise audit lines
* return a structured ReducerResult
* fail softly without corrupting unrelated state

Owner reducers must not:

* parse raw story text
* import Story Card prose
* read command output as story
* validate canon from scratch outside CanonValidator
* mutate another reducer’s bucket except through approved cross-bucket contract
* enable deferred systems
* create generated cards

⸻

6. Required ReducerRegistry functions

The final script should provide these functions or equivalents.

ReducerRegistry.applyAcceptedBatch(root, validationBatch)
ReducerRegistry.applyOne(root, validationResult)
ReducerRegistry.getOwner(validationResult)
ReducerRegistry.isAcceptedForCommit(validationResult)
ReducerRegistry.rejectNonAccepted(validationResult)
ReducerRegistry.routeToOwner(root, validationResult, ownerName)
ReducerRegistry.handleUnknownOwner(root, validationResult)
ReducerRegistry.collectReducerResult(root, reducerResult)
ReducerRegistry.auditBatch(root, reducerResults)
ReducerRegistry.failSoftReducer(root, validationResult, error)
ReducerRegistry.getRegistryMap()
ReducerRegistry.validateRegistryIntegrity(root)

Names may change in code, but these responsibilities must exist.

⸻

7. Required owner reducer functions

Each owner reducer should follow a shared function pattern.

Reducer.apply(root, validationResult)
Reducer.canHandle(validationResult)
Reducer.buildOperationKey(validationResult, root)
Reducer.checkDuplicate(root, validationResult, operationKey)
Reducer.commit(root, validationResult, operationKey)
Reducer.markDirty(root, validationResult, commitSummary)
Reducer.audit(root, validationResult, reducerResult)
Reducer.failSoft(root, validationResult, error)

Some reducers may be shell-only in early MVP, but they must honestly return deferred/shell results rather than pretending to commit.

⸻

8. ReducerResult schema

Every reducer returns a structured result.

ReducerResult {
  validationId: string,
  proposalId: string,
  family: string,
  eventType: string,
  ownerReducer: string,
  status: 'committed' | 'skipped_duplicate' | 'rejected_wrong_owner' | 'deferred' | 'no_op' | 'failed_softly',
  operationKey: string,
  operationKeyAdded: boolean,
  mutatedBuckets: string[],
  dirtyTargets: DirtyTarget[],
  auditLine: string,
  warnings: string[],
  errors: string[],
  notes: string[]
}

Reducers should never return raw undefined/null.

⸻

9. Accepted-result handoff contract

ReducerRegistry may process only:

ValidationResult.status = accepted
ValidationResult.canonAllowed = true
ValidationResult.reducerAllowed = true
ValidationResult.requiredReducer is known

Everything else must be excluded from the commit path:

rejected
pending
deferred
blocked
malformed
duplicate_skipped

These may be audited or displayed, but not committed.

⸻

10. Owner map

The reducer registry must use a clear owner map.

inventory -> InventoryReducer
ability -> AbilityReducer
body_status -> BodyReducer
body_injury -> BodyReducer
quest -> QuestReducerLite
reward -> QuestReducerLite / RewardReducerLite if separated later
actor -> ActorReducerLite
place_scene -> PlaceReducerLite
place -> PlaceReducerLite
time -> TimeReducerLite
progression -> ProgressionReducerLite
card_mirror -> CardSyncLite, not gameplay reducer
migration -> MigrationManager, not gameplay reducer
unknown -> no reducer

MVP may keep some reducers as shells, but the owner mapping must be explicit.

⸻

11. One-owner-per-truth-family rule

Each state truth family has one primary owner.

Truth family	State bucket	Owner reducer
player identity	player	PlayerReducer later / StateManager startup only in MVP
body resources/status/injury	body	BodyReducer
XP/level/stats	progression	ProgressionReducerLite
inventory/currency/equipment/scene items	inventory	InventoryReducer
abilities/evidence/status	abilities	AbilityReducer
quests/objectives/rewards	quests	QuestReducerLite
actors/memory/presence	actors	ActorReducerLite
places/current scene/travel intent	places	PlaceReducerLite
time/deadlines	time	TimeReducerLite
Story Card registry/mirrors	cards	CardSyncLite, not gameplay canon
migration/quarantine	migration/quarantine	MigrationManager / QuarantineManager
dirty queue	dirty	DirtyQueue helper via owner reducer results

No other module may directly mutate these buckets for gameplay truth.

⸻

12. Cross-bucket mutation policy

Some accepted events affect more than one bucket.

Examples:

reward payment -> quests + inventory currency
ability training -> abilities + maybe progression/statXP later
arrival -> places + scene actors/items cleanup later
quest completion -> quests + progression XP/reward readiness later
body recovery -> body + time/rest notes later

To avoid overlapping reducers, cross-bucket changes require explicit policy.

12.1 MVP preferred policy

For MVP, keep cross-bucket changes conservative.

A reducer may mutate only its primary bucket and return suggested follow-up dirty targets or follow-up proposals unless a cross-bucket contract exists.

12.2 Allowed MVP cross-bucket contracts

Allowed only when explicitly defined:

QuestReward accepted payment may call Inventory currency helper through RewardReducer contract, or be split into reward payment validation + inventory currency event.
PlaceReducer may update currentScene visibleItems/presentActors references but not actor permanent records unless ActorReducer validates.
BodyReducer may update body resources/status only, not progression.
AbilityReducer may record ability evidence only, not stat gain unless ProgressionReducer handles it.

12.3 Forbidden casual cross-bucket mutation

Forbidden:

InventoryReducer completing quests
AbilityReducer awarding XP
PlaceReducer creating persistent actors freely
ActorReducer changing current location
CardSync changing inventory
ContextBuilder changing anything
Cleanup resolving active quests

⸻

13. Operation key commit ordering

Repeat-sensitive reducer commits must use OperationLedger.

13.1 Standard commit order

Recommended MVP order:

1. Receive accepted ValidationResult.
2. Build or read operation key.
3. Check duplicate key.
4. If duplicate, return skipped_duplicate; do not mutate.
5. Apply state mutation.
6. Add operation key to correct ledger bucket.
7. Mark dirty targets.
8. Audit success.

13.2 If mutation succeeds but key add fails

This is a critical warning.

Reducer should:

* record warning/error
* avoid retry loops
* return committed with operationKeyAdded false and critical audit
* ReleaseCheck should warn/fail depending severity

13.3 If key is added before mutation

Avoid this in MVP unless rollback exists.

If key added first and mutation fails, effect may be permanently skipped.

MVP should generally mutate then add key, while keeping mutation simple and local.

13.4 Duplicate-sensitive key buckets

item pickup/transfer/consume/drop -> recent or permanent depending item
reward payment -> rewardKeys
XP/level/stat gains -> rewardKeys or permanent/progression keys
ability stabilization -> permanentKeys or ability-specific durable key
ability use evidence -> recentKeys unless durable evidence
card writes -> cardKeys
time/age advance -> permanent or recent depending event
startup import -> permanentKeys/startupOperationKeys
migration import/quarantine -> migrationOperationKeys/permanentKeys

⸻

14. Dirty target contract

Reducers do not write Story Cards or context directly.

They mark dirty targets.

Dirty target families:

player_sheet
body_status
abilities
inventory_equipment
quest_log
social_memory
current_location
context
card_registry
migration
audit

14.1 Required dirty mapping

Mutated bucket	Dirty families
player	player_sheet, context
body	body_status, context
progression	player_sheet, context
inventory	inventory_equipment, current_location, context
abilities	abilities, context
quests	quest_log, context
actors	social_memory, current_location, context
places	current_location, context
time	current_location, quest_log, context
cards	card_registry, audit
migration/quarantine	migration, audit

Dirty targets do not create gameplay truth.

⸻

15. Audit contract

Every reducer commit or skip must produce a concise audit line.

Examples:

Inventory committed: acquired red_healing_potion from Mara handoff.
Inventory skipped duplicate: item.acquire.red_healing_potion.mara.hash_abc already applied.
Ability evidence added: Ember Ward training intent recorded.
Place updated: current scene set to The Drunken Huntsman from confirmed arrival.
Reward skipped duplicate: quest.reward.clear_mill_wolves.old_bren.15_gold already paid.

Audit must not mutate gameplay state.

Audit must be capped.

Audit must not dump full raw output text.

⸻

16. Reducer fail-soft policy

If a reducer fails:

* do not crash the hook
* do not continue unsafe optional commits
* record runtime/reducer error
* return failed_softly ReducerResult
* preserve existing valid state
* do not reset the whole bucket
* do not enable fallback broad mutation

Reducer fail-soft must not become hidden repair logic.

⸻

17. InventoryReducer contract summary

InventoryReducer owns:

inventory.items
inventory.equipped
inventory.currency when explicitly authorized
inventory.sceneItems
inventory item access/custody state

It handles accepted validation events such as:

inventory.acquire_candidate
inventory.handoff_candidate
inventory.transfer_candidate
inventory.drop_candidate
inventory.lose_candidate
inventory.consume_candidate
inventory.destroy_candidate
inventory.visible_candidate as scene item only if accepted for scene item
inventory.available_loot_candidate as scene item only if accepted for available loot

It must not:

complete quests
pay promised rewards unless reward contract authorizes currency update
create abilities
change current location
parse command output
import card text

Key old-failure protections:

denied item no acquisition
failed grab no acquisition
abstract item no acquisition
dream item no acquisition
visible item not owned
available loot not owned until accepted acquisition
mixed clause target separation

⸻

18. AbilityReducer contract summary

AbilityReducer owns:

abilities.records
ability evidence
ability status transitions
ability costs/cooldowns/reliability labels if validated

It handles accepted validation events such as:

ability.train_intent
ability.train_candidate
ability.use_success_candidate
ability.use_failure_candidate
ability.evidence_candidate
ability.stabilize_candidate

It must not:

create stable ability from unsupported output-only claim
award XP/stat gains directly
create generated cards
clean item/place names
parse raw input

Key old-failure protections:

explicit training creates evidence/candidate, not automatic mastery
output-only learned ability not stable by default
failed/pretend/quoted ability not stable
owner-specific AbilityNameSanitizer result required
repeated ability use deduped

⸻

body.hp
body.mp

It handles accepted validation events such as:

body.resource_state_candidate

body.death_boundary_candidate

change player identity species unless transformation contract exists

turn fatigue into exhaustion without validated severity

Key old-failure protections:

exhausted visible when explicit
fatigued separate from exhausted

⸻

⸻

27. ReducerRegistry integrity checks

ReducerRegistry must verify at startup or releasecheck:

all active families map to owner reducers
no active family maps to multiple reducers
unknown families do not route loosely
deferred systems do not have active reducers
rejected/pending/deferred results cannot reach reducers
operation-key-required families declare key policy
dirty target mapping exists for owned buckets

28. Required reducer registry tests

BC13-001 — Rejected result not committed

Given rejected inventory validation.

Expected:

ReducerRegistry does not call InventoryReducer
state unchanged

⸻

Given pending ability output-only learning claim.

Expected:

AbilityReducer not called for stable creation
state may store evidence only if explicitly accepted as evidence in separate result

⸻

Given crafting proposal.

Expected:

no reducer called
no crafting state created

⸻

Given accepted red healing potion handoff.

Expected:

InventoryReducer called
inventory bucket only mutated as allowed
operation key created
inventory/context dirty targets marked

⸻

BC13-005 — Duplicate inventory skipped

Accepted inventory handoff with existing item operation key.

Expected:

ReducerResult skipped_duplicate
no second item

⸻

Given accepted Ember Ward training evidence.

Expected:

AbilityReducer called
ability evidence/candidate updated
no stable mastery unless validated

Given ability training result.

Expected:

abilities may update
progression unchanged unless separate ProgressionReducer event accepted

⸻

Given accepted exhausted status.

Expected:

BodyReducer updates body conditions/fatigue/status
inventory/quests unchanged

⸻

Given accepted reward promise.

Expected:

QuestReducer updates reward status promised
currency unchanged

⸻

Given accepted reward payment with existing reward key.

Expected:

no second currency change
reward duplicate audit

⸻

Given rejected/pending weak chapel reference.

Expected:

PlaceReducer not called for current scene overwrite

⸻

BC13-012 — Strong arrival routes to PlaceReducer

Given accepted chapel arrival.

Expected:

PlaceReducer updates current scene/location as allowed
current_location/context dirty targets marked

⸻

BC13-013 — Nonliteral time rejected not routed

Given rejected quote time skip.

Expected:

TimeReducer not called
age/time unchanged

⸻

Given accepted time advance with existing key.

Expected:

skipped_duplicate
no second time/age advance

⸻

BC13-015 — Absent actor not present

Given rejected present actor result for absent goblin.

Expected:

ActorReducer not called for present actor

⸻

BC13-016 — Unknown owner fails softly

Given accepted result with unknown owner.

Expected:

ReducerRegistry returns failed_softly/no_op
state unchanged
audit warning

⸻

BC13-017 — Wrong owner rejected

Send inventory validation result to AbilityReducer intentionally in test.

Expected:

AbilityReducer rejects wrong owner
state unchanged

⸻

BC13-018 — Dirty target mapping created

Given accepted inventory update.

Expected:

dirty targets include inventory_equipment and context

⸻

BC13-019 — Reducer audit compact

Reducer commit audit expected.

Expected:

compact readable line
no full raw output dump

⸻

BC13-020 — Reducer fail-soft preserves state

Simulate reducer error.

Expected:

failed_softly result
no unrelated bucket reset
runtime/audit error recorded

29. ReleaseCheck requirements for Build Chunk 13

ReleaseCheck must eventually verify:

ReducerRegistry exists
owner map exists
all active families have exactly one owner
rejected/pending/deferred/blocked/malformed results cannot reach reducers
unknown owner fails softly
wrong owner rejects
operation key policy exists for repeat-sensitive families
dirty target mapping exists
audit line produced for commits/skips/failures
reducers do not parse raw text
reducers do not call CardSync live writes
reducers do not mutate non-owned buckets without explicit contract
deferred systems have no active reducers

ReleaseCheck must fail if:

ParserHit or EventProposal can mutate state without validation and reducer
non-accepted validation result can mutate state
multiple reducers own same truth family
InventoryReducer can create abilities or complete quests casually
AbilityReducer can award XP directly without contract
PlaceReducer can create persistent actors directly
CardSync can create gameplay canon
ContextBuilder can call reducers
manual mutation commands bypass reducers in MVP

32. Final Build Chunk 13 doctrine

ReducerRegistry is the commit gate.

Owner reducers are the only gameplay writers.

Final rule:

No accepted validation, no reducer.
No owner reducer, no mutation.
No operation key for repeat-sensitive effect, no repeat-safe commit.
No dirty target, no mirror/context update.
No audit, no trust.

With ReducerRegistry and owner contracts locked, the next safe step is to detail the core reducers.

====================================================================================================
20_Build_Chunks_01_to_17/14_AIDRPG_Build_Chunk_14_Core_Reducer_Plans.txt
====================================================================================================

AIDRPG Build Chunk 14 — Core Reducer Plans

0. Document status

Document name: AIDRPG Build Chunk 14 — Core Reducer Plans: Inventory, Ability, Body, Quest/Reward, Actor, Place/Scene, Time, Progression
Build target: AIDRPG Core MVP v0.1 / final-core architecture path
Purpose: Define exact behavior for the current-stage owner reducers before context, cards, cleanup, releasecheck, and code-pass planning.
Status: Practical build document.
Follows: Build Chunk 13 — ReducerRegistry and Owner Reducer Contract Plan.
This document does not contain final script code.

This chunk exists because Build Chunk 13 locked reducer ownership, but each reducer still needs exact behavior.

A reducer is where accepted canon becomes state.

That means this layer must be precise.

Reducers are not allowed to guess.

Reducers do not parse raw story text.

Reducers do not validate canon from scratch.

Reducers only apply accepted validation results from CanonValidator.

⸻

1. Core reducer doctrine

Accepted validation result in.
Owned state mutation only.
Operation key checked.
Dirty targets marked.
Audit written.
No extra guessing.

Every reducer must obey:

No accepted validation, no mutation.
No ownership, no mutation.
No duplicate-safe key for repeat-sensitive event, no repeat-safe commit.
No dirty target, no mirror/context update.
No audit, no trust.

⸻

2. Reducers covered in this chunk

Current-stage core reducers:

InventoryReducer
AbilityReducer
BodyReducer
QuestReducerLite / RewardReducerLite behavior
ActorReducerLite
PlaceReducerLite
TimeReducerLite
ProgressionReducerLite shell

Non-gameplay owners mentioned but not fully detailed here:

CardSyncLite
MigrationManager
QuarantineManager
DirtyQueue
CleanupManagerLite
ContextBuilderLite

Those are expanded in Build Chunk 15.

⸻

3. Universal reducer input contract

Every reducer receives a ValidationResult from CanonValidator.

Required input:

status = accepted
canonAllowed = true
reducerAllowed = true
requiredReducer matches reducer
source
localClause
safety flags
targetKey
targetDisplayName
operationKey or operation suggestion when repeat-sensitive
dirtyFamiliesIfAccepted
reasons

If any required field is missing:

return failed_softly or rejected_wrong_owner
mutate nothing
audit warning

⸻

4. Universal reducer output contract

Every reducer returns a ReducerResult.

ReducerResult {
  validationId,
  proposalId,
  family,
  eventType,
  ownerReducer,
  status,
  operationKey,
  operationKeyAdded,
  mutatedBuckets,
  dirtyTargets,
  auditLine,
  warnings,
  errors,
  notes
}

No reducer may return raw undefined/null.

⸻

5. Universal duplicate policy

Repeat-sensitive reducer events must check OperationLedger before mutation.

5.1 Standard repeat-sensitive flow

1. Build/read operation key.
2. Check duplicate.
3. If duplicate: no mutation, return skipped_duplicate.
4. If not duplicate: mutate owned bucket.
5. Add operation key.
6. Mark dirty targets.
7. Audit.

5.2 Repeat-sensitive event families

item acquisition
item transfer
item consumption
item destruction
currency payment
reward payment
XP gain
stat gain
level up
time advance
age update
ability stabilization
durable ability evidence
quest objective completion
quest completion
actor death/status change
location arrival
startup import
migration import/quarantine
card create/update

⸻

6. Universal dirty target policy

Reducers do not write context or Story Cards directly.

Reducers mark dirty targets.

Common dirty mappings:

inventory -> inventory_equipment, current_location, context
abilities -> abilities, context
body -> body_status, context
quests -> quest_log, context
actors -> social_memory, current_location, context
places -> current_location, context
time -> current_location, quest_log, context
progression -> player_sheet, context

Dirty targets are not gameplay truth.

⸻

7. Universal reducer forbidden actions

Reducers must not:

parse raw story text
scan command output
scan context text
scan Story Card text
call EventScannerLite
call ProposalBuilder
call CanonValidator for new truth
write Story Cards directly
create generated cards
enable deferred systems
import old card prose
resolve quarantine automatically
mutate buckets outside ownership without explicit contract
erase whole buckets during normal gameplay

⸻

8. InventoryReducer detailed plan

8.1 Owned buckets

InventoryReducer owns:

inventory.items
inventory.equipped
inventory.currency only through explicit accepted currency/reward contract
inventory.sceneItems
inventory item access/custody state
inventory.inventoryWarnings

8.2 Accepted event types

InventoryReducer may handle:

inventory.acquire_candidate
inventory.handoff_candidate
inventory.transfer_candidate
inventory.drop_candidate
inventory.lose_candidate
inventory.consume_candidate
inventory.destroy_candidate
inventory.visible_candidate as scene item only
inventory.available_loot_candidate as scene item only
inventory.equip_candidate later if accepted
inventory.unequip_candidate later if accepted
currency.gain_candidate only through explicit reward/currency validation
currency.spend_candidate deferred until economy spec

8.3 Inventory acquisition behavior

For accepted acquisition/handoff:

1. Confirm accepted validation result.
2. Confirm item target key/display name.
3. Confirm not visible-only/available-only/denied/revoked/dream/quote/abstract.
4. Check item acquisition operation key.
5. If duplicate, skip.
6. Add item or increase quantity according to unique/stack policy.
7. Set accessState = held unless equipped/stored explicitly validated.
8. Set owner = player and custody = player.
9. Add sourceEventKey/createdTurn.
10. Mark inventory_equipment and context dirty.
11. Audit.

8.4 Scene item behavior

Visible and available-loot are not owned inventory.

Accepted visible/available scene events go to:

inventory.sceneItems

not:

inventory.items

Scene item access states:

visible
available_loot
inaccessible

8.5 Custody behavior

If item is in NPC/place custody:

accessState = npc_custody or inaccessible
custody = actor/place/unknown
custodyId = resolved target if known

InventoryReducer must not transfer custody unless validation result is an accepted transfer/handoff.

8.6 Drop/loss/consume/destroy behavior

Accepted events update accessState rather than deleting records when possible.

dropped -> scene item or accessState lost/stored depending context
lost -> accessState lost
consumed -> accessState consumed and quantity decreases
destroyed -> accessState destroyed
sold -> deferred until economy spec unless manually validated later

Do not erase unique item history unless cleanup policy later allows archival.

8.7 Currency behavior

Currency may change only from accepted currency/reward payment event.

Reward promise does not change currency.

Economy buy/sell/spend is deferred.

8.8 Inventory operation keys

Suggested keys:

item.acquire.<itemKey>.<sourceOrScene>.<clauseHash>
item.handoff.<itemKey>.<fromActor>.<toPlayer>.<clauseHash>
item.transfer.<itemKey>.<from>.<to>.<clauseHash>
item.consume.<itemKey>.<clauseHash>
item.destroy.<itemKey>.<clauseHash>
item.drop.<itemKey>.<clauseHash>
currency.gain.<amount>.<sourceKey>.<clauseHash>

8.9 Inventory old-failure protections

InventoryReducer must never acquire items from:

denied acquisition
failed grab
revoked handoff
abstract item idea/concept/title
quoted instruction
NPC says take it without actual transfer
dream/vision handoff
visible-only item
available loot before acquisition
command output
context text
Story Card text

CanonValidator should already reject those, but InventoryReducer must reject if such flags slip through.

8.10 Inventory tests

Accepted red healing potion handoff -> item held by player
Duplicate red healing potion handoff -> one item or one quantity change only
Visible silver ring -> sceneItems only, not inventory.items
Goblin drops rusty dagger -> available_loot scene item, not owned
Reward promise 15 gold -> no currency
Accepted reward payment 15 gold -> currency +15 once
Denied key result accidentally routed -> reducer rejects / no key
Command output item accidentally routed -> reducer rejects / no item

⸻

9. AbilityReducer detailed plan

9.1 Owned buckets

AbilityReducer owns:

abilities.records
ability status
ability evidence
ability cost labels
ability reliability labels
ability sealed/unavailable markers
ability warnings

9.2 Accepted event types

AbilityReducer may handle:

ability.train_intent
ability.train_candidate
ability.use_attempt
ability.use_success_candidate
ability.use_failure_candidate
ability.evidence_candidate
ability.learn_candidate only if validated source exists
ability.stabilize_candidate only if strongly validated
ability.seal_candidate
ability.unavailable_candidate

9.3 Ability status model

Ability statuses:

unknown
hint
candidate
unstable
stable
sealed
unavailable

Rules:

candidate is not stable
hint is not usable
failed evidence does not stabilize
output-only claim does not stabilize by default
training evidence may create/update candidate
stable requires validator-approved source or repeated/tracked mastery condition

9.4 Training behavior

Accepted training:

1. Resolve ability key.
2. Check duplicate durable training key if needed.
3. If ability exists, append/update evidence.
4. If ability missing, create candidate record.
5. Set status candidate or unstable, not stable, unless validation says stabilize.
6. Record evidence type training.
7. Mark abilities and context dirty.
8. Audit.

9.5 Use success behavior

Accepted use success may:

append success evidence
update reliability
update lastUsedTurn
apply cooldown/cost only if validated and resource system active

It must not create stable mastery automatically unless validator event type is stabilize and conditions are met.

9.6 Use failure behavior

Accepted failure may:

append failure evidence
lower reliability
keep as candidate/unstable
record no effect/fizzle/collapse notes

It must not create stable ability.

9.7 Stability behavior

Stable ability creation requires explicit accepted stabilize event.

Allowed sources may include:

startup structured ability before lock
teacher/training culmination
successful controlled demonstration
validated transformation/grant
future absorption/evolution spec when enabled

9.8 Ability operation keys

ability.train.<abilityKey>.<clauseHash>
ability.evidence.<abilityKey>.<evidenceKind>.<clauseHash>
ability.use.<abilityKey>.<eventKind>.<outputHash>
ability.stabilize.<abilityKey>.<sourceKey>
ability.seal.<abilityKey>.<sourceKey>

9.9 Ability old-failure protections

AbilityReducer must reject if routed with flags:

quoted instruction only
pretend use
failed/no usable technique as stable
output-only learned claim as stable
non-ability object/travel/inspect target
wrong sanitizer used

9.10 Ability tests

I train Ember Ward to protect allies -> candidate/evidence Ember Ward
I practice Frost Step by moving -> candidate/evidence Frost Step
You learned Moonblade -> no stable ability unless validator approved stabilize
Void Lantern Veil collapses -> failure evidence, no stable
Mira says "Use Hollow Star Palm" -> no stable ability
Repeated Ember Ward use -> deduped evidence, no spam

⸻

10. BodyReducer detailed plan

10.1 Owned buckets

BodyReducer owns:

body.hp
body.mp
body.ep
body.injuries
body.conditions
body.fatigue
body.deathBoundary
body.bodyTraits when validated as body trait
body.bodyWarnings

10.2 Accepted event types

BodyReducer may handle:

body.status_gain_candidate
body.status_loss_candidate
body.injury_candidate
body.resource_change_candidate
body.resource_state_candidate
body.recovery_candidate
body.death_boundary_candidate
body.trait_gain_candidate only if validated
body.trait_loss_candidate only if validated

10.3 Status behavior

Accepted status gain:

1. Resolve condition/status key.
2. Check duplicate if durable/repeat-sensitive.
3. Add or update condition record.
4. Set severity from validation.
5. Set status active.
6. Mark body_status and context dirty.
7. Audit.

Accepted status loss/recovery:

update condition status to resolved/healing/fading
or remove/compact only through cleanup later

10.4 Fatigue/exhaustion behavior

Rules:

fatigued/worn out -> fatigue tired or condition fatigued
exhausted/collapsed/drained completely -> fatigue exhausted/collapsed if validated
mild fatigue does not auto-upgrade to exhausted

10.5 Injury behavior

Accepted injury creates or updates InjuryRecord.

Fields:

injuryId
name
location
severity
status
sourceEventKey
createdTurn
notes

10.6 Resource behavior

Accepted resource change may update HP/MP/EP.

Rules:

clamp current between 0 and max
do not alter max unless max-change validated
state repair clamping is not healing
resource state candidate like exhausted may set EP current low/0 only if validator approved numeric/state rule

10.7 Death boundary behavior

DeathBoundary changes are high-risk.

Allowed values:

alive
unconscious
dying
dead
captured
unknown

Rules:

rumor death rejected before reducer
quote death rejected before reducer
dream death rejected before reducer
metaphor dead tired rejected before reducer
nonfatal hostile act cannot set dead

If death candidate reaches reducer with unsafe flags, reject/fail softly.

10.8 Body operation keys

body.status.add.<statusKey>.<clauseHash>
body.status.resolve.<statusKey>.<clauseHash>
body.injury.<injuryKey>.<clauseHash>
body.resource.<resource>.<deltaOrState>.<clauseHash>
body.deathboundary.<status>.<clauseHash>

10.9 Body tests

exhausted by climb -> fatigue exhausted/condition exhausted visible
fatigued and worn out -> fatigue tired/fatigued, not exhausted
bleeding stops -> bleeding resolved
Mara says "you are dead" -> reducer not called or rejects
People say you died -> no deathBoundary change
wolf bite arm -> injury active

⸻

11. QuestReducerLite and Reward behavior detailed plan

11.1 Owned buckets

QuestReducerLite owns:

quests.records
quest objectives
quest statuses
quest reward status
quest warnings

Reward currency/item mutation requires explicit contract with InventoryReducer or split event.

11.2 Accepted quest event types

quest.offer_candidate
quest.accept_candidate
quest.objective_progress_candidate
quest.objective_complete_candidate
quest.ready_to_turn_in_candidate
quest.completed_candidate
quest.failed_candidate
quest.abandoned_candidate
quest.archived_candidate later

11.3 Accepted reward event types

reward.promise_candidate
reward.ready_candidate
reward.payment_candidate
reward.denied_candidate
reward.disputed_candidate

11.4 Quest creation/offer behavior

Accepted offer:

create or update quest with status offered/rumored
store giverActorId if resolved
store objective text
store promised reward if present
mark quest_log and context dirty
audit

Accepted acceptance:

create or update quest status active
initialize objectives
preserve promised reward as promised
operation key quest.accept...

11.5 Objective behavior

Accepted objective progress:

update objective progressCurrent/status
never complete unrelated objectives

Accepted objective complete:

set objective status complete
if all required objectives complete, quest may become ready_to_turn_in or completed depending validation

Completion does not automatically pay reward.

11.6 Reward promise behavior

Accepted reward promise:

set reward.status = promised
store description/currency/items/xp if clear
no inventory currency change
no XP change

11.7 Reward payment behavior

Accepted reward payment:

MVP has two possible safe designs.

Preferred design:

RewardReducer marks reward paid and emits/uses explicit Inventory currency contract.

Alternative safer shell:

QuestReducer marks reward ready/paid status only; separate accepted inventory currency result must add currency.

The final code pass must choose one policy and test it.

In either case:

reward key must prevent duplicate payment
promise does not pay
objective completion does not pay
command /quests display does not pay

11.8 Quest/reward operation keys

quest.offer.<questKey>.<giverKey>.<clauseHash>
quest.accept.<questKey>.<giverKey>
quest.objective.progress.<questKey>.<objectiveKey>.<clauseHash>
quest.objective.complete.<questKey>.<objectiveKey>
quest.complete.<questKey>.<sourceKey>
quest.reward.promise.<questKey>.<rewardKey>
quest.reward.pay.<questKey>.<payerKey>.<rewardKey>

11.9 Quest/reward tests

Old Bren promises 15 gold -> reward promised, currency unchanged
Old Bren pays promised 15 gold -> reward paid and currency changes once only under chosen contract
same payment output replay -> duplicate skipped
quest offered but not accepted -> status offered, not active
I accept quest to repair north wall -> active repair quest
complete objective -> objective complete, reward not paid automatically

⸻

12. ActorReducerLite detailed plan

12.1 Owned buckets

ActorReducerLite owns:

actors.records
actors.sceneActors
actor presence
actor memories
actor relationship notes
actor aliases
actor custody references only as references
actor warnings

12.2 Accepted event types

actor.present_candidate
actor.scene_only_candidate
actor.absent_candidate
actor.reference_only_candidate
actor.memory_candidate
actor.relationship_claim_candidate
actor.status_candidate
actor.death_candidate only if validated direct actor death

12.3 Present actor behavior

Accepted present actor:

if named/important/known -> update actor record presence present and currentPlaceId
if generic -> create sceneActor only unless promoted by rules
mark social_memory/current_location/context dirty
audit

12.4 Scene-only actor behavior

Generic actors usually become sceneActors:

a guard
a merchant
a stranger
a wolf if scene enemy but not persistent yet

SceneActors may expire on scene change.

They are not durable major actors unless promoted.

12.5 Absent/reference-only behavior

Accepted absent/reference-only:

may record note or presence elsewhere/absent if actor known
must not create present actor
must not apply harm/action to actor

Examples:

goblin definitely not here -> absent/reference-only, no present goblin
training dummy named after Mira -> reference-only Mira, no harm to Mira

12.6 Memory behavior

Accepted memory/witness event:

only actors present or directly informed receive memory
absent actors receive no scene memory
private reports stay private/pending
public reputation deferred

12.7 Actor operation keys

actor.present.<actorKey>.<placeKey>.<clauseHash>
actor.scene.<actorKey>.<sceneId>.<clauseHash>
actor.memory.<actorKey>.<memoryHash>
actor.status.<actorKey>.<statusKey>.<clauseHash>
actor.absent.<actorKey>.<clauseHash>

12.8 Actor tests

Veteran Guard present -> one actor/scene actor, no duplicate Guard
High Priestess present -> one target
Generic guard -> sceneActor, not permanent major actor by default
goblin not here -> no present goblin
training dummy named after Mira -> Mira not harmed/present
Gate guard witnesses theft -> guard memory only
Mira absent -> no Mira memory

⸻

13. PlaceReducerLite detailed plan

13.1 Owned buckets

PlaceReducerLite owns:

places.currentLocation
places.currentScene
places.records
places.travelIntent
place warnings

13.2 Accepted event types

place.current_scene_confirm_candidate
place.arrival_candidate
place.travel_intent_candidate
place.visible_reference
place.nearby_reference
place.route_reference

13.3 Current scene confirmation behavior

Accepted current scene confirmation:

update currentScene summary/updatedTurn if needed
confirm currentLocation if target matches current place or stronger name
mark current_location and context dirty
audit

13.4 Arrival behavior

Accepted arrival:

set currentLocation to destination
create/update PlaceRecord
reset or update currentScene
clear or mark travelIntent arrived if target matches
expire sceneActors/sceneItems according to cleanup policy later
mark current_location and context dirty

13.5 Travel intent behavior

Accepted travel intent from input:

set places.travelIntent
status intended or in_progress
currentLocation unchanged

13.6 Weak reference behavior

Accepted weak reference may:

store known/nearby place reference if useful
not overwrite current location
not clear current scene

13.7 Route/interstitial behavior

Route/interstitial place records may be scene/transit-only.

They must not outrank named strong destination.

13.8 Place operation keys

place.scene.confirm.<placeKey>.<clauseHash>
place.arrive.<placeKey>.<clauseHash>
place.intent.<placeKey>.<inputHash>
place.reference.<placeKey>.<clauseHash>

13.9 Place tests

market lane remains busy -> confirms market lane
chapel visible from market lane -> no scene overwrite
I head toward chapel -> travelIntent chapel, current location unchanged
You enter chapel -> current location chapel
After the road, you enter The Drunken Huntsman -> location Drunken Huntsman, not road

⸻

14. TimeReducerLite detailed plan

14.1 Owned buckets

TimeReducerLite owns:

time.text
time.turn if gameplay turn policy requires
time.lastAdvance
time.deadlines
time warnings

Player age is protected player identity and requires explicit age policy. TimeReducer cannot casually mutate player.age unless accepted age event and age policy allow it.

14.2 Accepted event types

time.advance_candidate
time.age_candidate with age policy
time.deadline_candidate
time.deadline_complete_candidate
time.deadline_missed_candidate

14.3 Time advance behavior

Accepted time advance:

check operation key
update time.text if structured enough
set lastAdvance
update deadlines if deadline logic active
mark current_location, quest_log, context dirty
audit

14.4 Age behavior

Age update is high-risk.

Rules:

requires literal long time passage
requires player age known/numeric or age policy for species
requires no quote/dream/nonliteral flags
requires duplicate key
if uncertain, pending/defer rather than mutate

MVP may record long time passage without changing exact player.age if policy is incomplete.

14.5 Nonliteral rejection fallback

If a rejected nonliteral time event accidentally routes to TimeReducer, reducer must reject/no-op.

14.6 Time operation keys

time.advance.<amountKey>.<clauseHash>
time.age.<amountKey>.<clauseHash>
time.deadline.<deadlineKey>.<sourceKey>

14.7 Time tests

"Twenty years pass" said aloud -> reducer not called/no-op
Dream twenty years pass -> no-op
Twenty years pass in exile -> time advance accepted/pending; age only with policy
same time output replay -> duplicate skipped
I plan to rest -> no time advance
You sleep through the night -> time advance if validated

⸻

15. ProgressionReducerLite shell plan

15.1 Owned buckets

ProgressionReducerLite owns:

progression.level
progression.currentXP
progression.totalXP
progression.xpToNext
progression.stats
progression.statXP
progression.levelHistory
progression.xpHistory
progression warnings

15.2 MVP activation level

ProgressionReducerLite may be shell/limited in Core MVP v0.1.

Allowed early behavior:

accept explicit XP if feature flag/reducerCommitsEnabled allows
record XP history with operation key
calculate level only if formula support is locked
otherwise mark pending/deferred and audit

15.3 Accepted event types

progression.xp_gain_candidate
progression.level_up_candidate
progression.stat_gain_candidate
progression.training_progress_candidate

15.4 XP behavior

Accepted XP gain:

requires explicit numeric XP
requires source key
requires operation key
no vague experience wording
no duplicate output

15.5 Level/stat behavior

Level/stat automation requires formula matrix and role/stat policy.

If not active:

return deferred or pending
no stat mutation

15.6 World scaling boundary

ProgressionReducerLite does not scale world actors.

ThreatProfileSystem/StatBenchmarkSystem later handles actor/world relative levels.

Player level does not rubber-band all enemies.

15.7 Progression operation keys

xp.gain.<amount>.<sourceKey>.<clauseHash>
level.up.<fromLevel>.<toLevel>.<sourceKey>
stat.gain.<statKey>.<amount>.<sourceKey>

15.8 Progression tests

You gain 20 XP -> XP proposal may commit if active and key unique
This was a learning experience -> no XP
same XP output replay -> duplicate skipped
Level 1000 player does not scale village guard or dragon
formula automation disabled -> stat/level changes deferred honestly

⸻

16. Cross-reducer contracts for MVP

The final code must choose exact cross-reducer policy before implementing reward/payment and quest/XP.

16.1 Reward payment policy options

Option A — Split result policy

CanonValidator emits two accepted results:

reward.payment_candidate -> QuestReducer updates reward paid
currency.gain_candidate -> InventoryReducer adds currency

Both share a related reward key and must not duplicate.

Option B — Reward contract helper policy

QuestReducer handles reward payment and calls a narrow InventoryCurrencyHelper.

This is more complex and can blur ownership.

16.2 Recommended MVP choice

Use Option A where possible:

one accepted result per owner bucket

This preserves one-owner-per-truth-family.

16.3 Quest XP policy

Quest completion should not directly award XP unless separate ProgressionReducer result exists.

16.4 Place actor cleanup policy

PlaceReducer should not delete or promote actors.

Scene actor expiration belongs to CleanupManagerLite or ActorReducer with explicit scene-change event.

⸻

17. Required core reducer tests

BC14-001 — Inventory denied routed accidentally

Given denied item validation accidentally marked accepted with unsafe flag.

Expected:

InventoryReducer rejects/no-op due unsafe flags

BC14-002 — Inventory acquisition commits once

Accepted potion handoff.

Expected:

one potion acquired
operation key added
inventory/context dirty

BC14-003 — Scene item not owned

Accepted visible ring.

Expected:

sceneItems updated
items unchanged

BC14-004 — Ability training candidate

Accepted Ember Ward training.

Expected:

candidate/evidence added
not stable

BC14-005 — Ability output-only stable blocked

Unsupported output-only learning routed accidentally.

Expected:

AbilityReducer rejects/no-op for stable creation

BC14-006 — Body exhausted visible

Accepted exhausted status.

Expected:

body condition/fatigue updated
/status can display it

BC14-007 — Death rumor no-op

Unsafe death rumor routed accidentally.

Expected:

BodyReducer no-op/reject

BC14-008 — Quest promise no currency

Accepted reward promise.

Expected:

quest reward promised
inventory currency unchanged

BC14-009 — Reward payment split owner policy

Accepted reward payment under chosen split policy.

Expected:

QuestReducer marks paid
InventoryReducer currency event adds gold once

BC14-010 — Actor absent no present

Accepted absent/reference-only actor.

Expected:

no present scene actor

BC14-011 — Place travel intent not arrival

Input travel intent.

Expected:

travelIntent set
currentLocation unchanged

BC14-012 — Place arrival update

Output strong arrival.

Expected:

currentLocation/currentScene update

BC14-013 — Time nonliteral no-op

Unsafe nonliteral time routed accidentally.

Expected:

TimeReducer rejects/no-op

BC14-014 — Progression vague XP no-op

Vague learning experience routed accidentally.

Expected:

ProgressionReducer rejects/no-op

BC14-015 — Cross-bucket forbidden mutation

AbilityReducer tries to award XP in test.

Expected:

ReleaseCheck/test fails

BC14-016 — Dirty target generation

Any committed reducer update.

Expected:

correct dirty targets created

BC14-017 — Reducer audit compact

Any committed reducer update.

Expected:

compact audit line
no full raw text dump

BC14-018 — Duplicate key skip

Repeat same accepted event.

Expected:

second reducer result skipped_duplicate
state unchanged on second pass

BC14-019 — Reducer fail-soft

Simulated reducer exception.

Expected:

failed_softly
no unrelated bucket reset
error audit

BC14-020 — Deferred progression formula honest

Stat gain proposal while formula automation inactive.

Expected:

deferred/pending
no stat mutation

⸻

18. ReleaseCheck requirements for Build Chunk 14

ReleaseCheck must eventually verify:

InventoryReducer owns inventory only
AbilityReducer owns abilities only
BodyReducer owns body only
QuestReducer owns quests/reward status only
ActorReducer owns actors only
PlaceReducer owns places only
TimeReducer owns time only
ProgressionReducer owns progression only
repeat-sensitive reducers check OperationLedger
reducers create dirty targets
reducers audit commits/skips/failures
reducers reject unsafe flags if accidentally routed
reward payment ownership policy is explicit
progression/stat formula automation is honest if inactive
scene item vs owned item split exists
candidate ability vs stable ability split exists
promise vs payment split exists
travel intent vs arrival split exists

ReleaseCheck must fail if:

InventoryReducer accepts denied/dream/quote/abstract item
AbilityReducer creates stable ability from unsupported output-only claim
BodyReducer sets death from rumor/quote/dream
QuestReducer pays promised reward as currency without payment validation
ActorReducer makes absent/reference-only actor present
PlaceReducer turns weak mention into arrival
TimeReducer ages from nonliteral quote/dream
ProgressionReducer awards XP from vague experience wording
Any reducer mutates non-owned bucket without explicit contract
Any reducer parses raw story text
Any repeat-sensitive reducer lacks duplicate key policy

⸻

20. What Build Chunk 15 should do next

Build Chunk 15 — ContextBuilder, CardSyncLite, Migration, DirtyQueue, and Cleanup Integration Plan
