21. Final Build Chunk 14 doctrine

Reducers are the only gameplay writers.

But even reducers are not free to improvise.

Final rule:

Each reducer owns one truth family.
Each reducer applies only accepted validation results.
Each reducer checks duplicate keys for repeat-sensitive effects.
Each reducer marks dirty targets instead of writing mirrors directly.
Each reducer audits what it did.
Each reducer rejects unsafe routed data instead of trusting upstream blindly.

With core reducer behavior planned, the next safe step is context, cards, migration, dirty queue, and cleanup integration.

====================================================================================================
20_Build_Chunks_01_to_17/15_AIDRPG_Build_Chunk_15_ContextBuilder_CardSyncLite_Migration_DirtyQueue_and_Cleanup_Integration_Plan.txt
====================================================================================================

AIDRPG Build Chunk 15 — ContextBuilder, CardSyncLite, Migration, DirtyQueue, and Cleanup Integration Plan

0. Document status

Document name: AIDRPG Build Chunk 15 — ContextBuilder, CardSyncLite, Migration, DirtyQueue, and Cleanup Integration Plan
Build target: AIDRPG Core MVP v0.1 / final-core architecture path
Purpose: Define the exact behavior for read-only context, protected Story Card mirrors, migration/quarantine, dirty target processing, and non-destructive cleanup before ReleaseCheck and regression locking.
Status: Practical build document.
Follows: Build Chunk 14 — Core Reducer Plans.
This document does not contain final script code.

This chunk exists because previous versions repeatedly failed when support layers became hidden writers.

Context started acting like state.

Story Cards started acting like canon.

Migration started guessing from old prose.

Cleanup risked deleting important truth.

Dirty queues could bloat or process too much.

CardSync could duplicate cards, route to wrong targets, or create broad-trigger pollution.

Build Chunk 15 locks those systems as support systems, not gameplay writers.

⸻

1. Support-layer doctrine

Reducers commit truth.
DirtyQueue marks what needs mirroring.
ContextBuilder reads truth.
CardSync mirrors truth.
Migration repairs structure and quarantines unsafe data.
Cleanup caps and expires safe noise.
None of these layers invent gameplay canon.

Support layers may improve readability, persistence, and stability.

They may not decide gameplay consequences.

⸻

2. Systems covered in this chunk

This chunk covers:

ContextBuilderLite
Context packet budget/selection
CardSyncLite protected mirrors
Card API adapter boundary
Protected card singleton policy
Generated card disabled policy
Card tombstone/duplicate policy
MigrationManager
QuarantineManager
DirtyQueue
CleanupManagerLite
support-layer integration tests

This chunk does not activate:

generated Story Cards
economy/shop/crafting/investigation/domain/reputation/party/warfare/absorption systems
manual mutation commands
full world structure automation

⸻

3. Placement in final script

Recommended placement:

Section 17 — Dirty Queue
Section 18 — Context Builder Lite
Section 19 — Card API Adapter and CardSync Lite
Section 20 — Migration and Quarantine Manager
Section 21 — Cleanup Manager Lite

These follow:

StateManager
Runtime
AuditLogger
OperationLedger
CommandRouter
EventScannerLite
ProposalBuilder
CanonValidator
ReducerRegistry
OwnerReducers

They come before:

ReleaseCheck final integration
hook orchestration final assembly

⸻

4. Support-layer hard boundaries

These systems must not:

parse raw story output as canon
call EventScannerLite on their own text
call ProposalBuilder on their own text
call CanonValidator to invent new truth
call owner reducers to create gameplay consequences
import Story Card prose into state
turn command output into gameplay
resolve quarantine automatically
create generated cards in MVP
write live cards unless explicitly safe and enabled
perform destructive cleanup of active truth

If a support layer needs a state change, it must be represented as a future accepted validation result or explicit safe structural repair path.

⸻

5. DirtyQueue doctrine

DirtyQueue is the bridge between reducer commits and support-layer updates.

Reducers mark dirty targets.

DirtyQueue records and prioritizes them.

ContextBuilder and CardSync may read dirty targets to know what to refresh.

DirtyQueue itself does not mutate gameplay truth.

⸻

6. DirtyTarget schema

DirtyTarget {
  dirtyId: string,
  family: string,
  targetKey: string,
  reason: string,
  sourceReducer: string,
  sourceOperationKey: string,
  priority: number,
  status: 'queued' | 'processed' | 'blocked' | 'error' | 'skipped',
  createdTurn: number,
  processedTurn: number,
  attempts: number,
  notes: string[]
}

Required dirty families:

player_sheet
body_status
abilities
inventory_equipment
quest_log
social_memory
current_location
context
card_registry
migration
audit

⸻

7. DirtyQueue behavior

7.1 Add dirty target

DirtyQueue may add or refresh a dirty target when a reducer commits.

Rules:

same family + targetKey + reason may be deduped
higher priority wins
attempt count does not increase when merely refreshed
createdTurn preserved if same unresolved target

7.2 Processing policy

Early MVP does not need automatic full processing.

Allowed:

ContextBuilder reads dirty context targets
CardSyncLite dry-run reads dirty protected card targets
CleanupManager caps dirty queue
commands display dirty state

Forbidden:

DirtyQueue directly edits Story Cards
DirtyQueue directly mutates gameplay buckets
DirtyQueue directly resolves migration/quarantine

7.3 Dirty cap

Recommended cap:

maxDirtyTargets = 100

If exceeded:

* dedupe first
* keep highest priority targets
* keep protected card targets
* keep context-critical targets
* summarize/cap low-priority audit-only targets
* never drop unresolved migration/quarantine warnings silently

⸻

8. ContextBuilderLite doctrine

ContextBuilderLite is read-only.

It serializes important current state into concise context packets for the AI.

Context is not canon.

Context cannot mutate gameplay state.

Context cannot create Story Cards.

Context cannot parse itself.

⸻

9. Context packet schema

ContextPacket {
  packetId: string,
  family: string,
  priority: number,
  text: string,
  sourceStateKeys: string[],
  sourceHashes: string[],
  maxAgeTurns: number,
  createdTurn: number,
  expiresTurn: number,
  reason: string,
  safeForModel: boolean,
  notes: string[]
}

Packet text must be concise and player-safe.

Do not include giant raw JSON.

Do not include internal IDs unless diagnostic mode explicitly allows it.

⸻

10. Context packet families

Core MVP context families:

player_identity_summary
body_status_summary
current_location_summary
current_scene_summary
inventory_key_items
ability_summary
quest_summary
actor_presence_summary
social_memory_summary
time_deadline_summary
safety_reminders

Deferred or limited families:

economy
crafting
investigation
reputation
domain
warfare
multi_protagonist
absorption/evolution

⸻

11. Context selection priority

Recommended priority order:

1. Current location and current scene
2. Player identity protected facts
3. Immediate body danger/status
4. Active quest objective/deadline
5. Present important actors
6. Key inventory/custody facts
7. Stable/candidate ability summary
8. Recent accepted consequence summary
9. Social/witness memory if relevant
10. Deferred system disabled reminders only when needed

ContextBuilder should avoid broad lore spam.

⸻

12. Context budget rules

Recommended budget:

maxContextChars = configurable, default 3000–4500 depending platform safety
maxPackets = 12
maxPacketChars = 450

If over budget:

* keep highest priority packets
* summarize low-priority packets
* skip broad lore packets
* audit skipped packet count
* never include full audit logs
* never include full inventory if large

⸻

13. Context no-write enforcement

ContextBuilderLite may write only diagnostic/audit metadata such as:

contextAudit
lastContextHash
selectedPacketSummary
skippedPacketSummary

It may not mutate:

player
body
progression
inventory
abilities
quests
actors
places
time
cards
migration/quarantine

ReleaseCheck must fail if ContextBuilder can call reducers or mutate gameplay state.

⸻

14. Context stale packet policy

Context packets should be rebuilt from state, not treated as state.

If state changes:

reducer marks context dirty
ContextBuilder rebuilds packet from state
old packet is ignored or expires

Context packet text does not become canon.

If context says an item exists but state does not, state wins.

⸻

15. CardSyncLite doctrine

Story Cards are mirrors.

They are not canon.

State is canon.

Context is immediate model-facing summary.

Story Cards are long-term mirror summaries only.

CardSyncLite must not create gameplay truth.

⸻

16. Protected cards in MVP

MVP protected singleton cards:

protected:player_sheet
protected:body_status
protected:abilities
protected:inventory_equipment
protected:quest_log
protected:social_memory
protected:current_location

Protected cards mirror these state families:

Protected card	State source
player_sheet	player + progression summary
body_status	body
abilities	abilities
inventory_equipment	inventory
quest_log	quests
social_memory	actors/social memory
current_location	places + time + scene

⸻

17. Protected card registry schema

ProtectedCardRecord {
  key: string,
  title: string,
  cardId: string,
  status: 'missing' | 'present' | 'duplicate' | 'disabled' | 'tombstoned' | 'unknown',
  lastRenderedHash: string,
  lastSyncedTurn: number,
  dirty: boolean,
  duplicateIds: string[],
  tombstoneId: string,
  warnings: string[],
  notes: string[]
}

Registry lives in:

cards.protectedCards
cards.registry

⸻

18. Card API adapter boundary

CardSyncLite must talk to card APIs only through a CardApiAdapter.

Adapter capabilities:

listCards
createCard
updateCard
disableCard
removeCard if supported later
capabilityCheck

MVP may run adapter in:

dry-run
safe mode

If card API is unavailable, CardSyncLite must fail softly and audit.

⸻

19. CardSync modes

Required flags:

cardSyncDryRun = true by default
cardSyncSafeMode = true by default
protectedCardWritesEnabled = false by default in early MVP
generatedCardsEnabled = false
generatedCardCreatesPerTurn = 0
generatedCardUpdatesPerTurn = 0

Live protected writes may only activate after:

adapter tests pass
protected singleton tests pass
releasecheck gate passes
user/build explicitly enables later mode

Generated cards remain disabled in Core MVP v0.1.

⸻

20. Card render rules

CardSync renders from state only.

It may render:

confirmed player identity
confirmed body status
confirmed ability statuses
confirmed inventory/scene item separation
confirmed quest/reward statuses
confirmed actor/social memory
confirmed current location/time

It may not render as confirmed:

candidate as stable
visible item as owned
promised reward as paid
travel intent as arrival
rumor as fact
dream as canon
quarantined old data as truth

Card text should clearly label candidate/visible/promised/rumored states.

⸻

21. Card title and trigger hygiene

Use CardNameSanitizer and CardTriggerSanitizer from Build Chunk 12.

Reject broad triggers for generated cards.

Protected card triggers should be stable and controlled.

Forbidden broad generated triggers include:

street
road
town
village
forest
market
shop
sword
key
guard
man
woman
he
she
they

Generated cards disabled means these should not be created anyway, but ReleaseCheck must still guard future activation.

⸻

22. Protected singleton policy

For each protected card key, only one active card should exist.

If duplicates detected:

mark registry duplicate
choose one canonical card if safe
mark duplicates in duplicateWarnings
if live safe mode supports it later, disable duplicates carefully
never import duplicate card text into state

MVP dry-run should report duplicate handling without destructive changes.

⸻

23. Tombstone policy

Tombstones prevent deleted/disabled cards from being immediately recreated repeatedly.

A tombstone record includes:

tombstoneId
cardKey
oldCardId
reason
createdTurn
expiresTurn or permanent flag
notes

MVP may only audit tombstones unless card API behavior is confirmed.

Tombstones must not delete gameplay state.

⸻

24. CardSync write budget

Recommended MVP limits when live writes later enabled:

maxProtectedCardUpdatesPerTurn = 2
maxProtectedCardCreatesPerTurn = 1
maxCardApiOperationsPerTurn = 3
maxCardRenderCharsPerCard = 1200

Dry-run may simulate more but should cap audit rows.

If too many dirty cards:

* prioritize current_location, body_status, inventory_equipment, quest_log
* defer lower priority updates
* audit deferred count

⸻

25. Generated card disabled policy

In Core MVP v0.1:

generatedCardsEnabled = false

If text or command attempts to enable generated cards:

block
return disabled message
releasecheck fails if flag true

Generated card planning may exist as deferred spec only.

No generated actor/place/item/quest cards are created in MVP.

⸻

26. MigrationManager doctrine

Migration repairs structure.

Migration preserves valid truth.

Migration quarantines unsafe old data.

Migration does not guess canon from prose.

Old patches are evidence, not direct imports.

⸻

27. Migration safe actions

Migration may:

ensure state.aidrpg root exists
ensure required buckets exist
add missing fields with safe defaults
preserve valid existing fields
clamp invalid resource numbers structurally
record migration warnings
detect old roots or old patch layers
quarantine unsafe legacy data previews
force unsafe MVP feature flags false
disable generated cards
mark old card prose as non-canon if encountered

Migration may not:

scan all old Story Cards as canon
turn old inventory card prose into inventory
turn old ability card prose into stable ability
turn old quest card prose into active quest
pay old reward text
resolve quarantine automatically
delete old valid state recklessly

⸻

28. QuarantineManager doctrine

Quarantine is for unsafe or uncertain legacy claims.

Quarantined data is not active gameplay truth.

A quarantined item is not inventory.

A quarantined ability is not usable.

A quarantined quest is not active.

A quarantined actor is not present.

⸻

29. Quarantine record schema

QuarantineRecord {
  quarantineId: string,
  source: string,
  sourceKey: string,
  originalPreview: string,
  reason: string,
  severity: 'low' | 'medium' | 'high' | 'critical',
  suggestedAction: string,
  status: 'unresolved' | 'ignored' | 'resolved_later' | 'superseded',
  createdTurn: number,
  notes: string[]
}

Quarantine records must be deduped by sourceKey + reason where possible.

⸻

30. Startup import relationship

Startup import was planned earlier, but Build Chunk 15 confirms support-layer boundaries.

Startup import may only use:

structured setup source
startup operation keys
StateManager/StartupManager
owner reducers or structured init path before gameplay lock

Startup import must not use:

old card prose
context text
command output
AI-generated story claims after lock

After gameplay starts:

startup identity locked
setup cannot overwrite protected identity fields without future manual override spec

⸻

31. CleanupManagerLite doctrine

CleanupManagerLite keeps state small and safe.

It does not rewrite gameplay truth.

It does not delete important active records.

It does not resolve canon uncertainty.

It does not patch away bugs silently.

⸻

32. Cleanup allowed actions

CleanupManagerLite may:

cap audit arrays
cap runtime warnings/errors
cap hook traces
cap recent operation keys
cap dirty queue after dedupe
expire scene-only actors after scene change if policy exists
expire stale scene items if policy exists
compact resolved low-risk warnings
summarize old debug lines

CleanupManagerLite may not:

delete active inventory items
delete active quests
delete stable abilities
delete important actors
delete current location/time
clear reward keys
clear card keys
clear permanent keys
resolve quarantine automatically
turn candidate ability into stable
remove conditions/injuries without recovery event

⸻

33. Cleanup caps

Recommended caps:

runtime.hookTrace max 30
runtime.runtimeWarnings max 25
runtime.runtimeErrors max 20
audits.debugMessages max 50
audits.recentProposals max 50
audits.recentValidationResults max 50
audits.cardAudit max 50
audits.contextAudit max 50
audits.cleanupAudit max 50
audits.migrationAudit max 50
operationLedger.recentKeys max 250
dirty.targets max 100
quarantine display cap, not destructive cap

Do not cap durable keys as if they were recent noise.

⸻

34. Cleanup audit format

Examples:

Cleanup capped debugMessages from 75 to 50, preserving most recent entries.
Cleanup deduped dirty target inventory_equipment:item:red_healing_potion.
Cleanup skipped active quest records: destructive cleanup disabled.

Cleanup audit must be compact and capped.

⸻

35. Support-layer integration order after Output hook

Recommended Output hook order after reducers:

1. ReducerRegistry applies accepted results.
2. Reducers return dirty targets.
3. DirtyQueue records/dedupes targets.
4. Context dirty state is available for next Context hook.
5. CardSyncLite dry-run checks protected card updates if enabled.
6. CleanupManagerLite performs safe caps.
7. AuditLogger records summaries.
8. Runtime returns original output text.

CardSync should not block story output if it fails.

Cleanup should not block story output if it fails.

Both fail softly.

⸻

36. Command surfaces affected

Build Chunk 15 supports these commands:

/contextaudit
/cardaudit
/dirty
/migration
/quarantine
/stateaudit
/releasecheck
/debuglast

These commands inspect support-layer state.

They do not process dirty targets, write cards, resolve quarantine, or perform destructive cleanup.

⸻

37. Required support-layer tests

BC15-001 — Context read-only

Run ContextBuilderLite.

Expected:

context text/audit may update
player/body/inventory/abilities/quests/actors/places/time unchanged

Fail if context mutates gameplay truth.

⸻

BC15-002 — Context budget respected

Given many active state records.

Expected:

context packets capped/summarized
skipped packet audit exists
no giant raw dump

⸻

BC15-003 — Context state wins over stale packet

State says no Crown of Dawn.

Old/stale context packet mentions Crown.

Expected:

rebuilt context excludes Crown
no inventory mutation

⸻

BC15-004 — Dirty target created by reducer

Accepted inventory commit.

Expected:

dirty targets include inventory_equipment and context

⸻

BC15-005 — Dirty target dedupe

Add same dirty target repeatedly.

Expected:

one queued/refreshed target
priority preserved/escalated
no unbounded duplicates

⸻

BC15-006 — CardSync dry-run only

Feature flags default.

Expected:

CardSync reports planned protected update
no live card write

⸻

BC15-007 — Generated cards disabled

Attempt generated card creation.

Expected:

blocked/suppressed
generatedCardsEnabled false
no generated card record created except audit warning

⸻

BC15-008 — Protected singleton duplicate detected

Two Player Sheet cards exist in simulated adapter list.

Expected:

registry marks duplicate
duplicate warning audit
no card text imported as canon

⸻

BC15-009 — Manual card edit not canon

Manual card says:

Player owns Crown of Dawn.

Expected:

no inventory mutation
cardaudit warning possible

⸻

BC15-010 — Broad trigger rejected

Generated trigger candidate:

street

Expected:

trigger rejected too broad
no generated card

⸻

BC15-011 — Card API unavailable fail-soft

Simulate adapter unavailable.

Expected:

card audit warning
no crash
story output unaffected

⸻

BC15-012 — Migration missing bucket repair

State missing inventory bucket.

Expected:

inventory bucket recreated safely
existing player preserved
repair warning

⸻

BC15-013 — Migration old card prose quarantined

Old card text says:

Player owns Crown of Dawn.

Expected:

quarantine/warning only
no Crown inventory

⸻

BC15-014 — Quarantine inactive

Quarantine contains item claim.

Expected:

/quarantine displays it
/inventory does not show it as owned

⸻

BC15-015 — Future schema safe

State schema version is future/unknown.

Expected:

no crash
migration warning
safe conservative behavior

⸻

BC15-016 — Cleanup caps audits

Debug audit has 100 entries.

Expected:

capped to configured max
recent entries preserved
no gameplay mutation

⸻

BC15-017 — Cleanup does not delete durable keys

OperationLedger has rewardKeys/cardKeys/permanentKeys.

Expected:

cleanup does not remove them
recentKeys only capped

⸻

BC15-018 — Cleanup does not heal/remove condition

Body has active bleeding.

Run cleanup.

Expected:

bleeding remains active
no healing without reducer event

⸻

BC15-019 — Cleanup does not delete active quest

Active quest exists.

Run cleanup.

Expected:

quest preserved

⸻

BC15-020 — Support-layer fail-soft

Simulate CardSync/Cleanup error during Output hook.

Expected:

runtime returns original output
error audit recorded
no partial gameplay mutation by support layer

⸻

38. ReleaseCheck requirements for Build Chunk 15

ReleaseCheck must verify:

ContextBuilderLite exists or is honestly shell-only
ContextBuilderLite cannot mutate gameplay buckets
context budget/cap exists
DirtyQueue exists
DirtyQueue dedupes/caps targets
CardSyncLite protected mode exists or is honestly dry-run/shell-only
generated cards disabled in MVP
protected card singleton policy exists
Card API adapter boundary exists
manual card text cannot become canon
broad trigger rejection exists for generated cards
MigrationManager repairs structure conservatively
QuarantineManager keeps unsafe data inactive
CleanupManagerLite caps only safe arrays
Cleanup does not remove active truth or durable keys
support-layer fail-soft behavior exists

ReleaseCheck must fail if:

ContextBuilder mutates gameplay state
Story Card text can create gameplay canon
generatedCardsEnabled true in MVP
CardSync live writes enabled without adapter tests
protected card duplicates can crash or import text as canon
Migration imports old card prose as item/ability/quest truth

DirtyQueue grows unbounded
support-layer errors can crash hook output

41. Final Build Chunk 15 doctrine

Support layers are allowed to help the system remember, explain, mirror, and stay small.

They are not allowed to play the game.

Final rule:

Context reads.
Cards mirror.
Migration repairs structure.
Quarantine isolates unsafe claims.
DirtyQueue marks refresh needs.
Cleanup caps safe noise.
Reducers remain the only gameplay writers.

With context, cards, migration, dirty queue, and cleanup planned, the next safe step is ReleaseCheck and the regression matrix lock.

====================================================================================================
20_Build_Chunks_01_to_17/16_AIDRPG_Build_Chunk_16_ReleaseCheck_and_Regression_Matrix_Lock.txt
====================================================================================================

AIDRPG Build Chunk 16 — ReleaseCheck and Regression Matrix Lock

0. Document status

Document name: AIDRPG Build Chunk 16 — ReleaseCheck and Regression Matrix Lock
Build target: AIDRPG Core MVP v0.1 / final-core architecture path
Purpose: Define the exact ReleaseCheck gate system, regression matrix, old-failure coverage gates, deferred-system gates, and no-code-until-covered policy before Foundation Code Pass 1 planning.
Status: Practical build document.
Follows: Build Chunk 15 — ContextBuilder, CardSyncLite, Migration, DirtyQueue, and Cleanup Integration Plan.
This document does not contain final script code.

This chunk exists because previous AIDRPG builds passed normal checks while still failing under old patch-loop problems, adversarial wording, command/card/context contamination, repeated effects, and cross-system regressions.

A final build cannot rely on hope.

ReleaseCheck and the regression matrix are the gate that prevents old failures from being forgotten.

⸻

1. ReleaseCheck doctrine

If a current-stage system is active, ReleaseCheck must know how to inspect it.
If a known old failure is current-stage relevant, ReleaseCheck must know whether it is covered.
If a system is deferred, ReleaseCheck must verify it is actually disabled.
If a reducer can mutate state, ReleaseCheck must verify its owner boundary.
If a parser can detect events, ReleaseCheck must verify it cannot commit them.
If a support layer can mirror or summarize, ReleaseCheck must verify it cannot invent canon.

ReleaseCheck is not decoration.

ReleaseCheck is the final planning and runtime safety gate.

⸻

2. Regression matrix doctrine

Every known failure becomes a test.
Every old patch goal becomes a regression requirement.
Every new design decision gets a proof case.
Every deferred system gets a disabled-behavior test.
Every active reducer gets commit, duplicate, no-op, and fail-soft tests.
Every non-canon source gets a rejection test.

The regression matrix is not just for future QA.

It tells the script builder exactly what must not break.

⸻

3. Placement in final script/package

ReleaseCheckLite belongs in:

Section 22 — ReleaseCheck Lite

Regression Matrix belongs in the planning/test package as:

AIDRPG Regression Matrix v1

ReleaseCheck depends on:

StateManager
Runtime
Feature Flags
CommandRouter
AuditLogger
OperationLedger
EventScannerLite
ProposalBuilder
CanonValidator
Resolver/Sanitizer
ReducerRegistry
Owner Reducer declarations
DirtyQueue
ContextBuilderLite
CardSyncLite
Migration/Quarantine
CleanupManagerLite

ReleaseCheck must not:

mutate gameplay state
repair gameplay truth except through StateManager structural init
write Story Cards
process dirty targets
resolve quarantine
activate deferred systems
run expensive full-history scans

⸻

4. ReleaseCheck result statuses

ReleaseCheck returns one of four top-level results.

PASS
WARN
FAIL
BLOCKED

4.1 PASS

All current-stage mandatory gates pass.

Deferred systems are disabled safely.

Known current-stage failure classes have owner/rule/test coverage.

4.2 WARN

Current-stage core can operate, but non-blocking limitations exist.

Allowed WARN examples:

parser family detection is limited but honestly shell-only
CardSync live writes are dry-run only
progression formula automation is shell/deferred
WorldStructure deferred
reputation automation deferred

WARN must not hide unsafe active behavior.

4.3 FAIL

A current-stage safety requirement is broken.

Examples:

command output can be parsed as story
context can mutate gameplay
generated cards enabled in MVP
parser can write inventory directly
CanonValidator missing for active proposals
rejected results can reach reducers

FAIL means do not ship / do not proceed to code-finalization.

4.4 BLOCKED

A requested active system lacks required spec, tests, or gates.

Examples:

crafting enabled without Crafting Mini-Spec
economy enabled without Transaction Ledger Mini-Spec
generated cards enabled without Generated CardSync Beta Spec
manual mutation commands enabled without Manual Override Spec

BLOCKED means the system cannot be activated in the current build.

⸻

5. ReleaseCheck record schema

ReleaseCheck should produce a structured record.

ReleaseCheckRecord {
  turn: number,
  result: 'PASS' | 'WARN' | 'FAIL' | 'BLOCKED',
  buildName: string,
  buildVersion: string,
  schemaVersion: string,
  checkedAtTurn: number,
  passCount: number,
  warningCount: number,
  failureCount: number,
  blockerCount: number,
  passes: ReleaseCheckLine[],
  warnings: ReleaseCheckLine[],
  failures: ReleaseCheckLine[],
  blockers: ReleaseCheckLine[],
  deferredSystems: ReleaseCheckLine[],
  knownFailureCoverage: KnownFailureCoverageSummary[],
  notes: string[]
}

5.1 ReleaseCheckLine schema

ReleaseCheckLine {
  gateId: string,
  gateName: string,
  status: 'PASS' | 'WARN' | 'FAIL' | 'BLOCKED',
  severity: 'info' | 'low' | 'medium' | 'high' | 'critical',
  message: string,
  ownerModule: string,
  relatedFailureIds: string[],
  requiredAction: string
}

5.2 KnownFailureCoverageSummary schema

KnownFailureCoverageSummary {
  failureId: string,
  title: string,
  currentStage: boolean,
  ownerModule: string,
  ruleDefined: boolean,
  testDefined: boolean,
  releaseGateDefined: boolean,
  status: 'covered' | 'partial' | 'missing' | 'deferred'
}

⸻

6. ReleaseCheck output command format

/releasecheck should render a concise human-readable summary.

Required sections:

[AIDRPG] Release Check
Status: PASS/WARN/FAIL/BLOCKED
Build:
Schema:
Mode:
Summary:
- Passes:
- Warnings:
- Failures:
- Blockers:
Critical Gates:
- ...
Known Failure Coverage:
- ...
Deferred Systems:
- ...
Result:
No gameplay state was changed.

The command may store the ReleaseCheckRecord in audits through official audit path.

It must not mutate gameplay state.

⸻

7. Master gate families

ReleaseCheck must eventually check these families.

G01 Build Identity Gate
G02 State Schema Gate
G03 Runtime Hook Gate
G04 Command Non-Canon Gate
G05 Feature Flag / Deferred System Gate

G07 Proposal Local Binding Gate
G08 Canon Clause Gate

G21 Player-Breaker Adversarial Gate
G22 Package Readiness Gate

⸻

8. G01 — Build Identity Gate

Checks:

constants build name exists
constants build version exists
schema version exists
meta build/schema version exists
/version command agrees with constants
runtime identity is not downgraded by late patch

Fail if:

/version disagrees with constants
schema missing
build stage unknown
late patch identity mismatch detected

Related failures:

F011 runtime identity / build stage overwritten by patches

⸻

9. G02 — State Schema Gate

Checks:

state.aidrpg root exists
all required buckets exist
meta/features/runtime/startup/player/body/progression/inventory/abilities/quests/actors/places/time/cards/operationLedger/audits/migration/quarantine/dirty/cleanup exist
required runtime fields exist
required card flags safe
required ledger buckets exist
required audit buckets exist

Fail if:

required current-stage bucket missing and StateManager cannot repair
operationLedger reward/card/permanent keys missing
runtime duplicate output fields missing
cards generated enabled by default

Related failures:

F021 migration/save corruption/old patch layers
F022 runtime hook failure and timeout risk

⸻

Checks:

StateManager runs first in each hook
Input/Context/Output return valid shapes
fail-soft exists
hookDepth resets

Fail if:

test
risk if skipped

Fail if:

critical old fix has no classification
old protection silently dropped
old patch copied blindly without owner/rule/test

⸻

28. G21 — Player-Breaker Adversarial Gate

Checks that adversarial tests exist for:

negative inventory
failed acquisition
abstract inventory
quoted handoff
dream handoff
output-only ability learning
failed ability
pretend ability
nonliteral time skip
quote death/rumor death
weak scene mention
reward promise vs payment
command/context/card output injection
repeated output/reward/ability/time

Fail if active canon pipeline lacks adversarial rejection coverage.

⸻

Before final package/code pass:

Checks:

Build Chunks 1–17 present
Final Build Control Map present
known failure list current
open evidence queue acknowledged

code pass scope defined
install/use notes planned

Fail if:

planning documents contradict active build scope
remaining mandatory chunk missing
code pass scope unclear

⸻

30. Regression Matrix structure

The regression matrix should be stored as a table or structured document with fields:

Test ID
Failure ID
Module owner
Scenario name
Setup state
Input text
AI output text
Expected parser hits
Expected proposals
Expected validation results
Expected reducer result
Expected state diff
Expected dirty targets
Expected command/audit output
Expected ReleaseCheck gate
Status
Notes

Not every test needs every field in early planning, but final code tests should capture state diffs where possible.

⸻

31. Regression test tiers

The test suite should be organized into tiers.

T0 Smoke Tests
T1 State/Runtime/Command Foundation Tests
T2 Parser/Proposal/Validator Safety Tests
T3 Resolver/Sanitizer Tests
T4 Reducer Commit Tests
T5 Context/Card/Migration/Cleanup Tests
T6 Player-Breaker Adversarial Tests
T7 Long-Run Stability Tests
T8 Deferred System Disabled Tests
T9 Packaging/Install/Usage Tests

⸻

32. T0 — Smoke Tests

Minimum smoke tests:

fresh state initializes
/help works
/version works
/sheet works
/status works
/inventory works
/releasecheck runs
unknown command safe
non-command input returns valid shape
output hook returns valid shape
context hook returns valid shape

Fail if any command crashes.

⸻

33. T1 — State/Runtime/Command Foundation Tests

Must include:

fresh root creation
missing bucket repair preserves player
malformed root fail-soft
resource clamping structural only
feature flags forced safe
command output prefix
command output suppression
manual mutation commands disabled
unknown command no mutation
duplicate output hash skip
hookDepth resets after error
runtime warnings/errors capped

⸻

34. T2 — Parser/Proposal/Validator Safety Tests

Must include:

command output not parsed
context text not parsed
Story Card text not parsed
input item attempt only
output pickup candidate only until validation
quoted/dream/rumor/negated/hypothetical flags
local clause target binding
proposal source/local/target binding
CanonValidator rejection/acceptance/pending/defer

⸻

35. T3 — Resolver/Sanitizer Tests

Must include:

Ember Ward to protect allies -> Ember Ward
Frost Step by moving -> Frost Step
Stoneguard until stance holds -> Stoneguard
travel to smithy does not create ability
inspect door does not create ability
use iron key on cellar door does not create ability
target rusted iron key cleaned from failed grab clause
Veteran Guard not duplicate Guard
High Priestess preserved
chapel visible weak reference
road interstitial loses to named inn arrival
village well not settlement
broad trigger street rejected
ACTOR:AND_MIRA cleaned to ACTOR:MIRA

⸻

36. T4 — Reducer Commit Tests

Must include:

accepted potion handoff commits once
visible ring scene item not owned
denied item routed accidentally no-ops
candidate ability training creates candidate/evidence
output-only learned ability not stable
exhausted status visible
fatigue not exhaustion
death rumor no-op
quest promise no currency
reward payment once
absent actor not present
travel intent not arrival
strong arrival updates place
time quote no-op
literal time duplicate skipped
vague experience no XP

⸻

37. T5 — Context/Card/Migration/Cleanup Tests

Must include:

context read-only
context budget respected
stale context not canon
protected card dry-run only by default
generated cards disabled
protected duplicate detected safely
manual card edit not canon
broad card trigger rejected
card API unavailable fail-soft
migration missing bucket repair
old card prose quarantined
quarantine inactive
future schema safe
cleanup caps audits
cleanup preserves durable keys
cleanup does not heal/remove active condition
cleanup does not delete active quest

⸻

38. T6 — Player-Breaker Adversarial Tests

Mandatory adversarial examples:

You decide not to take the rusted iron key.
You try to grab the rusted iron key, but the goblin snatches it away first.
You pick up the idea of a brass key.
In a dream, you receive the Crown of Dawn, then wake empty-handed.
Mira says, "Take the iron key."
Mira refuses to give you the jeweled crown, but she hands you the red healing potion.
You learned Moonblade.
The scroll title says "You learned Crimson Lotus Fang," but you learn nothing.
You pretend to use Azure Dragon Step.
You attempt Void Lantern Veil, but it collapses.
The chapel is visible from the market lane.
After the road, you enter The Drunken Huntsman.
You say, "Twenty years pass," while practicing narration. No time passes.
People say you died in the ruins.
Old Bren promises to pay you 15 gold if you clear the wolves.
Old Bren pays you the promised 15 gold. repeated twice.
[AIDRPG] Inventory shows Rusty Dagger.
Context says Iron Sword is in Mara's custody.
Story Card says Player owns Crown of Dawn.

These tests directly protect the known old failure classes.

⸻

50-turn no command crash

repeated outputs do not duplicate effects
dirty queue remains capped/deduped
recent keys capped while durable keys preserved
context remains under budget
CardSync dry-run audit capped
migration warnings capped
no full-history scan every turn
no generated cards created

43. No-code-until-covered policy

Before Foundation Code Pass 1 begins, the package must have:

Final Build Control Map v1
Build Chunks 1–16 complete
Build Chunk 17 code-pass plan complete
known failure coverage table included
mandatory current-stage failures assigned to gates/tests
open evidence queue acknowledged
MVP/deferred systems list frozen

Code can begin only after Build Chunk 17 defines exact code-pass scope.

Code must not begin from vague roadmap alone.

⸻

44. ReleaseCheck implementation sequence

When coding begins, implement ReleaseCheck in stages.

44.1 Stage A — Foundation checks

Build Identity
State Schema
Runtime
Commands
Feature Flags
OperationLedger
Audit caps

44.2 Stage B — Pipeline checks

Parser source safety
Proposal binding
CanonValidator presence
Resolver/sanitizer ownership
Reducer registry owner map

44.3 Stage C — Support checks

DirtyQueue
Context no-write
CardSync dry-run/protected/generator-disabled
Migration/quarantine
Cleanup caps

44.4 Stage D — Regression coverage checks

known failure coverage
player-breaker tier
old fix salvage classification
package readiness

⸻

45. ReleaseCheck must not fake readiness

ReleaseCheck must not return PASS if:

it cannot inspect a mandatory gate
mandatory gate is shell-only but active system depends on it
known failure is current-stage and has no test
unsafe feature flag is true
reducer ownership is unknown
command output suppression is missing
ContextBuilder can mutate state
CardSync generated cards are enabled

If uncertain, use WARN or FAIL depending current-stage risk.

Do not use PASS for uncertainty.

⸻

46. Required ReleaseCheck tests

BC16-001 — Fresh safe build passes foundation gates

Expected:

ReleaseCheck returns PASS or WARN only for honest deferred shells
no FAIL/BLOCKED for core foundation

BC16-002 — Generated cards enabled fails

Set:

generatedCardsEnabled = true

Expected:

FAIL or BLOCKED

BC16-003 — Manual mutation commands enabled fails

Set:

manualMutationCommandsEnabled = true

Expected:

FAIL/BLOCKED

BC16-004 — Missing operationLedger rewardKeys fails

Expected:

FAIL

BC16-005 — Command output suppression missing fails

Expected:

FAIL

BC16-006 — Parser direct mutation path fails

Expected:

FAIL

BC16-007 — Proposal missing local clause fails

Expected:

FAIL if ProposalBuilder active

BC16-008 — CanonValidator missing fails

Expected:

FAIL if reducers active

BC16-009 — Rejected result can reach reducer fails

Expected:

FAIL

BC16-010 — Context mutation path fails

Expected:

FAIL

BC16-011 — Story Card text import path fails

Expected:

FAIL

BC16-012 — Cleanup deletes durable keys fails

Expected:

FAIL

BC16-013 — Known failure missing test fails coverage gate

Remove F006 test coverage.

Expected:

FAIL or BLOCKED depending stage

BC16-014 — Deferred crafting enabled without spec blocked

Expected:

BLOCKED

BC16-015 — ReleaseCheck command non-mutating

Run /releasecheck.

Expected:

ReleaseCheck audit may update
no gameplay state mutation

⸻

47. Relationship to Final Build Control Map v1

This chunk operationalizes the Control Map.

The Control Map says:

Old patch goals become core rules.
Core rules get owner modules.
Owner modules get state fields.
State changes go through validators and reducers.
Repeat-sensitive effects get ledger keys.
Every old failure gets a regression test.
ReleaseCheck blocks missing safety.

Build Chunk 16 turns that into ReleaseCheck gates and regression tiers.

⸻

48. What Build Chunk 17 should do next

Next build document:

Build Chunk 17 — Foundation Code Pass 1 Plan

Build Chunk 17 must define the exact first code generation scope.

It must include:

* what sections are generated in Code Pass 1
* what sections are shells only
* what systems stay disabled
* exact order of code assembly
* maximum safe code size strategy
* testing order after code generation
* known limitations of Code Pass 1
* what must not be added yet
* packaging plan after Code Pass 1

Build Chunk 17 is the final planning chunk before actual script generation.

⸻

49. Final Build Chunk 16 doctrine

ReleaseCheck and the Regression Matrix are the anti-patch-loop gate.

Final rule:

No silent old failure drop.
No active system without a gate.
No gate without a test.
No test without an owner.
No PASS on uncertainty.
No code-finalization until current-stage known failures are covered.

With ReleaseCheck and the regression matrix locked, the next safe step is the Foundation Code Pass 1 Plan.

====================================================================================================
20_Build_Chunks_01_to_17/17_AIDRPG_Build_Chunk_17_Foundation_Code_Pass_1_Plan.txt
====================================================================================================

AIDRPG Build Chunk 17 — Foundation Code Pass 1 Plan

0. Document status

Document name: AIDRPG Build Chunk 17 — Foundation Code Pass 1 Plan
Build target: AIDRPG Core MVP v0.1 / final-core architecture path
Purpose: Define the exact first real script generation scope, assembly order, shell boundaries, disabled systems, test order, package strategy, and no-overbuild limits before actual code is created.
Status: Final pre-code planning chunk.
Follows: Build Chunk 16 — ReleaseCheck and Regression Matrix Lock.
This document does not contain final script code.

This chunk is the transition point from planning into implementation.

The purpose of Code Pass 1 is not to build the entire final dream system.

The purpose of Code Pass 1 is to build the safe foundation that prevents old failure classes from returning.

If Code Pass 1 is too ambitious, it will become another patch pile.

If Code Pass 1 is too vague, it will repeat old bugs.

This plan defines the exact safe middle path.

⸻

1. Code Pass 1 doctrine

Build the foundation first.
Make unsafe systems impossible by default.
Make every command honest.
Make every hook fail softly.
Make every state bucket stable.
Make parser/proposal/validator/reducer boundaries real.
Make support layers non-canon.
Make ReleaseCheck tell the truth.
Do not overbuild deferred systems.

Code Pass 1 should create a working, installable, inspectable script foundation.

It should not pretend to be a complete automation game engine yet.

⸻

2. Code Pass 1 success definition

Code Pass 1 is successful when the script can:

initialize state safely
repair missing state buckets safely
run AI Dungeon hooks without crashing
route commands safely
render commands honestly
suppress command output from story parsing
track duplicate output hashes
run parser/proposal/validator/reducer shells safely
commit only limited accepted MVP-safe events if enabled
or shell-skip commits honestly if disabled
record audits and ledger keys
keep context read-only
keep CardSync dry-run/safe
keep generated cards disabled
quarantine unsafe old data
cap logs and dirty queues
run /releasecheck honestly

The first code pass is allowed to be conservative.

It is not allowed to lie.

⸻

3. Code Pass 1 non-goals

Do not build these in Code Pass 1:

full combat automation
advanced progression scaling
complete level 1–1000 stat engine
full economy/shop/buy/sell/barter/debt
crafting/material recipes
investigation/case/evidence automation
domain/labor/workers/projects
reputation/faction/title automation
absorption/evolution/devour systems
advanced party/summon controls
multi-protagonist support
warfare/group combat/army systems
generated Story Cards
manual mutation commands
live CardSync writes by default
full old-save conversion from prose
full WorldStructure settlement/project extraction

These remain deferred behind ReleaseCheck gates and disabled commands.

⸻

4. Code Pass 1 active systems

Code Pass 1 should actively implement:

Constants and version identity
Feature flags and safe defaults
Utility helpers
State schema defaults
StateManager
Runtime hook contract
AuditLogger
OperationLedger
CommandRouter
Command renderers
ReleaseCheckLite foundation and gates
EventScannerLite safe shell
Resolver and owner-specific sanitizers
ProposalBuilder safe shell
CanonValidator safe shell
ReducerRegistry
Core reducer shells / limited reducers
DirtyQueue
ContextBuilderLite read-only shell
CardSyncLite dry-run/protected shell
Migration/Quarantine conservative shell
CleanupManagerLite safe caps
Hook orchestration

⸻

5. Code Pass 1 exact section order

The first script should be assembled in this order:

01. Constants and Build Identity
02. Feature Flags and Defaults
03. Utility Helpers
04. State Schema Defaults
05. StateManager
06. Runtime Hook Contract
07. AuditLogger
08. OperationLedger
09. CommandRouter
10. Command Renderers
11. ReleaseCheckLite Foundation
12. Resolver and Owner-Specific Sanitizers
13. EventScannerLite Parser Shell
14. ProposalBuilder Shell
15. CanonValidator Shell
16. ReducerRegistry
17. Core Reducer Shells / Limited Reducers
18. DirtyQueue
19. ContextBuilderLite
20. CardApiAdapter Shell
21. CardSyncLite Dry-Run Protected Shell
22. Migration and Quarantine Manager
23. CleanupManagerLite
24. Hook Orchestration
25. Export / Platform Hook Binding

This order prevents circular dependency and keeps state/runtime/commands safe before parser and reducer logic.

⸻

6. Required namespace strategy

Use one root namespace.

Recommended:

AIDRPG

All modules live under that namespace or local scoped constants inside it.

Avoid global scattered variables.

Avoid old patch names at top-level.

Avoid multiple competing roots such as:

state.tasis
state.patchData
state.cardSync
state.aidrpgOld

Only:

state.aidrpg

is final runtime canon state.

⸻

7. Required build identity

Constants must include:

BUILD_NAME = 'AIDRPG Core MVP'
BUILD_VERSION = '0.1.0-foundation'
SCHEMA_VERSION = 'aidrpg.schema.v1'
BUILD_STAGE = 'Foundation Code Pass 1'

Exact string can be adjusted during code generation, but one source of truth must exist.

/version and /releasecheck must read from this authority.

Late patches must not overwrite it.

⸻

Default active/safe:

releaseCheckEnabled = true
auditEnabled = true
operationLedgerEnabled = true
parserShellEnabled = true

canonValidatorShellEnabled = true
reducerRegistryEnabled = true
coreReducersLimitedEnabled = true
contextBuilderLiteEnabled = true
cardSyncLiteEnabled = true
migrationLiteEnabled = true
cleanupLiteEnabled = true

Default disabled:

generatedCardsEnabled = false
protectedCardWritesEnabled = false
manualMutationCommandsEnabled = false
economyEnabled = false
craftingEnabled = false
investigationEnabled = false
reputationEnabled = false
domainEnabled = false
absorptionEnabled = false
advancedCombatEnabled = false
warfareEnabled = false
multiProtagonistEnabled = false
partyAdvancedEnabled = false

CardSync defaults:

cardSyncDryRun = true

generatedCardCreatesPerTurn = 0
generatedCardUpdatesPerTurn = 0

17. CanonValidator implementation scope

Required active rule coverage:

⸻

18. Reducer implementation scope

Code Pass 1 may implement limited reducers, but they must be real where they commit.

18.1 InventoryReducer

Must commit safely:

accepted acquisition/handoff
visible/available scene items separate from owned items
currency gain only from explicit accepted currency/reward result
consume/drop/loss/destroy limited if validation accepted

Must reject unsafe routed flags.

18.2 AbilityReducer

Must commit safely:

training evidence/candidate
failure evidence
use success evidence if accepted
stable only from explicit stabilize validation

Must not create stable ability from output-only unsupported claim.

18.3 BodyReducer

Must commit safely:

exhausted/fatigued/status gain
status resolution
injury candidate
resource clamp/change if validated

Must not set death from rumor/quote/dream.

18.4 QuestReducerLite

Must commit safely:

quest offer
quest acceptance
objective progress/complete
reward promise
reward payment status through explicit policy

Reward/currency policy must be explicit. Prefer split owner policy.

18.5 ActorReducerLite

Must commit safely:

present named/important actor
scene-only generic actor
absent/reference-only note without presence
memory only for present/directly informed actors

18.6 PlaceReducerLite

Must commit safely:

current scene confirmation
strong arrival
travel intent
weak references as references only

18.7 TimeReducerLite

May commit cautiously:

literal time advance text
deadline notes

Age mutation may remain pending unless age policy is fully coded.

18.8 ProgressionReducerLite

Can be shell/limited:

explicit XP only if active
vague XP rejected
level/stat automation deferred unless formula support included

⸻

19. Context/Card/Migration/Cleanup implementation scope

19.1 DirtyQueue

Must implement:

add/refresh target

cap
summary for /dirty

state-wins-over-stale-packet behavior

⸻

22. Hook orchestration for Code Pass 1

Input hook:

StateManager.init
Runtime.beginHook
if command -> CommandRouter.safeRoute -> Runtime.setCommandSuppression -> return command output
else mark input, maybe parser intent shell, lock startup if first gameplay input, return input
Runtime.endHook/failSoft

Context hook:

StateManager.init
Runtime.beginHook
ContextBuilderLite.build read-only packets
Runtime.endHook/failSoft
return context text

Output hook:

StateManager.init
Runtime.beginHook
if command output suppressed -> skip scan, return output
if duplicate output -> skip consequences, return output
EventScannerLite.scanOutput
ProposalBuilder.fromHits
CanonValidator.validateAll
ReducerRegistry.applyAcceptedBatch
DirtyQueue.add from reducer results
CardSyncLite dry-run/shell if dirty cards
CleanupManagerLite.safeCaps
Runtime.markOutputApplied
Runtime.endHook/failSoft
return original output

⸻

23. Code size strategy

The user has indicated script size tolerance up to 16 MB.

Still, Code Pass 1 should be organized and avoid waste.

Strategies:

use compact helpers
avoid huge inline repeated test data inside script
keep regression matrix outside runtime script if too large
keep command output concise
avoid full design text in script comments
include short module headers, not massive prose blocks
use shared safe append/cap helpers

Do not flag size warnings unless generated script exceeds 16 MB.

⸻

24. Comment strategy

Code should include enough comments to maintain safety, but not the full planning documents.

Recommended comments:

module purpose
forbidden actions
important old-failure rule references by short ID
function contract notes

Avoid embedding long markdown chunks inside script.

⸻

25. Regression tests after Code Pass 1

Run tests in this order:

T0 Smoke Tests
T1 State/Runtime/Command Foundation Tests
T3 Resolver/Sanitizer Tests
T2 Parser/Proposal/Validator Safety Tests
T4 Reducer Commit Tests
T5 Context/Card/Migration/Cleanup Tests
T6 Player-Breaker Adversarial Tests
T8 Deferred System Disabled Tests
T7 Long-Run Stability Tests
T9 Packaging/Install/Usage Tests

Reason:

Commands/state/runtime must work before deeper parser/reducer tests.

Resolver tests should run before parser/proposal/validator tests because target binding depends on them.

⸻

You decide not to take the rusted iron key -> no item

The chapel is visible from market lane -> no scene overwrite
After the road, you enter The Drunken Huntsman -> scene inn, not road
"Twenty years pass" while practicing narration -> no aging
People say you died -> no death
Old Bren promises 15 gold -> no currency
Old Bren pays promised 15 gold twice -> paid once
[AIDRPG] Inventory shows Rusty Dagger -> no duplicate dagger
Context says Iron Sword in Mara custody -> no mutation
Story Card says Player owns Crown -> no mutation

⸻

28. Known limitations of Code Pass 1

Code Pass 1 may honestly state:

advanced combat not active
full progression/stat matrix not active unless separately implemented
world scaling formula tests planned but not fully automated
CardSync live writes disabled by default
generated cards disabled
economy/crafting/investigation/domain/reputation deferred
manual mutation commands disabled
migration from old prose is conservative/quarantine-only
ability system supports candidate/evidence/stable safety but not full ability economy yet
context is summary-only

These are acceptable if ReleaseCheck reports them as WARN/deferred, not PASS as active.

⸻

29. What must not happen during Code Pass 1

Do not add:

quick manual /additem command
quick generated card creation
quick economy transaction handling
quick crafting recipes
quick combat damage automation without body/combat spec
quick absorption/evolution stat gain
quick reputation mutation
quick domain worker creation
quick old card prose import
quick cleanup that deletes active records
quick parser direct writes for convenience

Every “quick” shortcut here is a known patch-loop risk.

⸻

30. Open evidence handling during code

If more old reports arrive during or after Code Pass 1:

add evidence to Open Evidence Queue
map to existing failure ID or create new failure ID
assign owner/gate/test
update Control Map if needed
only change code through owner module rule, not broad patch

Do not paste old patch code blindly.

Do not add emergency global regex fixes.

⸻

31. Code Pass 1 acceptance checklist

Code Pass 1 is acceptable when:

all active MVP commands run safely
fresh state initializes
malformed state fails softly
/releasecheck runs honestly
manual/generated/deferred systems blocked
parser does not mutate
proposal layer binds source/clause/target
CanonValidator blocks known false canon examples
reducers only commit accepted results
support layers do not mutate gameplay
command/context/card/audit text cannot become canon
duplicate output/reward/item/time/ability protections exist
critical player-breaker tests pass or are honestly shell-blocked

⸻

32. Final pre-code decision

After Build Chunk 17, the planning phase for the foundation is complete enough to start script generation.

The correct next step is not another generic chunk.

The correct next step is:

Generate AIDRPG Core MVP v0.1 Foundation Script — Code Pass 1

If the user wants a package first, compile the planning docs and control map into a zip before code.

If the user wants code next, generate the script in manageable code chunks according to this plan.

⸻

33. Final Build Chunk 17 doctrine

Foundation Code Pass 1 must be boring, strict, and safe.

It should not try to impress by activating every dream feature.

It should impress by not repeating old failures.

Final rule:

Build the core truth pipeline first.
Disable what is not safely specified.
Make every unsafe source non-canon.
Make every state mutation go through validation and owner reducers.
Make every repeated effect idempotent.
Make every command honest.
Make ReleaseCheck tell the truth.
Then build upward.

With Build Chunk 17 complete, the project is ready to move from final planning into Code Pass 1.
